# ImFrame — Architectural Decisions

> These decisions are **settled**. Do not re-litigate them. Implement them.
> If a new decision is needed, append a new row — never edit existing rows.

Format: date, decision, alternatives considered, rationale, impact.

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-05-31 | `Thread` is non-copyable and non-moveable | Moveable via `shared_ptr<atomic<bool>>` for `_running` | Constructor lambda captures `&_running` and `&_name` by address; non-moveable removes the race at zero cost | Callers must manage Thread lifetime in-place (storage, unique_ptr) |
| 2026-05-31 | `ThreadPool` uses Dmitry Vyukov MPMC ring buffer + `counting_semaphore` | mutex+CV queue | Lock-free push/pop eliminates contention in the hot dispatch path; semaphore gives workers a cheap blocking wait without spurious wakes | Queue capacity capped at 1024; Submit() spin-yields when full |
| 2026-05-31 | `BackgroundWorker` uses `mutex` + `deque` + two CVs (`_taskCv` / `_drainCv`) | Lock-free single-consumer queue | FIFO + `_inFlight` coordination for `Drain()` is simpler and correct with a mutex; lock-free would complicate `Drain()` significantly | Contention only on queue push/pop, not on the task body |
| 2026-05-31 | `BackgroundWorker` wraps `Thread` (not raw `std::jthread`) | Raw `std::jthread` + duplicate name logic | Reuses platform naming without duplicating the `ApplyCurrentThreadName` implementation or exposing it publicly | Adds `Thread.hpp` to the `BackgroundWorker.hpp` include chain |
| 2026-05-31 | `std::jthread` invariant enforced project-wide from Phase 4 | Allow `std::thread` in pre-Phase-4 code | Cooperative cancellation via `std::stop_token` is the correct C++20+ pattern; `FileWatcher.cpp` migrated in this phase to comply | CI grep for `std::thread[^_id]` catches regressions |
| 2026-06-01 | `Delegate` uses fixed 16-byte buffer with three stored fn-ptrs (`_invoke`, `_destroy`, `_copy`) | `std::function` (heap), `std::move_only_function` (heap) | Zero allocation for all free fns and single-inheritance member fns; compile error on oversized callables via `requires` clause catches misuse at build time | `Bind<Method>(obj)` stores only the object pointer + template trampoline — avoids MSVC multi-inheritance PMF size issues entirely |
| 2026-06-01 | `Signal::Emit()` and `ThreadSafeSignal::Emit()` copy the handler list before iterating | Iterate in-place with re-entrant guard flag | Consistent with `EventBus::Dispatch()` pattern; re-entrant connect/disconnect during emit is safe without extra bookkeeping or iterator invalidation | New subscriptions or unsubscriptions during emit take effect on the next call |
| 2026-06-01 | `EventBus::Impl` is `shared_ptr`-managed (Pimpl); `SubscriptionToken` holds `weak_ptr<Impl>` | `EventBus` directly holds state; `SubscriptionToken` holds `EventBus*` + `atomic<bool>` alive flag | Token destruction after `EventBus` destruction is a safe no-op: `weak_ptr::lock()` returns null; no separate flag needed | `SubscriptionToken` and `Connection` store only `std::function<void()>` in the header — full `Impl` definition stays in the `.cpp` |
| 2026-06-01 | `EventBus::DispatchAsync()` reuses `BackgroundWorker` from Phase 4 | Dedicated thread pool, `std::async` | Zero new threading primitives; `BackgroundWorker` already provides FIFO ordering and a named thread visible in debuggers; event copy captured via `shared_ptr<EventType>` | Async handlers run on the single `BackgroundWorker` thread — callers must not assume parallelism between async dispatches |
| 2026-06-01 | `ThreadSafeSignal` is a separate class, not a policy template parameter of `Signal` | `Signal<Sig, ThreadSafePolicy>` | Avoids policy proliferation and keeps `Signal` simple; `ThreadSafeSignal` is only needed in two known Phase 12/28 sites; separate class makes the thread-safety contract explicit in the type name | No shared base class — code is slightly duplicated but both types remain independently maintainable |
| 2026-06-02 | `Timer::Tick(dt)` fires repeating timers multiple times per tick (inner while loop) | Single firing per tick per timer | A large dt (e.g. app resuming after sleep) fires all elapsed intervals; matches proposal's O(k) cost model where k counts individual firings | |
| 2026-06-02 | `ConsoleSink`/`FileSink`/`UiSink` declared in public `Logger.hpp` (not private headers) | Sinks in src/ with factory functions only | Users need concrete types to configure sinks (esp. `FileSink` rotation threshold, `UiSink::GetEntries()` for Phase 17 viewer); public types remove awkward downcast patterns | |
| 2026-06-02 | `Logger::Write()` always dispatches to `BackgroundWorker`; calling thread never blocks on I/O | Synchronous sink writes | Logger invariant: hot render loop is never stalled; `Shutdown()/Drain()` guarantees delivery before process exit | |
| 2026-06-02 | `Config::Get<T>` / `Set<T>` are inline templates delegating to non-template helpers (`GetValuePtr`, `SetValue`) | extern template + definitions only in .cpp | Inline templates that call non-template methods keep Impl fully private; avoids extern-template complexity; templates reduce to one-liners | |
| 2026-06-02 | `Config::PollWatcher()` is caller-driven; Application calls once per frame | Background polling thread with sleep | Consistent with FileWatcher::Poll() design; avoids a dedicated thread; Application drives it in Phase 7 | |
| 2026-06-02 | `Config::Set()` coalesces saves via single `atomic<bool>` CAS | Queue each Set individually | O(1) coalescing; at most one pending save task; all Sets that arrive before the save runs are captured in that single save | |
| 2026-06-03 | `Application` takes `std::unique_ptr<IBackend>` (dependency injection) rather than creating the backend internally | Application creates backend based on a compile-time flag | DI makes the Application testable with a fake backend without linking GLFW/OpenGL; users pass `make_unique<GLFWOpenGL3Backend>()` in their main | `CreateHeadless()` constructs with the internal `HeadlessBackend` stub |
| 2026-06-03 | `IBackend::CancelClose()` added for close-veto support | Store a "veto pending" flag in Application; no backend change | Without resetting GLFW's close flag, `glfwWindowShouldClose()` returns true again on the next `Poll()`, causing infinite close-request/veto cycles | Each backend resets its own platform-specific close state |
| 2026-06-03 | `DockSpace::InitDefaultLayout` is guarded by `DockBuilderGetNode(id) == nullptr` (not a first-frame bool) | `_firstFrame` member flag | Node existence is the canonical check; if the user clears nodes at runtime the layout re-initialises automatically; the flag approach would require resetting manually | Phase 16 persistent layout overrides this check by providing its own nodes before the first frame |
| 2026-06-03 | `WindowScope` has `_valid` flag (false after move) so the destructor skips `ImGui::End()` in the moved-from state | `bool* _alreadyEnded` sentinel | Prevents double-End() when `BeginWindow()` returns by value — C++17 NRVO elides the move in the common `if (auto w = BeginWindow(...))` pattern but the move constructor is required as a fallback | Non-copyable; `operator=(WindowScope&&)` deleted to avoid accidental reassignment |
| 2026-06-04 | `ColorToken` has no `operator ImVec4()` — `Apply()` constructs `ImVec4{t.r, t.g, t.b, t.a}` inline from fields | Implicit conversion operator declared in the header with a conditional include guard | Any `operator ImVec4()` definition in a public header would require `<imgui.h>`, violating the no-ImGui-in-public-headers invariant; inline construction at each of the ~63 call sites is trivial | All ImGui type conversions remain confined to `src/Theme/Theme.cpp` |
| 2026-06-04 | `ThemeBuilder` is fully header-only (inline method bodies in `ThemeBuilder.hpp`) | Separate `src/Theme/ThemeBuilder.cpp` with the switch + field-assignment bodies | None of the four methods call ImGui APIs — they are pure struct field operations; putting them in a `.cpp` would add a translation unit with trivial content; follows `Delegate` precedent for header-only types | Changing ThemeBuilder method bodies triggers recompilation of all TUs that include it |
| 2026-06-04 | Built-in themes live in `namespace ImFrame::Themes` (not `ImFrame::Theme::Themes`) | Triple-nested `ImFrame::Theme::Themes` as the stub files used | Proposal-authoritative; `ImFrame::Themes::Dracula` reads naturally at call sites; `Theme::` sub-namespace is for types, `Themes::` (plural) is for named value constants; CLAUDE.md namespace table updated accordingly |`Application.hpp` forward-declaration changed from `class Theme` to `struct Theme` to fix MSVC `/WX` mismatch error |
| 2026-06-06 | Icon glyph constants are `inline constexpr const char*` in `namespace ImFrame::Icons::Fa`; `IMF_ICON_LABEL` requires a string-literal argument | `#define`-only constants (FA style); `constexpr` with a dedicated `IMF_FA_*` macro layer | Namespace constants give IDE autocompletion and type safety for runtime use. Macros cannot be namespaced, so the literal form is used for compile-time concatenation — the comment in the header makes this explicit. Both patterns coexist: `Fa::House` for passing to ImGui; `"\xef\x80\x95"` or a `#define` with `IMF_ICON_LABEL` for zero-cost labels | Macro users must write raw literals or add their own `#define` aliases |
| 2026-06-06 | `ImFont*` and `ImFontAtlas*` are forward-declared (not included) in `IconFont.hpp` | Include `<imgui.h>` in the public header; use `void*` opaque handles | Forward declarations keep the public header ImGui-free (architecture invariant); pointer-only usage in function signatures requires no definition. `ImWchar` cannot be forward-declared (it is a typedef), so range sentinels use `uint32_t` in the public API and cast to `ImWchar` inside `IconFont.cpp` | `uint32_t` sentinels are always wide enough for both default and `IMGUI_USE_WCHAR32` configurations |
| 2026-06-06 | Glyph range array lifetime managed via `static std::vector<std::array<ImWchar,3>>` inside `IconFont::Load()` | Caller provides and owns the range array; heap-allocate per call | ImGui atlas stores only a pointer to the glyph range — the data must outlive `atlas->Build()`. A static vector accumulates one entry per `Load()` call at program scope; this is safe for all practical startup configurations (≤ 3 icon fonts) | The static vector is never freed during program lifetime; intentional for atlas correctness |
| 2026-06-06 | FA6 renamed the "home" icon to "house" (U+F015 codepoint unchanged) | Keep alias `Home = House` in the header | FA6 canonical name is "house"; adding a deprecated alias would encourage using the wrong name. Tests and documentation use `Fa::House` | `Fa::Home` does not exist — callers who relied on the Phase 9 plan's "Home" example must use `Fa::House` |
| 2026-06-07 | Widget API uses `ImFrame::Widgets::Vec2` and `Vec4` instead of `ImVec2`/`ImVec4`; `TextureHandle = void*` instead of `ImTextureID` | Include `<imgui.h>` in public widget headers; `#ifndef IMGUI_VERSION` guard for redeclarations | `ImVec2`/`ImVec4` are value types (not pointers), so forward-declaration fails for stored members. Re-declaring them guarded by `#ifndef IMGUI_VERSION` risks ODR violations when imgui.h is included after the widget header in the same TU — the two struct definitions have different constructors. Own types are layout-identical (verified by static_assert in each .cpp) and consistent with the Phase 8 ColorToken precedent (no `operator ImVec4()` in public headers). | Widget .cpp files convert Vec2/Vec4 ↔ ImVec2/ImVec4 by copying individual float fields. Phase 11+ layout types use `Widgets::Vec2` as their size type. |
| 2026-06-07 | Template widget `Show()` methods (TextInput, Slider, Combo) are inline in public headers; they call non-template `ImFrame::Internal::*` helpers declared in the same header and defined in `src/Widgets/WidgetImpl.cpp` | Explicit template instantiations in .cpp; `imgui_stdlib.h` for TextInput | Explicit instantiations would expose ImGui types through the return/parameter signatures; `imgui_stdlib.h` is not guaranteed to be available. Inline dispatch via `if constexpr` is zero-overhead and keeps the header self-contained. Internal helpers are declared in a clearly-labelled section of each template header. | `<imgui.h>` is confined to `src/Widgets/`. The `ImFrame::Internal` namespace section in each header is a documented extension point, not part of the public API. |
| 2026-06-07 | `ChildScope` destructor is declared non-inline in the header and defined in `src/Layout/ChildScope.cpp` (calls `ImGui::EndChild()`) | Inline destructor with a conditional `imgui.h` include; opaque handle via `void*` | `ChildScope` is a public return type — its destructor must be visible at every use site. Defining it in a .cpp confines `imgui.h` to that TU, preserving the no-ImGui-in-public-headers invariant. The `_active` flag prevents double `EndChild()` after a move. | Same pattern used by `WindowScope` (Phase 7); `ChildScope` is moveable-only so returned by value from `Panel::Begin()` and `ScrollArea::Begin()` with NRVO eliminating the move in the common `if (auto s = ...) {}` pattern. |
| 2026-06-07 | Layout template `Render()` methods (HStack, VStack, Grid) delegate ImGui calls to non-template `ImFrame::Internal` helpers declared in the layout headers and defined in `src/Layout/LayoutImpl.cpp` | Include `<imgui.h>` in layout headers | Identical pattern to Phase 10 widget templates; avoids `imgui.h` in public headers. Grid helpers (`GridBeginTable`/`GridNextColumn`/`GridEndTable`) keep `EndTable()` guarded by `BeginTable()`'s return value — `EndTable()` is only called on a successful open (ImGui 1.90+ contract, opposite of `EndChild()`). | Three ImGui calls per layout type confined to one TU; layout headers include only `Widgets/Types.hpp` for the `Renderable` concept. |
| 2026-06-07 | `ScrollArea` scroll helpers (`ScrollToBottom`, `ScrollPosition`, `SetScrollPosition`) are instance methods, not static helpers | Free functions in Layout namespace; static methods | Methods are called on the `ScrollArea` object inside the child scope, making the relationship explicit. `ScrollArea area("id"); if (auto s = area.Begin()) { area.ScrollToBottom(); }` reads naturally. All three methods call ImGui functions that operate on the current window context set by `BeginChild()`. | Implementations in `ScrollArea.cpp` have zero state — they are pure ImGui pass-throughs. Caller is responsible for calling them inside an active child scope. |
| 2026-06-08 | Easing lambdas in `Easing.hpp` are `inline auto` (not `inline constexpr`) | `inline constexpr auto` as the proposal stated | `std::pow`, `std::sin`, `std::exp` are not constexpr in MSVC's current C++23 implementation despite P0533R9 standardisation; declaring the lambdas constexpr would silently compile but fail if used in a constexpr context. All easing functions are runtime-only by nature (UI animation); `inline` is correct. | No observable API difference; easing functions are always evaluated at runtime. |
| 2026-06-08 | `Tween::OnComplete` accepts `std::function<void()>` instead of `Delegate<void()>` | `Delegate<void()>` as the proposal stated | `OnComplete` fires at most once — the hot-path SBO benefit of `Delegate` is irrelevant. `std::function` accepts any callable without the 16-byte SBO constraint, keeping `Tween.hpp` free of the `Utility/Delegate.hpp` dependency and keeping the animation subsystem self-contained. | `Anim/` depends only on `Easing.hpp` and standard library headers. |
| 2026-06-08 | `ToastManager::Render()` uses `ImGui::GetForegroundDrawList(viewport)` directly; no ImGui window | Dedicated `##imframe_toasts` overlay window with `ImGuiWindowFlags_NoDecoration` | ForegroundDrawList draws above all other windows without any window management overhead; no `Begin/End` pair needed, no z-order issues, no popup interaction interference. Position and size computed from `viewport->WorkPos` + `WorkSize`. | Toast rendering is purely visual — no ImGui ID space consumed, no hit-testing needed. |
| 2026-06-08 | `PopupScope` destructor calls `ImGui::EndPopupModal()` ONLY when `_open == true` | Always call EndPopupModal (like ChildScope calls EndChild always) | ImGui 1.90+ contract: `EndPopupModal` must be called **if and only if** `BeginPopupModal` returned true — calling it unconditionally triggers an internal assert. Opposite of `EndChild` which is always called. | `PopupScope` is the only scope type in ImFrame where the end-call is conditional on the begin return value. |
| 2026-06-08 | `ContextMenu::Item` actions use `std::function<void()>`; `CloseCurrentPopup()` called inside each item handler | `Delegate<void()>` action; caller-driven close | Menu items typically capture lambdas by value; `std::function` handles any capture size naturally. Closing the popup immediately on item selection matches the expected UX pattern and prevents half-open popup states. Items are persistent (not cleared on Show), allowing the ContextMenu object to be reused each frame. | |
| 2026-06-09 | `Table::Render()` and `ColumnDef::Renderer` use `std::function<void(int)>` for all row/column callbacks | `Delegate<void(int)>` as the proposal stated | Row renderers typically capture data vectors, selected-row sets, or multiple model references whose total capture size exceeds the 16-byte Delegate SBO. `std::function` avoids compile errors on common usage patterns. The one-shot performance concern that motivated `Delegate` for `OnClick` does not apply here — `Render()` calls the callback up to ~50 times per frame, making heap allocation per `Render()` call (not per-callback-invocation) the actual cost, which is negligible. | `Table` callbacks are stored as `std::function` members on the `Table` object, so the heap allocation occurs once at construction, not per frame. |
| 2026-06-09 | `Table::GetSortState()` returns a custom `SortState` wrapper (with `SortDirection` enum + `ColumnSortSpec` vector) instead of `const ImGuiTableSortSpecs*` | Return `const ImGuiTableSortSpecs*` as the proposal stated | Returning a raw ImGui type in a public method would require `<imgui.h>` in `Table.hpp`, violating the no-ImGui-in-public-headers invariant. `SortState` provides the same information (column index, direction, dirty flag) through ImFrame-native types. | Callers cannot use `ImGuiSortDirection_*` constants directly; `SortDirection::Ascending`/`Descending` are the ImFrame equivalents. |
| 2026-06-10 | All four plot widget `Show()` implementations (`LinePlot`, `BarPlot`, `ScatterPlot`, `HeatMap`) are defined in a single TU `src/Widgets/PlotContext.cpp` alongside `PlotContext` | One `.cpp` per widget class (like Phase 10 widgets) | All plot widgets call `ImPlot::BeginPlot`/`ImPlot::EndPlot`; confining every ImPlot call to one TU means `<implot.h>` is included exactly once and never leaks into public headers. The file-local `PlotScope` RAII guard can be shared across all four `Show()` methods without a separate header. | Adding a fifth plot widget in a later phase requires extending this one TU, not creating a new include/implot.h consumer. |
| 2026-06-10 | `PlotContext` is a value member of `Application` (not a singleton, not a `shared_ptr`) | Singleton (like `ToastManager`); `unique_ptr<PlotContext>` | ImPlot has exactly one context per ImGui context; `Application` already owns the ImGui context lifecycle. A value member makes the init/shutdown ordering (Init after ImGui::CreateContext, Shutdown before ImGui::DestroyContext) explicit and verifiable at the call site in `Application.cpp`. | `PlotContext` is non-copyable and non-moveable; callers never see it through the public API. |
| 2026-06-10 | `PlotColormap` enum mirrors `ImPlotColormap_` integer values exactly (Viridis=0 … Deep=9) | Map via a switch; store `ImPlotColormap` directly | Storing the ImPlot enum directly in a public header would require `<implot.h>` in `HeatMap.hpp`. An enum with matching integer values allows a simple `static_cast<ImPlotColormap>(static_cast<int>(_colormap))` cast in the TU where implot.h is already included, with zero overhead. | If implot changes its colormap ordinals in a future version, `PlotColormap` values must be updated to match. |
| 2026-06-10 | implot v1.0 `ApplyTheme` maps only 3 `ImPlotCol_*` values (`PlotBg`, `FrameBg`, `AxisText`); `ImPlotCol_Line` is not mapped | Map `ImPlotCol_Line` to `theme.accentDefault` as the proposal stated | `ImPlotCol_Line` was removed from `ImPlotCol_` in implot v1.0; per-item line color is now set via `ImPlotSpec::LineColor` on each `PlotLine` call. Attempting to use it triggers a compile error. The 3-color mapping covers the plot background and axis text, which are the visually significant theme integration points. | Per-series line colors are left at implot's default colormap; callers requiring a custom line color must extend `LinePlot` with a per-series `LineColor` setter that populates `ImPlotSpec`. |
| 2026-06-09 | `Table::Render()` selects rendering mode automatically: per-column mode when ANY `ColumnDef` has a `Renderer`; row-renderer mode otherwise | Explicit mode selector method (e.g., `UseColumnRenderers(bool)`) | Detection via `std::any_of` on column defs is zero-overhead and eliminates a redundant API knob. The two modes are naturally mutually exclusive: a table is either column-typed (each cell has a typed renderer) or row-typed (the caller handles all columns). Making the choice explicit in the data avoids misconfiguration. | If a user accidentally mixes column renderers and a row renderer, the column renderers take priority and the row renderer is silently ignored — this matches the "any column renderer → per-column mode" contract documented in the class header. |
| 2026-06-09 | `PropertyGridScope` uses the same RAII pattern as `ChildScope`: moveable, destructor calls `EndTable()` when `_open == true`, `Row<TWidget>()` is a header-only template calling `Internal::PropertyGridBeginRow/EndRow` helpers | Callback-based API (`PropertyGrid::Render(callable)`); non-RAII `Begin()/End()` pair | Scope-based RAII prevents orphaned `EndTable()` calls and matches the established Phase 11 `ChildScope`/`PopupScope` pattern. Header-only `Row<TWidget>` with internal helper delegation keeps `<imgui.h>` out of the public header while supporting any `Renderable` widget type without virtual dispatch. | `PropertyGridEndRow()` is currently a no-op (ImGui advances rows automatically); it is retained as a hook for future per-row post-processing (e.g., tooltip, drag-drop target). |
| 2026-06-12 | `Application` owns a `Utility::Config _layoutConfig` value member (in-memory by default); `DockSpace` receives a non-owning `Config*` via `SetConfig()` called in `Run()` | `DockSpace` holds its own `Config` instance; `Application` exposes `WithLayoutConfig(path)` builder | Application already owns all lifecycle boundaries; wiring the Config pointer before the render loop makes the dependency explicit and testable. Users get layout persistence for free without needing to provide a config path for the basic case. | File-backed layout persistence requires a future `Application::WithLayoutFile()` builder — out of scope for Phase 16. |
| 2026-06-12 | Layout names tracked via `_layoutNames` vector (insertion order) + persisted in Config under `"Layout._index"` as semicolon-separated string | Store names in a `std::set` (sorted); query all Config keys with `"Layout."` prefix | `Config` has no key-enumeration API; a separate index is required. Vector preserves insertion order (user-visible list matches save order). Semicolon separator is safe because layout names are arbitrary user strings (semicolons uncommon). | Names containing `;` would corrupt the index — the restriction is intentional and documented in the header. |
| 2026-06-12 | `ResetLayout()` reloads `_defaultIni` (the ini captured immediately after `InitDefaultLayout()` ran) rather than re-running `InitDefaultLayout()` | Re-run `InitDefaultLayout()` to recompute node geometry fresh | Re-running DockBuilder clears all existing dock assignments, leaving all user panels floating with no home. Restoring the captured ini preserves the original three-column slots while resetting panel sizes/positions to first-run defaults. | `_defaultIni` is empty until the first frame renders; calling `ResetLayout()` before `Begin()` has run is a documented no-op. |
| 2026-06-12 | `WindowManager::RenderLayoutMenu()` uses an inline `InputText + Save` button inside the `BeginMenu` block | Modal popup via `ImGui::OpenPopup` + `BeginPopupModal` | A modal requires `OpenPopup` and `BeginPopupModal` to be called from the same ImGui window context. `RenderLayoutMenu` is called by user code inside a menu bar — the menu closes when any item is clicked, making the popup context unavailable on the next frame. Inline InputText avoids this ownership problem entirely while remaining functional. | `_saveLayoutBuf[128]` is a private member of `WindowManager`; the buffer persists between frames so partially-typed names are preserved while the menu is open. |
| 2026-06-13 | `Application` stores the hot-reloaded theme as `optional<Theme> _hotTheme`; `_pendingTheme` is pointed at `*_hotTheme` | Re-use `_pendingTheme` / static variable | `WithTheme()` stores a raw `const Theme*` to a caller-owned theme. A hot-reloaded theme has no external owner — storing it in `_hotTheme` keeps the value alive for the frame and beyond without introducing a static or heap allocation. | If `WithTheme()` is called after hot-reload, `_pendingTheme` is updated to the caller's pointer and `_hotTheme` is left occupied but orphaned; the next hot-reload will overwrite `_hotTheme` safely. |
| 2026-06-13 | `ThemeHotReload` TOML parser handles ONLY `[r, g, b, a]` float-array values; other value types and sections are silently skipped | Reuse `Config`'s existing TOML parser (extended with array support) | Theme TOML files contain exclusively RGBA arrays — supporting scalars/strings/booleans adds complexity with zero benefit. A minimal parser in `ThemeHotReload.cpp` stays self-contained and is auditable in ~40 lines. `std::from_chars` for `float` avoids locale issues that affect `stof`. | If a future theme key requires a scalar value (e.g., radius or spacing), the parser must be extended in `ThemeHotReload.cpp` only. |
| 2026-06-13 | All Phase 17 DevTools code is wrapped in `#if defined(IMF_DEV_TOOLS)` in both headers and source files; `IMF_DEV_TOOLS` is set PUBLIC via `target_compile_definitions` on the ImFrame target | Separate `ImFrame_DevTools` static library target; compiler flag only in `.cpp` files | PUBLIC propagation ensures consumer TUs (tests, Examples) see the same guard without re-specifying it. A separate target would require consumers to link two targets. Header-guarding the DevTools includes in `Application.hpp` and `ImFrame.hpp` prevents any DevTools type from leaking into the public API in Release builds. | `IMF_DEV_TOOLS` ON in Debug, OFF in Release is enforced by the CMake option logic that checks `CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE`; the Release CI build verifies no DevTools symbols appear. |
| 2026-06-14 | `check_api_leaks.sh` allowlists `include/ImFrame/Icons/IconFont.hpp` for ImGui type names (Check 3) rather than removing the check | Remove Check 3 entirely; use a per-type whitelist at grep level | `IconFont.hpp` is the intentional bridge between ImFrame and ImGui's font subsystem — forward-declaring `ImFont`/`ImFontAtlas` in a public header is unavoidable for a font-loader that returns `ImFont*`. All other ImGui type leaks are real violations. An explicit per-file exemption in the script with a one-line comment makes the intent auditable without suppressing detection of real leaks. | If a second header legitimately needs ImGui types, add it to the exemption list with justification — the current allowlist is a `grep -v` pattern that can be extended. |
| 2026-06-14 | `check_api_leaks.sh` comment-filtering uses `grep -vE ':[0-9]+:[[:space:]]*(//|/\*|\*)'` on `grep -n` output | Parse file content directly with awk; use Perl regex via `grep -P` | ERE pattern on grep's `file:linenum:content` output format is portable (`-P` requires pcre which is not always installed); the pattern matches only the first non-space character of the code content, which is sufficient to identify `//`, `/* `, and ` * ` comment lines. Block-comment opening `/**` never contains ImGui type names in practice. | The filter produces false negatives for `/* ImVec2 */` on a line that starts with non-whitespace (rare in practice); the goal is zero false positives in CI, not perfect comment parsing. |
| 2026-06-14 | Sanitizer jobs in CI use `Debug` build type with `CMAKE_CXX_FLAGS="-fsanitize=..."` rather than CMake Sanitize presets | `CMAKE_INTERPROCEDURAL_OPTIMIZATION=OFF` guard; dedicated CMake `SANITIZE` option | Passing flags directly is portable and requires no CMake changes to the library itself. ASan + TSan are incompatible and run as separate matrix jobs. `ASAN_OPTIONS=detect_leaks=0` is set to suppress LeakSanitizer false positives from vcpkg dependencies. | Coverage job cannot run sanitizer-instrumented builds simultaneously (gcov flags and ASan are incompatible); they are separate jobs. |
| 2026-06-14 | `DemoApp::CMakeLists.txt` links `imgui::imgui` explicitly; it was NOT added to the `ImFrame` PUBLIC interface | Add imgui as a PUBLIC dependency of ImFrame | Architecture invariant: `include/ImFrame/` headers never include `<imgui.h>`. Making imgui PUBLIC would imply consumers need imgui for the ImFrame API, which is false. The DemoApp is an unusual consumer that calls ImGui directly (for `BeginMainMenuBar` and table cell renderers); the explicit link makes this dependency visible at the DemoApp target level. | All other consumers (Tests/) use the HeadlessBackend and never call ImGui directly; they do not need this explicit link. |

The "canvas point at top-left" convention also matches how every pan implementation works: dragging right decrements `_position.x` (the top-left canvas point moves left), which moves the canvas right visually. | `FitToRect` computes `_position = rect_centre - viewportSize/2 / zoom`; panning adds `−mouseDelta / zoom` to `_position`. Modulating alpha post-capture would require re-encoding the `ImU32`, which needs `IM_COL32` bit manipulation on every replayed draw command. `PushStyleVar(Alpha)` affects only ImGui widget rendering — `ImDrawList::Add*` calls bypass the style system entirely and use the stored `ImU32` verbatim. Implementing true per-layer opacity requires either (a) storing colours as `Vec4` in every lambda (8 bytes instead of 4) or (b) an additional opacity-multiply pass at replay time.
