| 2026-06-28 | `BeginStatic`/`EndStatic` cache stores `DeferredDrawCmd` vector copies keyed by `uint64_t` in a `std::unordered_map<uint64_t, CachedBlock>` persisted on `CanvasImpl` across frames | Serialise ImDrawList commands (vtx/idx buffers); store `std::vector<std::function<…>>` | Caching `DeferredDrawCmd` (which IS the `std::function<void(ImDrawList*)>` lambda) avoids re-serialising ImGui vertex/index buffers and avoids ImDrawListSharedData management. On cache hit, commands are appended to the deferred vector exactly as if they were freshly issued — no special replay path needed. On miss, the range `[staticBlockStart, deferred.end())` is copied into the cache entry. Subsequent frames replay those copies by appending them — O(k) for k cached commands, same as O(k) if re-drawn. | Cache keys are caller-supplied `uint64_t`. Key collisions produce visual glitches that are the caller's responsibility (documented invariant in DrawContext.hpp). The cache map persists on `CanvasImpl` indefinitely; entries are never evicted. For large canvases with many unique keys, callers should invalidate by using keys derived from object identity. |
| 2026-06-29 | ImGui 1.92 API: `Image()`/`AddImage()` take `ImTextureRef` instead of `ImTextureID` | Cast `void*` → `ImTextureID` → `static_cast` (the old Phase 24 pattern) | Verified against installed imgui 1.92.8 headers. `ImTextureRef{void* p}` and `ImTextureRef{ImU64 v}` are the two constructors. `static_cast<ImTextureID>(textureId)` (where `textureId` is `void*`) no longer compiles as `ImTextureID = ImU64` — pointer-to-integer cast is not implicit. Updated: `DrawContext.cpp` `AddImage(ImTextureRef{textureId}, ...)`, `Viewport.cpp` `ImGui::Image(ImTextureRef{static_cast<ImTextureID>(entry.imTextureId)}, ...)`. | All future `Image()`/`AddImage()` calls must use `ImTextureRef{...}` constructors. |
| 2026-06-29 | `InputEvent` lives in `namespace ImFrame` (not `ImFrame::Backends`) — `Viewport.hpp`/`Viewport.cpp` must use unqualified `InputEvent` | Qualify as `Backends::InputEvent` per Phase 19 naming assumptions | `InputEvent` is declared in `include/ImFrame/Backends/InputEvent.hpp` with `namespace ImFrame {` (not `namespace ImFrame::Backends`). The `Backends/` directory is a physical location, not a namespace indicator. `Viewport.hpp`'s three `OnInput` declarations and `Viewport.cpp`'s `OnInput`/`FireOnInput` definitions all needed `Backends::InputEvent` replaced with `InputEvent`. | The same applies to `FrameInfo`, `WindowHandle`, and `WindowConfig` — all in `namespace ImFrame`. |
| 2026-06-29 | `HeadlessBackend` class has no `final` specifier — tests subclass it via `FrameLimitedHeadlessBackend` | Keep `final`; use a composition or factory pattern for frame limiting | Tests in Phase 24/25 (Viewport_test, Canvas2D_test, ViewportConformance_test) override `Poll()` and `BeginFrame()` to implement a finite run loop. Removing `final` is the only change needed; `HeadlessBackend` has no virtual methods that would be dangerous to override and its destructor is already `override`. | `HeadlessBackend` is not part of the public API — the `final` restriction would only matter if consumers were expected to never subclass it, which contradicts the test-infrastructure use case documented in its header. |
| 2026-06-29 | Canvas2D tests wrap `canvas.Show()` inside `ImGui::Begin("##canvas_test", ...)/End()` with `SetNextWindowSize({600,500}, ImGuiCond_Always)` | Call `canvas.Show()` directly inside the DockSpace's `OnUi` callback (as originally written) | `Application::RunOneFrame()` wraps `OnUi` inside a `DockSpace::Begin()` / `DockSpace::End()` block. `DockSpace::Begin()` calls `ImGui::DockSpace(id, {0,0})` which consumes all available window space. After that call, `GetContentRegionAvail()` returns ~{0,0} and `BeginChild({200,200})` is positioned past the clip rect, causing `BeginChild` to return false — `Canvas2D::Show()` does nothing, `OnDraw` never fires. A separate `ImGui::Begin` window creates its own content region independent of the DockSpace clip. `ImGuiWindowFlags_NoSavedSettings` prevents ini-file lookups; `ImGuiCond_Always` ensures the 600×500 frame is always available. | Tests include `#include <imgui.h>` — this is acceptable because test TUs are `internal`, not public headers. The no-ImGui-in-public-headers rule applies to `include/ImFrame/` only. |
| 2026-06-29 | Viewport tests that omit `vp.Size()` add an explicit `vp.Size({200.0f, 200.0f})` instead of relying on `GetContentRegionAvail()` fallback | Wrap in `ImGui::Begin/End` so `GetContentRegionAvail()` returns a sensible value | `Viewport::Show()` computes `w = max(static_cast<uint32_t>(avail.x), 1u)` when `_hasExplicitSize` is false. Inside the DockSpace host window, `avail.x` is negative. `static_cast<uint32_t>(negative_float)` is undefined behavior; in MSVC it wraps to ~4 GB. `GetOrCreate(this, ~4GB, ~4GB, ...)` calls `CreateViewportFramebuffer(~4GB, ~4GB)` → `_pixels.assign(~68 GB, 0)` → `std::bad_alloc`. Adding explicit sizes is the minimal fix: it bypasses the `avail` path entirely. | All four failing Viewport tests ("timing_test", "scale_test", "dtor_test", "vp_a"/"vp_b") lacked `Size()` calls; all four are now fixed. Viewport tests with existing explicit sizes (OnResize tests, all ViewportConformance tests) were unaffected. |

## Phase 26 — Viewport3D, Camera3D, Gizmo

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-06-29 | Camera3D uses pitch/yaw spherical coords; yaw=0 → forward=(1,0,0) (+X direction) | Euler angles with separate yaw/pitch axes; arbitrary initial forward | Consistent with orbit formula: `eye = target + dist*(cos(pitch)*cos(yaw), sin(pitch), cos(pitch)*sin(yaw))`. Fly mode reuses the same angles so the two modes share pitch/yaw state across transitions. | Tests must account for yaw=0 → +X forward (not −Z). Documented in PHASE_STATUS.md. |
| 2026-06-29 | All 4×4 matrices are column-major throughout Camera3D, Viewport3D, Transform3D | Row-major (DirectX convention) | ImGui, OpenGL, Vulkan, Metal all use column-major column-vector form. Consistent with Phase 24 RenderContext which passes GL framebuffers to shaders. Row-major users must transpose. | Documented on every public matrix API. |
| 2026-06-29 | Viewport3D wraps `Viewport` internally (composition not inheritance) | Inherit from `Viewport` | Viewport3D has an entirely different builder API (OnRender with Viewport3DRenderInfo, not RenderContext). Inheritance would expose all Viewport setters and create confusion about which callback to register. Composition allows clean forwarding and a separate `_viewport.OnRender` that builds the Viewport3DRenderInfo. | Viewport3D cannot be used where a `Viewport` is expected; the internal `Viewport` is completely hidden from users. |
| 2026-06-29 | Viewport3D camera interaction uses ImGui polling in `Show()`, not `OnInput` callback | Register OnInput delegate and process InputEvent variant | OnInput fires asynchronously via the backend event loop; ImGui polling (`IsMouseDragging`, `GetIO().MouseDelta`, `IsKeyDown`) is already frame-synchronized with Show() and requires no event queuing. Fly mode needs DeltaTime from `io.DeltaTime` which is only meaningful per frame. | Camera interaction is tied to the ImGui frame rate, not the backend event rate. No behavior difference on most platforms. |
| 2026-06-29 | Gizmo uses screen-space hit testing and drag (2D), not 3D ray-to-axis intersection | Full 3D ray-intersection with cylinder/cone per axis | 3D intersection is more accurate but much harder to implement robustly (degenerate cases when axis is nearly perpendicular to view). Screen-space: project axis endpoints to screen, test mouse distance to segment, project mouse-delta onto screen-axis direction. Simple, robust, consistent visual size. | Accuracy degrades when axis is very short on screen (nearly edge-on to camera); sensitivity is constant in screen pixels per world-unit regardless of camera distance. |
| 2026-06-29 | `Viewport3DRenderInfo` stores `const RenderContext*` (pointer), not `const RenderContext&` (reference) | Store a copy or a reference | `RenderContext` has a `const NativeGraphicsContext&` reference member, making it non-copyable and non-moveable. Storing a reference would require an existing object to bind to. A pointer is valid for the duration of the OnRender callback and is clearly documented as callback-scoped. | Callers must not retain the pointer past the callback. Documented on the field. |

## Phase 27 — Widget tree core (Widget/Element/Component/Primitives/Reconciler)

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-01 | `Widget` wraps a `std::shared_ptr<const Internal::WidgetConcept>` (one heap allocation per node at `Build()` time; copies are refcount bumps) | A closed `std::variant` of all widget types; raw stack-only storage | True zero-allocation heterogeneous trees are not achievable in standard C++ without a closed variant of every possible widget type, which would make `Component` impossible for downstream code to extend with new types in other translation units. Matches the cost profile of `std::function`'s own type erasure. | Phase 28's `State<T>`/dirty-tracking should be designed knowing each `Widget` node costs one `shared_ptr` allocation; minimizing rebuild frequency (not node allocation) is the optimization lever. |
| 2026-07-01 | Type-erasure internals (`WidgetConcept`, `PrimitiveModel<T>`, `ComponentModel<T>`, `ComponentElement<T>`) live in the single project-wide `ImFrame::Internal::` namespace, not a nested `ImFrame::Tree::Internal::` | Per-module nested `Internal` namespaces (`Tree::Internal`, mirroring `Rendering::Internal` if it existed) | Discovered build failure: code inside `ImFrame::Tree::Primitives` doing unqualified `Internal::BoxElement` resolves `Internal` via ordinary lookup to the nearest enclosing namespace with that name — `ImFrame::Tree::Internal` (Widget.hpp's original location) shadowed the intended `ImFrame::Internal` (where `RenderObjects/*.cpp` define concrete `Element` subclasses), causing `'BoxElement': is not a member of 'ImFrame::Tree::Internal'`. Moving Widget.hpp's internals to the single top-level `ImFrame::Internal::` (the project's established implementation-details namespace) resolves the collision and matches existing convention (`Internal::ViewportRegistry`, `Internal::IBackend`, etc. are not nested per-module either). | Any future templated type-erasure machinery added to a public header in a different module (Phase 28's `Signal`/`Computed`?) must also live in the single `ImFrame::Internal::`, never a module-nested `Internal`. |
| 2026-07-01 | `Element`/`RenderObject` are merged into one concrete class per primitive (e.g. `Internal::BoxElement` both owns children and emits ImGui calls) rather than two separate parallel class hierarchies | Literal two-layer split: `Element` owns child `Element`s, delegates Layout/Paint to a separate owned `RenderObject` | The proposal's three-layer architecture (Widget/Element/RenderObject) is satisfied at the *file* and *namespace* level (`RenderObjects/*.cpp`, `ImFrame::Internal::`, "only code that calls raw ImGui") without a second class hierarchy. A separate `RenderObject` base would only matter if multiple `Element`s needed to share one `RenderObject` instance, which Phase 27 never does. | Keeps `Element`'s public interface (`Layout`/`Paint`) free of any `RenderObject*` type, which would otherwise have had to appear in the public `Element.hpp` header — `RenderObject` was never declared in a public header at all (see next row). |
| 2026-07-01 | `Element`'s public interface exposes `Layout(BoxConstraints) -> Vec2` / `Paint(Vec2)` directly — no `RenderObject*` getter | `virtual RenderObject* GetRenderObject()` on `Element`, with `RenderObject` defined in `ImFrame::Internal::` | A `RenderObject*`-returning method on the public `Element` class would leak an `ImFrame::Internal::` type into a public header (`include/ImFrame/Tree/Element.hpp`), violating "`ImFrame::Internal::` — never expose in public headers." Each concrete `Element` subclass (in `ImFrame::Internal::`) implements `Layout`/`Paint` directly, and `ComponentElement<T>::Layout`/`Paint` are pure pass-through to its single child `Element`. | `BoxConstraints` itself had to become a public `ImFrame::Tree::` type (declared in `Element.hpp`) since it's a parameter of a public virtual method — documented as intentionally public, unlike most of the Internal:: layout machinery. |
| 2026-07-01 | `DockSpace::Begin()` already calls `ImGui::DockSpace(id, {0,0})`, consuming the dockspace host window's entire content region — `Reconciler::Show()` opens its own dedicated window (`RootBridge::BeginRootWindow()`/`EndRootWindow()`), pinned every frame to `ImGui::GetMainViewport()->WorkPos`/`WorkSize` via `ImGuiCond_Always` | Paint the tree directly into the dockspace host window's remaining cursor position (as `Application::OnUi()` callbacks already do) | Same root cause already documented in the Phase 25/26 entries above (`Canvas2D` tests needed their own `ImGui::Begin` window for the same reason) — but this time it broke production `SetRoot()` code, not just tests: `GestureRegion`'s `InvisibleButton` never registered hover because the whole subtree painted outside the host window's clip rect at `(0, height+4)`. A second, independent bug: without an explicit position/size, `ImGui::Begin("##ImFrameTreeRoot", ...)` floats at ImGui's auto-cascading window position (observed ~(68,68)) with a tiny default size — fixed by pinning position+size to the main viewport's work area with `ImGuiWindowFlags_NoMove \| ImGuiWindowFlags_NoResize`. | The tree root is currently always a fixed, non-dockable fullscreen window. Real docking integration (letting users drag the tree's window into the `DockSpace` like any other panel) is deferred — flag this as a Phase 27 follow-up if the proposal for Phase 28+ assumes the root is dockable. |
| 2026-07-01 | `Spacer::GetFactor()` always returns `1` (treated identically to `Expanded(...).Factor(1)` with no child) | Factor `0` ("zero-factor Expanded" read literally from the Phase 27 proposal text) | The proposal's own description is internally inconsistent: "fills remaining space in a Flex. Zero-factor Expanded." A factor of `0` is `Flex`'s code for "not flexible, sized to intrinsic content" (see `DistributeFlexFactors`) and would NOT fill remaining space — it would collapse to zero size. Factor `1` is the only value consistent with "fills remaining space" and matches Flutter's `Spacer` (literally `Expanded(child: SizedBox.shrink())`, factor 1) and SwiftUI's `Spacer()`. | Any future primitive named "zero-factor" should be understood as "not flexible" (factor 0), distinct from `Spacer`'s actual factor-1 behavior — naming in the original proposal text is misleading and should not be copied forward into Phase 28+ proposals without correction. |

## Phase 28 — Reactive state (State/Signal/Computed/InheritedWidget)

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-03 | `Tree::InheritedWidget<T>` is forward-declared (`template <typename T> class InheritedWidget;`) in `InheritedWidget.hpp` before the `ImFrame::Internal::InheritedElement<T>` block that names it | Reorder the file so `Tree::InheritedWidget<T>` is fully defined first, then `Internal::InheritedElement<T>` after | `InheritedElement<T>::Mount()`/`Update()` needs `widget.As<Tree::InheritedWidget<T>>()`, and MSVC cannot parse `Tree::InheritedWidget<T>`'s `<T>` as a template-argument list unless the primary template `InheritedWidget` has already been declared as a template somewhere in the TU — without the forward declaration this produced a cascade of `C3878`/`C2872` parse errors reading `<` as less-than. Reordering the whole file was rejected because `InheritedWidget<T>::CreateElement()` needs `Internal::InheritedElement<T>` complete, creating a real circular dependency that only a forward declaration resolves cleanly. | Any future public-header type pair with this "wrapper widget defined after its Internal:: element, but the element needs to name the wrapper type templated" shape needs the same one-line forward declaration ahead of the `Internal::` block. |
| 2026-07-03 | `ComponentElement<T>::Update()` always assigns `_component = newWidget.As<T>()` before checking the dirty flag, even when the element turns out not to be dirty | Skip re-assigning `_component` entirely when not dirty, relying on the old `T` instance | The incoming `Widget` may carry updated captured data (e.g. a parent passed a new `Delegate` or changed a non-`State`/`Signal` field) even when no `State<T>`/`Signal<T>` fired — `Component` values are plain data, not required to route every field through reactive primitives. Always re-assigning is what the Phase 27 `ComponentElement<T>::Update()` already did (unconditional `_component.Build()` before Phase 28), so this preserves that guarantee while adding the dirty-skip only around the *rebuild* (child reconciliation), not around field propagation. | Any component field that is NOT wrapped in `State<T>`/`Signal<T>`/`Computed<T>` still updates every frame regardless of dirty tracking — only the `Build()` call (and its resulting child-tree reconciliation) is skipped when clean. |
| 2026-07-03 | Catch2 `TEST_CASE` names use plain ASCII `-`, never an em-dash (`—`, U+2014) | Keep em-dashes in test names for visual consistency with prose comments elsewhere in the file | `ctest -R "<test name>"` (and Catch2's own `--` name-filter re-invocation, which `ctest`'s `catch_discover_tests` uses to run each `TEST_CASE` as its own CTest entry) passes the filter string through the Windows console codepage; on this MSVC/Windows toolchain the em-dash mangles to `G\xEF\xBF\xBD\xEF\xBF\xBD` before reaching the Catch2 binary, so the filter matches nothing and CTest reports "No tests ran" as a failure — even though the same test passes when the `.exe` is run directly with `--success` and no filter. Discovered when 3 of 29 new Phase 28 tests failed only under `ctest`, never under direct invocation. | `Tests/Tree/State_test.cpp` and any future test file must restrict `TEST_CASE` name strings to ASCII; em-dashes remain fine in `//` comments and Doxygen blocks, which are never used as CTest filter arguments. |

## Phase 29 — Widget Library Reimplementation (Portal, VirtualList, Component-based widgets)

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-05 | `PortalElement` registers itself with a thread-local registry on every `Mount()`/`Update()`; `Reconciler::Show()` drains it after the main tree paints and calls `RenderDeferred()` on each — reusing the *same* `"##ImFrameTreeRoot"` window (via ImGui's documented multi-`Begin()`-same-ID append behavior) rather than opening a second window | A dedicated always-on-top ImGui window/viewport for portal content | Reusing the same window means portal content simply draws *after* the main tree's draw calls within one draw list — later commands render on top, no z-ordering bookkeeping needed. A second window would need its own position/size management and still requires manual z-order control (`ImGui::BringWindowToDisplayFront` or similar) to guarantee it's always frontmost. | Portal content is confined to the same screen region as the tree root (the main viewport work area) — it cannot escape to a genuinely separate OS window. Acceptable for Toast/Modal/ContextMenu use cases; revisit if a future widget needs true multi-window portals. |
| 2026-07-05 | `VirtualListElement` computes the visible row range by reading `ImGui::GetScrollY()`/`GetWindowHeight()` live inside its own `Paint()`, rather than tracking scroll position via `Tree::State<float>` as the proposal's prose suggested | `State<float>` member updated from a scroll callback | `State<T>` only provides value (dirty-tracking, `Context::Of()` lookups) for `Component`-satisfying types rebuilt via `ComponentElement<T>::Rebuild()`. `VirtualListElement` is a primitive `Element`, not a `Component` — it isn't gated by dirty-tracking at all; the Reconciler calls its `Layout()`/`Paint()` unconditionally every frame regardless of any Component's dirty state. A plain member (matching `GestureRegionElement`'s own `_wasHovered`/`_wasActive` pattern) is simpler and has identical behavior with zero `State<T>` overhead. | Any future primitive `Element` needing per-frame transient state (not full reactive tracking) should default to a plain member first — reach for `State<T>` only inside genuine `Component`s. |
| 2026-07-05 | `ButtonWidget`/`CheckboxWidget`/`SliderWidget<T>`/`TextInputWidget<T>`/`ComboWidget<T>` are new **primitives** with their own `Element` calling the exact same single ImGui function the deprecated `Show()` used (`ImGui::Button`, `ImGui::Checkbox`, `ShowSliderInt/Float/Double`, `ShowTextInputStr/U8`, `ShowComboImpl`) — not `Component`s composed from `GestureRegion`+`Box`+`Flex`+`Text` as the proposal's prose describes | Composition from Phase 27 primitives, with per-instance interaction state (hover, focus) tracked via `Tree::State<T>` | Each old `Show()` implementation is already a *single* ImGui call that internally handles all hover/press/focus visual states via ImGui's own style system (`ImGuiCol_Button`/`ButtonHovered`/`ButtonActive`, etc.) — there is no manual color/state management to "declaratively" reimplement. Composing from primitives would require introducing new interaction state at the `Component` level to replicate what ImGui already does internally, and doing that via `State<T>` runs directly into the state-reset-on-parent-rebuild gap (next row) with no clean fix available this phase. Reusing the existing single call is simpler, correct, and satisfies the Foundation's stated goal ("same ImGui calls, different path") more literally than a hand-rolled composition would. | Any interactive widget added in a future phase should default to this "new primitive wrapping the existing Show() call" pattern unless there's a concrete reason the visual can't be expressed as one ImGui call. |
| 2026-07-05 | Discovered, not fixed: `ComponentElement<T>::Update()` unconditionally does `_component = newWidget.As<T>();` every frame a parent passes it a widget, even when the parent constructs a **fresh** `T` value (as opposed to reusing a persistent instance it already held). If `T` has a `State<T>`-typed member, that member's identity (its `shared_ptr<Node>`) resets to a fresh default whenever the parent rebuilds for *any* reason — not just when the component's own state changes. This was never exercised by Phase 28's own tests, which always reused the identical `Widget`/component value across `Update()` calls rather than reconstructing a fresh one. | (a) Extend `ComponentElement<T>` to diff and selectively preserve fields. (b) Add a "preserve across rebuild" marker to `State<T>`. (c) Move transient state to the `Element` layer instead (what this phase actually does for the interactive widgets). | (a) and (b) are non-trivial reactive-system design work with no proposal guidance and real edge cases (what counts as "the same instance"?) — attempting either mid-phase risked an unreviewed, under-tested core-architecture change. (c) sidesteps the problem entirely for this phase's concrete needs (see previous row) without touching `ComponentElement<T>`'s contract at all. | Documented as a known gap. Any future `Component` that embeds `State<T>` directly as a member (rather than binding to caller-owned state via pointer) must ensure its parent never reconstructs it fresh except when a genuine identity change is intended — or the state will silently reset. Revisit before or during Phase 30. |
| 2026-07-05 | `ComboWidget<T>`, `ModalWidget`, `ContextMenuWidget` do not use `Portal` — they call the same native `ImGui::BeginCombo`/`BeginPopupModal`/`BeginPopupContextItem` the deprecated classes used | Route all three through `Portal` uniformly, per the proposal's literal wording ("Modal uses Portal for the backdrop overlay... ContextMenu uses Portal for the popup") | These three ImGui popup APIs already render on ImGui's own independent overlay draw layer, entirely outside the parent window's clip rect — the exact problem `Portal` exists to solve is already solved natively for them. Routing them through `Portal` instead would mean reimplementing backdrop dimming, focus-trapping, dismiss-on-outside-click, and popup positioning from scratch with no functional improvement over the existing, working native mechanism — a large scope increase for zero user-visible benefit. | `Portal` is reserved in this codebase for overlays with **no** native escape-clipping mechanism — currently only `ToastOverlayWidget` (see next row). If a future overlay type also lacks a native popup equivalent, follow the Toast pattern, not attempt to force it through the ImGui popup APIs. |
| 2026-07-05 | `ToastOverlayWidget` is stateless — it takes a `std::vector<ToastSnapshot>` parameter (typically `ToastManager::Instance().Snapshot()`, a new accessor added this phase) rather than owning its own toast queue/fade state | Give `ToastOverlayWidget` its own internal queue and `AnimatedValue<float>`-driven fade, duplicating `ToastManager::Impl`'s existing logic | A stateful `ToastOverlayWidget` would hit the exact `ComponentElement<T>`-state-reset gap two rows up the moment any ancestor rebuilds. Being stateless sidesteps it entirely — there is nothing to lose. Reusing `ToastManager::Impl`'s existing, tested queue/fade logic via a new read-only `Snapshot()` accessor avoids duplicating that logic a second time. | `Application::RunOneFrame()`'s automatic `ToastManager::Instance().Render(dt)` call was deliberately left unchanged (not made conditional) — using both `Render(dt)` and a `ToastOverlayWidget` in the same app double-renders toasts. `Docs/Migration_v1_to_v2.md` documents this as "use one or the other." |
| 2026-07-05 | MSVC's C4996 fires on several deprecated classes' own out-of-line fluent setters (`Table::Flags`, `ContextMenu::Item`, `PropertyGrid::SplitRatio`, `Modal::Size`, etc. — all return the class type by reference) even inside the class's *own* `.cpp`, unlike setters inline in the header (exempt); suppressed per-file with `#pragma warning(push/pop, disable: 4996)` scoped around just the affected block | Remove `/WX` project-wide, or rewrite every affected setter as inline-in-header | Removing `/WX` project-wide would silence unrelated new warnings elsewhere, a much bigger loss of safety than this narrow, well-understood MSVC quirk deserves. Rewriting call-site-stable public API signatures (moving definitions into headers) is a larger, riskier change to code that's already scheduled for removal in Phase 30 — not worth it for deprecated code. | `Examples/DemoApp/main.cpp` (pre-existing, uses ~15 deprecated widgets extensively, explicitly links the `/WX`-enabling `imframe_warnings` interface target) got the same file-wide `#pragma warning(disable: 4996)` treatment — it is exactly the kind of pre-existing "baseline" deprecated-API usage the proposal's `deprecated_usage.txt` concept describes, and migrating the whole demo app is out of scope for this phase. |

## Phase 30.1 — Widget Replacement Gap Closure (Separator, Image, ProgressBar, ColorEdit, Radio, Grid, PropertyGrid)

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-07 | `SeparatorWidget`/`ProgressBarWidget`/`ColorEditWidget`/`RadioWidget` are new **primitives** (own `Element`, single reused ImGui call), same pattern as Phase 29's `ButtonWidget`/`CheckboxWidget` | Compose from `Box`/`Flex`/`GestureRegion` | Same reasoning as the Phase 29 entry above: each deprecated `Show()` is a single ImGui call that already owns its full visual state (or, for `Separator`/`ProgressBar`, no interaction state at all) — nothing to gain from decomposing into primitives. | Confirms the "new primitive wrapping the existing `Show()` call" pattern generalizes to every remaining Phase 10–14 widget, not just the Phase 29 batch. |
| 2026-07-07 | `ImageWidget` collapses the old dual `Show()`/`ShowButton()` API into one widget, calling `ImGui::Image()` when no `OnClick` is set or `ImGui::ImageButton()` when one is | Keep two separate widgets (`ImageWidget` and `ImageButtonWidget`), mirroring the old two-method split | `ButtonWidget`/`CheckboxWidget` already establish the convention that interactivity is expressed via an `OnClick`/`OnChange` delegate, not a separate call path — a second `ImageButtonWidget` type would just be `ImageWidget` with one field always set, adding a type for no behavioral gain. | Any future widget with an old dual imperative/interactive `Show()`/`ShowX()` split should default to one widget gated on whether its interaction delegate is set, not two types. |
| 2026-07-07 | `GridWidget` is pure layout math computed in `Layout()`/`Paint()` — no `ImGui::BeginTable()` call at all, unlike the deprecated `Grid` | Reuse the deprecated class's existing `Internal::GridBeginTable`/`GridNextColumn`/`GridEndTable` helpers (`src/Layout/LayoutImpl.cpp`) inside the new `Element` | Those helpers exist only for the deprecated imperative class and are deleted with it in Phase 30.2 — building the new primitive on top of soon-to-be-deleted internals would just create a second removal problem. This mirrors the precedent already set when `HStack`/`VStack` were replaced by the pure-math `Flex` primitive (Phase 27) instead of an ImGui-table-backed equivalent — `GridElement` (`src/Tree/RenderObjects/GridRO.cpp`) is modeled directly on `FlexElement`, using the same `ReconcileChildren` child-list diffing, computing column width as an equal share of available width and each row's height as its tallest child. | Any future N-up/wrapping layout primitive should default to pure `Layout()`/`Paint()` math reusing `ReconcileChildren`, not an ImGui table container call. |
| 2026-07-07 | `PropertyGridWidget` is **pure composition** via `Build()` — no new primitive or `Element` was written for it at all; each row is `Flex(Horizontal){ Expanded(Text(label), factor), Expanded(rowWidget, 100-factor) }`, with `SplitRatio` expressed as a pair of `Flex` factors (`round(ratio*100)` / `100-that`) rather than a fixed pixel width | Add a dedicated `PropertyGridElement` that resolves `SplitRatio` into an absolute label-column width during `Layout()` | `Component::Build()` runs before any `Layout()` pass has constraint information, so a fractional split ratio can't be resolved into a fixed pixel width at `Build()` time — but `Flex`'s existing `DistributeFlexFactors()` (used by every `Flex`, unchanged since Phase 27) already solves exactly this class of proportional-width problem. Reusing it needs zero new layout code, matching how `TableWidget` was "genuinely composed via `Build()`" in Phase 29 rather than written as a new primitive. | Group-separator rows reuse `SeparatorWidget` (`SeparatorWidget().Label(groupName)`) from this same sub-phase — built first specifically so `PropertyGridWidget::Build()` could depend on it. Any future declarative widget needing a caller-specified proportional split should default to two `Expanded` factors inside a `Flex`, not a new primitive resolving pixel widths. |
| 2026-07-07 | `PropertyGridWidget`'s imperative `Begin()`/`Row()`/`Separator()` RAII scope pattern has no declarative equivalent — replaced with a flat `Rows(std::vector<PropertyGridRow>)` API, where `PropertyGridRow` is a label/widget pair or (via the private-constructor `PropertyGridRow::Separator(groupName)` factory) a group-separator marker | Give `PropertyGridWidget` a `Build()`-time callback/builder object mimicking the old scope's row-by-row call sequence | `Build()` returns one complete `Widget` tree per call — there is no equivalent of "call `Row()` several times against a live scope object" in a declarative model where the whole tree is constructed and returned at once. A flat row list is the natural declarative shape and matches how `TableWidget::Column()` and `Flex::Children()` already take a fully-formed collection rather than a builder callback. | Confirmed by test (`PropertyGridWidget: Build() composes one Flex row per property, split by SplitRatio`) that a `SplitRatio(0.4f)` row produces label/value `Expanded` factors of exactly 40/60. |

## Phase 30.2 — Deprecated API Removal (21 headers deleted, DemoApp migrated)

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-09 | `scripts/check_api_leaks.sh`'s `strip_comments()` (renamed `filter_real_leaks()`) now re-tests its leak pattern against each matched line with any trailing `//...` comment stripped, instead of only recognizing full-line comments | Leave the script as-is and add `BackendInfo.hpp:264` to a per-line allowlist | The false positive (a `///<` doc comment mentioning `ImGui::Image()` on an otherwise-clean field) was a general blind spot, not a one-off — any future trailing Doxygen comment naming an ImGui type would hit the same bug. Fixing the matcher once is more durable than allowlisting individual lines as they're discovered. Verified the fix still catches genuine leaks (injected a real `ImVec2` leak into a scratch header, confirmed it's still flagged) before relying on it as this phase's removal lock-in gate. | Committed separately (`6cae4ff`) before the removal work, since it was a pre-existing bug unrelated to any specific deleted class. |
| 2026-07-09 | `Examples/DemoApp/main.cpp` drives each of its six panels through a small **local** `TreePanel` helper (Mount/Update + Layout/Paint via public `Tree::Widget`/`Tree::Element` API, called inside the panel's own `ImGui::Begin()`/`End()`) instead of `Application::SetRoot()` | Migrate to `SetRoot()`, collapsing all six panels into one tree | `Application::SetRoot()` → `Internal::Reconciler::Show()` always opens one single fixed, non-dockable root window (Phase 27 `DECISIONS.md`: "real docking integration is deferred") — it cannot produce six independently dockable panels. `Reconciler` itself is `ImFrame::Internal::`, not public API. `Tree::Widget::CreateElement()` and `Tree::Element::Mount/Update/CanUpdate/Layout/Paint` are public and need no ImGui window of their own (`Paint()` only calls `SetCursorScreenPos` and draws) — `TreePanel` reproduces exactly what `Reconciler::Show()` does, minus the window it opens, entirely in application code with zero library changes. | `TreePanel` instances live as `DemoState` members (not separate `main()` locals) specifically so `OnUi`'s lambda only captures `[&app, &state]` — two pointers, exactly `Utility::Delegate<void()>`'s 16-byte small-buffer limit. The first migration attempt captured all 5 `TreePanel`s plus `app`/`state` individually (56 bytes) and failed to compile (`C2664`) until refactored. Any future ImFrame app needing multiple independently dockable tree-driven panels should copy this `TreePanel` pattern rather than waiting for first-class multi-root support. |
| 2026-07-09 | `PropertyGridScope` and its exclusive backing functions (`Internal::PropertyGridBeginRow/EndRow/Separator`) were deleted outright, along with the now-unused `Widgets::Renderable` concept | Keep `PropertyGridScope`, making its constructor public (or picking a new friend) so it stays instantiable | Discovered mid-removal: `PropertyGridScope`'s only non-default constructor was `private`, friended exclusively to the just-deleted `PropertyGrid` class — with `PropertyGrid` gone it was already uninstantiable by anyone, not merely uncalled. `PropertyGridWidget` (Phase 30.1) never used it — it composes `Flex`/`Expanded` directly. Reviving it would mean inventing new public API surface (a public constructor, or a new friend) with zero driving consumer, which is exactly the kind of speculative work the project's coding standards warn against. `Renderable` (`{ t.Show() } -> bool`) had no remaining use once `PropertyGridScope::Row<TWidget>` — its last caller anywhere in the codebase — was gone. | Contrast with `ChildScope` (`include/ImFrame/Layout/ChildScope.hpp`), kept in this same phase: `ChildScope` is a generic, already-public, already-instantiable `BeginChild`/`EndChild` RAII wrapper with no coupling to the deleted imperative widget API — it just happened to lose its only caller when `Panel`/`ScrollArea` were deleted. `PropertyGridScope` was never general-purpose; it was tightly coupled to the specific ImGui-table-row-at-a-time pattern `PropertyGridWidget` deliberately replaced. "Uncalled but generic and working" → keep; "uncalled *and* structurally broken *and* narrowly coupled to a pattern that's being replaced" → delete. |
| 2026-07-09 | `src/Layout/LayoutImpl.cpp` (backing `HStackSameLine`/`VStackDummy`/`GridBeginTable`/`GridNextColumn`/`GridEndTable`) was deleted in full once all four callers (`HStack`, `VStack`, and the deprecated `Grid` class) were gone | Leave the file in place with no callers, to be cleaned up in a later phase | Not part of the original Phase 30 proposal text (which only lists header/class removal), but a direct, mechanically-verifiable consequence of it — found during the removal survey, not left as a follow-up item. | Any future "strip class X from file Y" pass should grep for the `Internal::` helper functions X exclusively declared/used, not just delete X's own class body — this is the second phase in a row (after Phase 30.1's `PropertyGridScope`/`ChildScope` near-miss) where a deprecated class's private backing helpers outlived the class itself until specifically checked for. |

## Phase 30.3 — DockSpace Reimplementation (DockSpaceRO extraction, opt-in DockSpaceWidget)

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-09 | `Application::RunOneFrame()`'s automatic `_dockSpace.Begin()`/`_onUi()`/reconciler/`_dockSpace.End()` sequence is left **completely unchanged** — `App::DockSpace`'s public API, state, and calling contract are identical to before this phase | Remove the automatic dockspace and make it opt-in via the tree (the more literal reading of "real docking integration" that Phase 27 `DECISIONS.md` deferred) | Asked the user which scope was intended (conservative same-behavior reimplementation vs. the more aggressive opt-in restructuring) and got no response this session. Proceeded with the lower-risk reading: every prior Phase 30 sub-phase preserved identical behavior end-to-end, and `Tests/App/DockSpace_test.cpp` explicitly asserts "Application drives Begin()/End() automatically from RunOneFrame()" — changing that would be a breaking change for every existing `OnUi()`-only app, not just a reimplementation. | Flagged explicitly in the plan and here: if the more aggressive reading was actually wanted, `Application`'s automatic dockspace, tree-root dockability, and a second `DemoApp` migration pass are still open work for a future phase. |
| 2026-07-09 | `DockSpace.cpp`'s raw ImGui/`DockBuilder`/`SaveIniSettingsToMemory`/`LoadIniSettingsFromMemory` calls were extracted into `src/Tree/RenderObjects/DockSpaceRO.hpp`/`.cpp` as free functions — not a `Tree::Element` subclass | Wrap `DockSpace::Begin()`/`End()` in a real `Tree::Element` with `Layout()`/`Paint()` | `Application::RunOneFrame()`'s sequence (`_dockSpace.Begin()` → `_onUi()` → reconciler → `_dockSpace.End()`) has `Begin()`/`End()` bracketing an arbitrary caller-controlled sequence — an `Element`'s `Paint()` only owns painting its own children, it has no way to "pause" mid-`Paint()` for the caller to run other code. Free functions (mirroring the *existing* `RootBridge.hpp`/`.cpp` shape exactly) sidestep this entirely: `App::DockSpace`'s own methods just call them in the same order as before. `DockSpace.cpp` no longer includes `<imgui.h>`/`<imgui_internal.h>` at all — closing the "only `RenderObjects/` touches raw ImGui" gap it was the last holdout on. | `App::DockSpace` is genuinely stateful infrastructure (layout persistence via `Utility::Config`, named-layout index), analogous to `ToastManager` — not a per-frame declarative value, which is exactly why it doesn't get a `Build()`-based Component the way `TableWidget`/`PropertyGridWidget` did. |
| 2026-07-09 | A new, genuinely public `App::DockSpaceWidget` (`Tree::PrimitiveWidget`, no child) was added on top of the same `DockSpaceRO` free functions, for opt-in use inside a user's own `SetRoot()` tree — but it is **not** wired into `Application`'s automatic path | Wire the automatic path through `DockSpaceWidget` too, or skip adding a widget at all since nothing in the library consumes one yet | Satisfies the proposal's literal ask for "a `DockSpaceWidget` component... via a `RenderObject`" without the higher-risk work of restructuring `Application`'s calling contract (see the first row above). Purely additive — cannot regress any existing app. | Discovered and fixed a real bug before shipping: the widget's `Paint()` originally reused `App::DockSpace`'s exact `"##DockSpace"`/`"MainDockSpace"` window/dockspace-id strings. Since `Application::RunOneFrame()` always drives the automatic `App::DockSpace` too, a `SetRoot()` tree using `DockSpaceWidget` would open the *same* ImGui window ID twice in the same frame — an assertion/crash. Fixed by parameterizing `BeginDockSpaceWindow(menuBar, windowName, dockspaceIdName)` so the two paths use distinct literals (`"##DockSpace"`/`"MainDockSpace"` vs. `"##ImFrameDockSpaceWidget"`/`"ImFrameDockSpaceWidget"`). Any future second dockspace-like primitive must follow the same distinct-identifier rule. |
| 2026-07-09 | `DockSpaceWidget` has **no child/content slot** in this phase — `Layout()` returns `{0,0}` and `Paint()` ignores its `position` argument, mirroring `PortalElement`'s "occupies no space, self-positions elsewhere" precedent exactly | Give it a `Child(Widget)` setter, painted inside the same dockspace host window after `ImGui::DockSpace()` | `RootBridge.hpp`'s own file comment documents the reason this doesn't work: `ImGui::DockSpace()` consumes its entire host window's content region to register the dock node — anything painted directly afterward in that same window is clipped away (the identical problem `RootBridge` exists to solve for the tree's own root window). Properly hosting a child here needs its own dedicated, uniquely-identified inner window (like `RootBridge`'s) — real design work with no concrete driving use case yet, so deferred rather than half-built. | Documented as a known limitation in `DockSpaceWidget.hpp`'s doc comment: pair it with separately opened dockable windows (raw `ImGui::Begin()`, or another element's own window) that dock into it — exactly how `App::DockSpace` already works today. |
| 2026-07-09 | `Tests/Tree/DockSpaceWidget_test.cpp`'s "initialises the default layout" test checks node existence from *inside* `OnUi()` on the *second* frame, reproducing the dock-node ImGuiID hash manually (`ImHashStr(name, 0, win->ID)`) rather than calling `ImGui::GetID()` directly | Call `ImGui::GetID("ImFrameDockSpaceWidget")` directly, or check after `app.Run()` returns | Two real bugs found while writing this test: (1) `ImGui::GetID()` is scoped to the *current* window's ID stack — called from `OnUi()` (which runs inside `Application`'s own `"##DockSpace"` window), it hashes to a different value than `DockSpaceWidgetElement::Paint()` sees from inside its own `"##ImFrameDockSpaceWidget"` window. `ImGui::FindWindowByName()` + manually reproducing the hash with that window's ID as seed sidesteps the mismatch. (2) Checking *after* `app.Run()` returns segfaults — `Run()` tears down the ImGui context before returning, so `FindWindowByName()` dereferences a dead context. (3) `OnUi()` runs *before* the `SetRoot()` tree's reconciler pass each frame, so the window/node don't exist yet during frame 1's `OnUi()` — needs 2 frames, checking on the second. | Any future test asserting ImGui-internal state (dock nodes, window pointers) created by a `Tree::Element` must check from inside a live frame callback, account for `OnUi()`-vs-reconciler ordering, and never assume `ImGui::GetID()` is context-independent. |

## Interstitial — `Widget(...)` Implicit-Conversion Cleanup (between Phase 30.3 and 30.4)

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-10 | Removed every redundant explicit `Widget(...)`/`Tree::Widget(...)` wrap across the codebase (source, tests, demo, docs) — `Tree::Widget`'s converting constructor was already non-`explicit` and needed no change | Add a factory function (e.g. `MakeWidget(T)`) or CTAD deduction guide to provide a *different* terser spelling | The user found the pervasive `Widget(ButtonWidget(...))` wrapping unnatural compared to conventional declarative-UI trees. Investigation found the constructor was already marked `NOLINT(google-explicit-constructor) — intentional implicit conversion` — the capability existed since Phase 27 and was simply never used, because the first Phase 27 examples wrapped everything and every subsequent file copied that convention without questioning it. Verified empirically (compiled scratch code against the real headers with MSVC) that `return ButtonWidget("Save");`, `Children({ButtonWidget("A"), Text("Right")})`, and `Expanded(Text("Hi"))` all compile with zero explicit wraps before touching a single real file. No new library API was needed — this is a pure usage/documentation cleanup, `Tree::Widget` itself is unchanged. | Any future `@example` block, test, or application code written against the Tree API should never write `Widget(SomeType(...))` — pass `SomeType(...)` directly wherever a `Widget` is expected (return values, `std::vector<Widget>` initializer-list elements, single-`Widget` setters). |
| 2026-07-10 | The two mechanical sweeps (19 header `@example` blocks; `Tests/Tree/*.cpp` ×8 + `Examples/DemoApp/main.cpp`) were delegated to parallel background agents with an explicit rule list distinguishing real redundant-wrap sites from false positives (constructor declarations of types whose name happens to end in `Widget`, function-type template params like `Delegate<Widget(int)>`, and `Widget.hpp`/`VirtualList.hpp`/`Application.hpp` themselves) | Do the entire sweep by hand | ~160+ call sites across ~30 files is exactly the kind of large, well-specified, low-ambiguity mechanical task suited to delegation — matches the same pattern used for Phase 30.2's deprecated-class stripping. The 3 implementation files with real logic (`Toast.cpp`, `PropertyGrid.cpp`, `Table.cpp`) and the migration doc were done by hand instead, since mistakes there would be actual bugs or confusing prose, not just style. | Both agents' output was spot-checked and then verified with a repo-wide grep sweep (excluding every `FooWidget(` constructor-call pattern) before trusting it — found zero incorrect strips and zero missed sites; the 3 remaining `Widget(` hits post-cleanup are all legitimate (a template param, an actual constructor parameter declaration, and this cleanup's own prose in the migration doc). |

## Phase 30.4 — `WidgetTestDriver` Headless Test Harness (logic mode only)

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-11 | `WidgetTestDriver<T>` provides no runtime string/index "path" addressing (unlike the proposal's literal `driver.SimulateClick(path)`/`driver.State<T>(path)` wording) — structural navigation is compile-time and type-directed via a new `FindDescendant<Target>(Widget)` free function | Add a generic runtime path-resolution system, matching the proposal's prose literally | Three research agents confirmed `Tree::Element` (the live, mounted node) exposes *no* child-traversal API at all — only `Parent()`, one direction — and concrete `Element` subclasses are `Internal::`, defined in `.cpp` files with no public header. Building a generic runtime resolver would require a new virtual hook on `Element` itself (e.g. a `VisitChildren()`-equivalent, unlike Flutter's `Element` which has this as a first-class method every subclass implements) — a real core-module change, not attempted this phase. `FindDescendant` instead walks the immutable `Widget` returned by `Build()`, reusing the exact idiom `Tests/Tree/Widgets_test.cpp` already established (pull the built `Widget`, `.As<T>()` into it) — zero framework changes needed. | Documented as a known scope boundary in the header's own file comment: `FindDescendant` only recognises the enumerated built-in `Tree::Primitives` + a handful of interactive `*Widget` types: it does not expand `Tree::VirtualList` (lazy/unbounded rows) and cannot descend into a third-party `Component` it has no compile-time knowledge of. A future phase adding a generic `Element::VisitChildren()` hook could upgrade this without changing `WidgetTestDriver`'s own public surface. |
| 2026-07-11 | `driver.State<T>(path)` was not implemented; `driver.Component()` gives direct read/write access to the top-level component under test instead | Add a `GetInheritedValue`-style type-erased virtual accessor to `Element`/`ComponentElement<T>` so state could be read from an arbitrary path | Confirmed via a full read of `Tests/Tree/State_test.cpp`/`Reconciler_test.cpp`: **every existing test in this codebase already inspects state by keeping its own copy of the `State<T>`/component handle in scope** — none reach into a mounted `ComponentElement<T>` (whose `_component` member is private with no accessor). `driver.Component()` is exactly that established pattern, just owned by the driver instead of a separate test-local variable — no new precedent, no framework change. | Nested descendant-component state (a `State<T>` several `Component`s deep, not on the driver's own top-level `T`) isn't reachable through the driver at all — same boundary every existing test already lives within; test authors needing that should hold their own handle copy exactly as `State_test.cpp` already does. |
| 2026-07-11 | The proposal's "rendering" test mode (`HeadlessViewport::ReadPixels()`-backed visual assertions) was not attempted at all this phase — not even a stub | Build a thin pass-through that calls `ReadPixels()` and documents it as "not yet meaningful" | Confirmed via full reads: `Internal::HeadlessBackend::ReadPixels()` always returns a zero-filled buffer (Phase 19's own doc comment: "requires a GPU-backed implementation"); `Rendering::HeadlessViewport::ReadPixels()` only reflects whatever a test's own `OnRender` callback manually wrote into it — neither performs real ImGui draw-list rasterization. A "rendering mode" built on top of either would assert on nothing meaningful, which is worse than not having it (a false sense of visual coverage). Phase 31's own "What Phase 31 Inherits" section already anticipates `WidgetTestDriver` evolving once real command-buffer/rendering capture exists. | Explicitly flagged as deferred, not silently dropped — future phases adding real headless rasterization (Phase 31 candidate) should extend `WidgetTestDriver` with a rendering mode at that point, not before. |
| 2026-07-11 | The internal `ChildrenOf()` dispatch helper lives in the single project-wide `ImFrame::Internal::` namespace block (unqualified `Internal::ChildrenOf(...)` at the call site inside `Tree::FindDescendant`), not a `Tree`-nested one — mirroring `Widget.hpp`'s established `ComponentElement<T>` placement exactly | Write `ImFrame::Internal::ChildrenOf(...)` fully-qualified at the call site for clarity | Caught by `check_api_leaks.sh` Check 2 (no `ImFrame::Internal::` in public headers) on first attempt — the fully-qualified form contains the literal forbidden string even though the *unqualified* form (relying on ordinary enclosing-namespace lookup, safe here since no `Tree::Internal` namespace exists to collide with, unlike the Phase 27 incident) does not. Fixed by removing the qualification. | Confirms the Phase 27 lesson generalizes: any future public-header code that needs to call into the shared `ImFrame::Internal::` namespace from inside `ImFrame::Tree::` (or any other module) should call it unqualified, not spell out `ImFrame::Internal::`, both to pass the leak-check and to avoid the lookup-collision class of bug it guards against. |

## Phase 30.5 — Reconciler Performance Audit (test-only; no algorithm changes)

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-14 | The audit found `Internal::ReconcileChildren`/`ReconcileChild` (`src/Tree/ElementInternal.cpp`) and `ComponentElement<T>`'s dirty-flag coalescing (`include/ImFrame/Tree/Widget.hpp`) already satisfy every one of the proposal's adversarial cases — no algorithm changes were made this phase, only new regression/stress test coverage (`Tests/Tree/ReconcilerPerformance_test.cpp`, 5 test cases) | Rewrite `ReconcileChildren` proactively (e.g. an explicit LIS-based reorder-minimising diff, matching React's key-reconciliation algorithm) in case the existing linear-scan-with-cursor approach hid a subtle O(n²) case | Read through the algorithm line-by-line before writing any test: keyed matching is an `unordered_map` lookup (O(1) amortised); unkeyed matching uses a monotonic cursor that only ever advances on a successful match and never rescans, so each new widget costs O(1) amortised against the old list regardless of how many consecutive mismatches occur — confirmed empirically via a 2,500-vs-10,000-item timed reconciliation (`largeMs < smallMs * 10 + 50`; actual measured ratio was ~5x for a 4x input, consistent with O(n), far below the ~16x an O(n²) algorithm would show). `ComponentElement<T>`'s dirty flag is a single `atomic<bool>`, so any number of `State<T>::Set()` calls between `Update()` polls collapses to exactly one `Rebuild()` — verified directly (1000 `Set()` calls → `buildCalls` increments by exactly 1). | If a future phase adds a feature to `ReconcileChildren` (e.g. `LayoutBuilder`-style reordering hints), re-run `ReconcilerPerformance_test.cpp`'s large-flat-list case before merging — it is the regression guard for "no O(n^2) case" going forward, not just a one-time audit artifact. |
| 2026-07-14 | Adversarial coverage added: 100-level deep nesting (no stack overflow), 10,000-item flat list (O(n) scaling via timed ratio check), alternating widget types at one position over 2,000 frames (no leaked elements), 1000 `State::Set()` calls in one frame (coalesces to 1 rebuild), and 10,000 frames of continuous structural churn across 50 live slots with periodic type-changes (no leaked elements) | Use a real profiler (e.g. Tracy, VTune) to produce flamegraphs/allocation traces instead of hand-written Catch2 timing assertions | No profiler is currently wired into the build; adding one is out of scope for a test-only audit phase. Catch2 assertions with a generous absolute time ceiling (`< 500ms` for 10,000 items) plus a loose scaling-ratio check (`< 10x` for a 4x input, when O(n) empirically measured ~5x and O(n²) would be ~16x) are robust enough to catch a genuine regression by orders of magnitude without being flaky on CI hardware variance — the same style of test this codebase already uses for its other perf-sensitive paths (e.g. `ThreadPool`). Leak-freedom is verified via the same `Counters{created, mounted, updated, unmounted}` instrumented-`Element` idiom `Reconciler_test.cpp` already established, not a heap profiler — `created == unmounted` after a full unmount is the exact no-leak invariant this architecture guarantees when the algorithm is correct. | If a real profiling toolchain is added in a later phase, these tests remain valid regression guards and don't need to be replaced — they'd be a useful *addition* to, not a *replacement* for, flamegraph-based investigation of any newly-discovered hot path. |

## Phase 30.6 — Backend Conformance Expansion (widget-tree coverage; cross-backend pixel-diff still deferred)

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-15 | The proposal's literal design — render `ConformanceApp` on `HeadlessBackend`, then diff all five real backends against that render within a 3-per-channel pixel threshold — was **not attempted**; the user was asked directly and chose to scope down rather than pause the Phase 30 roadmap or skip this sub-phase entirely | Attempt the literal cross-backend pixel-diff anyway, using whichever backend renders "close enough" as an ad-hoc reference | Confirmed via full reads that `Internal::HeadlessBackend::ReadPixels()` (`Backends/Headless/HeadlessBackend.cpp`) returns a buffer that is zero-initialised at `Init()` and never written — `EndFrame()` explicitly discards the ImGui draw data with the comment "no GPU submission in headless mode." This was already known and deferred to Phase 36's planned `SoftwareRenderer` as far back as 2026-06-17 (`VulkanConformance_test.cpp`'s own file header, and this file's `DECISIONS.md` row from that date) — Phase 30.6 does not change that fact, it was never going to. A pixel-diff against a backend that renders nothing would either assert on garbage or silently pass on an empty comparison, which is worse than not having the check. | Cross-backend pixel-equality (the proposal's actual v2.0.0 release-criteria wording, "all five backends pass conformance tests within pixel threshold") remains an open v2.0.0 gap, tracked here and in `PHASE_STATUS.md`, not silently dropped — revisit once Phase 36 lands a real headless rasterizer. |
| 2026-07-15 | `GLFWOpenGL3Backend` gained a new `ReadPixels()` method (`Backends/GLFWOpenGL3/GLFWOpenGL3Backend.{hpp,cpp}`) — a real, if small, backend-implementation change, not test-only scope | Skip OpenGL3 entirely and only expand Vulkan/WebGPU/DX12 (the three backends that already had a `ReadPixels()` hook from Phases 20-22) | `IBackend`'s virtual interface never declared `ReadPixels()` — every backend that had one (Vulkan/DX12/WebGPU/Headless) added it individually as a backend-specific test hook. `GLFWOpenGL3Backend` simply never got one, and OpenGL3 is explicitly named in the proposal as the intended reference backend ("`HeadlessBackend` (OpenGL3)"). Captured during `EndFrame()`, before `glfwSwapBuffers()` — reading the default framebuffer after a swap returns the *next* frame's stale back buffer — with a row flip to normalise OpenGL's bottom-left readback origin to the top-left-origin convention every other backend's `ReadPixels()` already uses. | Established the pattern any future ImFrame backend without a `ReadPixels()` hook should follow: capture inside `EndFrame()` before presentation, non-virtual (not part of `IBackend`), top-left-origin RGBA8. |
| 2026-07-15 | Discovered mid-implementation: `GLFWOpenGL3Backend::Init()` calls `glfwShowWindow()` despite the `GLFW_VISIBLE, GLFW_FALSE` window hint set earlier — the window is a real, briefly-visible OS window, not a true headless surface (unlike Vulkan/DX12/WebGPU's offscreen render targets). On this dev machine, a requested 128×128 `WindowConfig` sometimes produces an actual framebuffer with a different height (observed: 128×148) — an OS-level minimum-window-size effect, not a bug in `ReadPixels()` itself (the captured buffer's size *did* correctly match the real framebuffer; the mismatch was against the *requested* config, not against what was actually rendered) | Force an exact window size via additional GLFW hints/`glfwSetWindowSizeLimits`, or hard-code an assumption that requested size always equals actual size | The generic fix — query `IBackend::WindowSize()` (already public, already implemented) after `Init()` and use *that* for all pixel-offset math and size assertions, rather than assuming it equals the `WindowConfig` request — sidesteps the platform quirk entirely regardless of its exact root cause, and is more correct in general (any backend's actual framebuffer size is the only truth that matters for `ReadPixels()`). Vulkan/DX12/WebGPU's offscreen render targets don't have this problem (confirmed: their pre-existing tests already passed with hardcoded sizes), so only `OpenGL3Conformance_test.cpp` needed this adjustment. | Any future OpenGL3-backend test that calls `ReadPixels()` should query `WindowSize()` rather than assume the requested `WindowConfig` size, on this and possibly other platforms. |
| 2026-07-15 | `Tests/Backends/ConformanceApp.hpp` (new, shared across all 4 conformance test files) touches a representative cross-section of the widget library — `ButtonWidget`, `CheckboxWidget`, `SliderWidget<float>`, `TextInputWidget<std::string>`, `ComboWidget<int>`, `ProgressBarWidget`, `ColorEditWidget`, `RadioWidget` ×3, `GridWidget`, `TableWidget`, plus `Flex`/`Box`/`Expanded` — rather than a literal enumeration of every `Tree::Primitives::*`/`Tree::*Widget` type | Enumerate genuinely every remaining widget type (also `VirtualList`, `Portal`, `DockSpaceWidget`, `PropertyGridWidget`) | Matches the same documented-boundary precedent `WidgetTestDriver::FindDescendant` already established in Phase 30.4 — a representative, deliberately-bounded cross-section that actually exercises `Layout()`/`Paint()` through a real backend, sized so 4 themes × 3 layout sizes × 4 backends stays fast (the full expanded suite added ~16 seconds total across `unit`/`vulkan`/`webgpu` labels). `VirtualList`'s lazy/unbounded rows and `Portal`'s separate-registry rendering path are structurally awkward to fit into a single-frame conformance snapshot and are already covered by their own dedicated unit tests (`Tests/Tree/VirtualList_test.cpp`, `Portal_test.cpp`). | If a later phase wants stricter "every widget type" coverage, extend `ConformanceApp.hpp` directly — every conformance test file automatically picks up the change since they all share this one fixture. |
| 2026-07-15 | `DX12Conformance_test.cpp`'s expansion was written and reviewed following the exact pattern verified working on Vulkan/WebGPU/OpenGL3, but **not locally compiled or run** (`IMF_BUILD_DX12_BACKEND=OFF` in this dev environment's CMake cache) | Flip `IMF_BUILD_DX12_BACKEND=ON` in this environment to verify it | Matches the existing precedent already set for the Metal backend (see this file's earlier Metal row: "written and reviewed without ever compiling it") — this dev machine's cache has DX12 off by default, and changing that flag to chase a one-off verification is a bigger footprint than this test-only sub-phase warrants. The code is structurally identical to the already-verified Vulkan/WebGPU expansions, which reduces risk, but this is an explicit, tracked exception to the "always build+run what you write" rule. | Whoever next builds with `IMF_BUILD_DX12_BACKEND=ON` (e.g. CI, or a future phase) should treat `DX12Conformance_test.cpp`'s new theme/layout-size test cases as first-run-unverified and watch them closely. |
