Genericizing these would require inventing new command types that are really just "call this specific ImGui function," defeating the abstraction's purpose. | The "RenderObjects/ is the only code permitted to call raw ImGui functions" invariant (already true today) is unchanged by Phase 31 — it just gains a second, narrower invariant on top: `BoxElement`/`TextElement` specifically must route through `CommandBuffer` rather than calling ImGui directly. `grep -r "ImGui::" src/Tree/RenderObjects/` will NOT return zero after Phase 31, unlike the proposal's literal invariant text — this is a deliberate, documented deviation, not an oversight. A future phase wanting GestureRegion/VirtualList/DockSpace to also be renderer-agnostic would need new interaction-abstraction primitives (e.g. a `HitTestRegion` command or an `IInputProvider` interface), which is out of scope here. |

## Phase 31.1 — `CommandBuffer` Core Type

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-16 | No `src/Rendering/CommandBuffer.cpp` was written, despite the proposal's "New Files" list naming one | Write an empty or near-empty `.cpp` to match the proposal's literal file list | Every `CommandBuffer`/`TextureId`/`FontId` method (`Push`, `Reset`, `Size`, `Empty`, `Capacity`, equality) is a one-line, header-inlinable operation — there is no non-trivial logic to put in a separate translation unit. This matches the existing project precedent (`include/ImFrame/Widgets/Types.hpp` has no `Types.cpp` either) rather than creating a ceremonial empty file. | If `CommandBuffer` later grows genuinely non-trivial logic (e.g. a real multi-buffer pool once frame pipelining exists), add the `.cpp` then — this is not a standing decision to never have one. |
| 2026-07-16 | `CommandBuffer`'s "pool" is a single reused `std::vector<Command>` cleared via `Reset()` (which keeps capacity), not a literal pool of multiple buffer instances | Implement an actual ring-buffer/pool of N `CommandBuffer` instances, matching the proposal's literal "allocated per frame from a pool" wording | The render loop (`Reconciler::Show()`) is single-threaded and synchronous — `Paint()` fully completes before the next frame's reconciliation begins, so there is exactly one `CommandBuffer` in flight at any time. A multi-buffer pool exists to let one frame's GPU submission overlap with the next frame's CPU recording (frame pipelining), which this codebase doesn't do anywhere yet. `vector::clear()` already gives "no reallocation once warmed up" for free, verified directly (`CommandBuffer_test.cpp`'s "Reset() clears commands but retains allocated capacity" test: capacity is identical before and after a `Reset()` + re-push cycle of the same size). | If a future phase introduces frame pipelining (recording frame N+1 while frame N's renderer is still processing), revisit this — a real multi-buffer pool would then have something to pipeline against. |
| 2026-07-16 | `Rendering::TextureId`/`Rendering::FontId` are new opaque wrapper types (`uint64_t`/`uint32_t` respectively), distinct from the already-existing `Widgets::TextureHandle` (`using TextureHandle = void*`, matching ImGui's default `ImTextureID`) | Reuse `Widgets::TextureHandle` directly as `CommandBuffer::DrawImage`'s texture field | The proposal is explicit that `TextureId` must be renderer-interpreted: `ImGuiCompatRenderer` treats it as an `ImTextureID`, but a future native renderer (Phase 32) treats it as an index into its own texture registry — a raw `void*` doesn't fit that second meaning at all (an index is not a pointer). Keeping them as two distinct types now avoids a silent, wrong implicit conversion once Phase 32 exists. | Phase 32's `NativeRenderer` will need its own `TextureId -> internal registry index` mapping; `ImGuiCompatRenderer` (Phase 31.2) will need a `TextureId -> ImTextureID` mapping (likely just `static_cast<ImTextureID>(id.Value())` given `ImTextureID` is itself `void*`-sized on this ImGui build, but that conversion belongs in the renderer, not in the opaque type itself). |

## Phase 31.2 — `IRenderer` Interface + `ImGuiCompatRenderer`

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-16 | **Confirmed at implementation time**: this ImGui build (vcpkg imgui 1.92.8) defines `ImTextureID = ImU64` (a 64-bit integer), not `void*` — the pre-1.92 default this project's own `Widgets::TextureHandle` doc comment assumed. `ImDrawList::AddImage`/`AddImageRounded` now take `ImTextureRef` (constructible directly from an `ImTextureID`/`ImU64`, or from `void*` via a legacy-compat constructor) | Route `Rendering::TextureId::Value()` through the `void*` legacy-compat `ImTextureRef` constructor, matching `Widgets::TextureHandle`'s existing assumption | `TextureId::Value()` is already a `uint64_t` — constructing `ImTextureRef{static_cast<ImTextureID>(id.Value())}` is a direct, lossless match to the modern API with no pointer-punning involved, whereas going through the `void*` overload would be an unnecessary, less honest detour for a type that was never a pointer in the first place. | `Widgets::TextureHandle`'s doc comment ("matching the default `ImTextureID = void*`") is now stale for this ImGui version — out of scope to fix here since it's an unrelated, pre-existing type this phase doesn't touch, but flagged for whoever next touches `ImageWidget`/`DrawContext::DrawImage`. |
| 2026-07-16 | `PushOpacityLayer`/`PushBlendLayer`/`PopLayer` are implemented as structural no-ops in `ImGuiCompatRenderer` — the `Opacity`/`BlendMode` values are accepted (no assertion/crash) but not applied to pixel output | Wire up `ImDrawListSplitter` (`ChannelsSplit`/`ChannelsSetCurrent`/`ChannelsMerge`) for real layer compositing | `ImDrawListSplitter` only reorders *when* draw commands execute within one draw list — it does not blend one channel's output over another at a given opacity or blend mode; genuine per-layer alpha/blend compositing needs an offscreen render target (an FBO), which ImGui's immediate-mode draw-list model doesn't provide without building real render-to-texture infrastructure (arguably a Phase 32-native-renderer-scale undertaking). Zero current producers emit these commands (`BoxElement`/`TextElement`, the only two migrated so far, never use opacity/blend layers) — implementing broken-looking-functional compositing now would be worse than an honest no-op. | Verified explicitly: the corresponding `ImGuiCompatRenderer_test.cpp` test only asserts these commands don't crash `Render()`/`EndFrame()`, not that they visually composite. A future phase adding real layer support (once offscreen render targets exist) should replace this no-op, not extend it. |
| 2026-07-16 | `DrawPath`'s `FillRule` field is accepted but not applied — fill always goes through `ImDrawList::AddConvexPolyFilled` via the `Path*` stateful API, which is only correct for convex polygons regardless of the requested winding rule | Implement a real tessellator supporting non-zero/even-odd winding for concave/self-intersecting paths | Matches the proposal's own framing of the ImGui compat renderer's `DrawPath` translation as "path approximation," and — same as the opacity-layer decision above — `DrawPath` currently has zero producers (only `Box`/`Text` emit commands after Phase 31.3), so there is nothing yet depending on concave-path correctness. A full tessellator (e.g. via `earcut`-style triangulation) is real scope, not appropriate to add speculatively. | If a future primitive needs genuine concave/self-intersecting path fills (e.g. an SVG-icon renderer), that phase should add real tessellation rather than assume this compat renderer already handles it. |
| 2026-07-16 | **Bug found in existing `BoxRO.cpp`, naturally fixed as a side effect of this phase (not patched directly)**: `BoxElement::Paint()`'s border-stroke call, `dl->AddRect(pMin, pMax, ToImU32(border), _config.GetRadius(), 0, _config.GetBorderWidth())`, was written against the OLD (pre-1.92.8) `AddRect(min, max, col, rounding, flags, thickness)` parameter order. This ImGui build's current signature is `AddRect(min, max, col, rounding, thickness, flags)` (the old order is `= delete`d in this header) — meaning the existing call actually passes `thickness=0` and reinterprets the real border-width float as an `ImDrawFlags` bit-flag value | Patch `BoxRO.cpp`'s existing call directly, in this phase, as an out-of-scope drive-by fix | `ImGuiCompatRenderer.cpp`'s new `DrawRect` translation (`dl->AddRect(pMin, pMax, col, rounding, rect.StrokeWidth, flags)`) was written correctly from scratch against the current signature — verified directly by `ImGuiCompatRenderer_test.cpp`'s stroke test (a 4px-wide green border renders and is detected via `ReadPixels()`, with the interior correctly unfilled). Phase 31.3 replaces `BoxElement::Paint()`'s direct ImGui calls with `CommandBuffer` pushes entirely, so the buggy line is deleted, not edited, as a natural consequence of the migration — patching it separately first would be redundant and outside this phase's stated scope (translating `CommandBuffer`, not auditing pre-existing `RenderObjects/` code). | Do not treat this as "Box borders never worked" — `AddRect`'s internal thickness defaulting/clamping behavior may still produce *some* visible border with `thickness=0`; the actual severity (hairline vs. invisible vs. `GetBorderWidth()`'s bit pattern accidentally toggling a real corner-rounding flag) was not further investigated since Phase 31.3 removes the line outright. If Phase 31.3 is delayed or descoped for any reason, this bug remains live in `BoxRO.cpp` and should be fixed on its own. |

## Phase 31.3 — Thread `CommandBuffer` Through `Element::Paint()`; Migrate Box/Text

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-16 | **Scope was much wider than the Phase 31.1 survey found**: `src/Widgets/*.cpp` (`Button`, `Checkbox`, `Radio`, `Separator`, `ProgressBar`, `ColorEdit`, `Image`, plus the templated `Slider<T>`/`TextInput<T>`/`Combo<T>`) and `src/Overlay/*.cpp` (`Modal`, `ContextMenu`) each define their OWN concrete `Element` subclass calling ImGui directly (e.g. `ButtonElement::Paint()` calls `ImGui::Button()`) — contradicting `Migration_v1_to_v2.md`'s claim that these are `GestureRegion` *compositions*. They are not; they're independent `PrimitiveWidget`s | Trust the Phase 31.1 survey (scoped only to `src/Tree/RenderObjects/`) and assume the rest of the widget library was GestureRegion-composed as documented | Discovered when changing `Element::Paint()`'s signature and letting the compiler enumerate every override that broke — ~20 additional files surfaced beyond the 14 already known from Phase 31.1. Confirms the earlier survey's conclusion is even stronger than stated: virtually nothing outside `Box`/`Text` can be genericized behind `CommandBuffer` — the entire public widget library is bucket-B (ImGui-interaction-coupled), not just the 4 `RenderObjects/` files previously identified. | `Migration_v1_to_v2.md`'s "composed from GestureRegion" framing is now known-stale for `Button`/`Checkbox`/etc. — out of scope to correct in this phase (it's describing Phase 29 history, not Phase 31 work), but flag for whoever next edits that guide. |
| 2026-07-16 | The ~20-file mechanical signature update (add `Rendering::CommandBuffer&` as `Paint()`'s first parameter, forward it unchanged to any child `Paint()` call, mark it `/*cmd*/` when genuinely unused) was delegated to a background agent with an exact per-file rule and a full file list, while `Element.hpp`/`Widget.hpp`/`InheritedWidget.hpp`/`BoxRO.cpp`/`TextRO.cpp`/`VirtualListRO.cpp`/`PortalRO.hpp`/`Reconciler.{hpp,cpp}` (the files with actual logic decisions) were hand-edited | Do the entire sweep by hand | Matches the established pattern from Phase 30's `Widget(...)` cleanup — large, well-specified, low-ambiguity mechanical changes are exactly what a background agent handles reliably, freeing hand-editing time for the files where a wrong choice would be a real bug (the CommandBuffer design itself, the VirtualList clip-rect fix, the Reconciler wiring). | Explicitly instructed the agent to *read* `Modal.cpp`/`ContextMenu.cpp` rather than assume "no forwarding" for them, since overlays plausibly paint a child `Widget`. It correctly found both DO forward (to their popup content) and handled them as forwarding cases — verified by a full-solution rebuild afterward, which caught zero remaining signature mismatches. |
| 2026-07-16 | **`VirtualListElement` gives each frame's visible rows their own scoped, locally-flushed `CommandBuffer`** (built during the row-paint loop, replayed via a fresh `ImGuiCompatRenderer` immediately after the loop, *before* `ImGui::EndChild()`) — it does NOT push into the caller's frame-wide buffer the way every other forwarding `Element` does | Push rows' commands into the same frame-wide buffer `Reconciler::Show()` replays once at the very end of the frame, matching every other forwarding element | `VirtualList` is the one migrated-adjacent case that opens its own real nested ImGui window (`BeginChild`/`EndChild`) around content that can itself be `Box`/`Text` (confirmed: `ConformanceApp`'s `TableWidget` is `VirtualList`-based and renders `Text` cells). If row commands were pushed into the shared frame-wide buffer instead, they wouldn't replay until `Reconciler::Show()`'s single end-of-frame `Render()` call — by which point `EndChild()` has already closed that child window, so `ImGui::GetWindowDrawList()` at replay time returns the ROOT window's draw list, not the child's: the rows would render with no scroll-region clipping and in the wrong z-order relative to the child window's own background. Flushing locally, while the child window is still current, avoids this entirely. | This is now the established pattern for any future bucket-B `Element` that opens its own nested ImGui window *and* may have `Box`/`Text`-capable children: flush a locally-scoped buffer before closing that window, don't push into the ambient one. `GestureRegion` doesn't need this (no nested window — `InvisibleButton` is just an item in the current window); `RootBridge`/`Portal` don't need it either (both stay in the root window's context through to the single end-of-frame `Render()` call). Verified end-to-end: all `ConformanceApp` conformance tests (which include a `TableWidget`) pass with real pixel output through the actual `GLFWOpenGL3Backend`/`SDL3VulkanBackend`/`DawnWebGPUBackend`. |
| 2026-07-16 | `Reconciler` owns a fixed `Internal::ImGuiCompatRenderer _renderer` member directly (not an `Application`-level swappable `std::unique_ptr<Internal::IRenderer>`, despite the proposal's "`Application` holds a `unique_ptr<IRenderer>`. The default is `ImGuiCompatRenderer`" framing) | Add the `unique_ptr<IRenderer>` indirection to `Application` now, threading it down into `Reconciler` | There is only one `IRenderer` implementation today — the swappable-pointer indirection has nothing to select between yet, and adding it now would be pure ceremony ahead of need. `Application`'s own public API (`WithFont`/`WithTheme`/`SetRoot`/etc.) is untouched by this phase, keeping Phase 31.3's surface area to the tree-internals layer only. | Phase 32 (`NativeRenderer`) is the natural point to promote this to an `Application`-level `unique_ptr<IRenderer>` choice — when `Reconciler` first needs to actually select between two implementations, not before. |
| 2026-07-16 | `Examples/DemoApp`'s local `TreePanel` helper reaches directly into `src/Rendering/Renderers/ImGuiCompatRenderer.hpp` (an `Internal::` header, not public API) via a new `target_include_directories(DemoApp PRIVATE ${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/src)` — there is currently no PUBLIC way for a consumer to replay a `CommandBuffer` into ImGui | Give `TreePanel` (or a public equivalent) a real public API for this, e.g. exposing `Internal::ImGuiCompatRenderer` publicly or adding a thin public wrapper | `TreePanel` already reaches into `Internal::` territory by design — its own file comment says it "reproduces exactly what `Internal::Reconciler::Show()` does, minus the window it opens," specifically because `Application::SetRoot()` cannot support DemoApp's independently-dockable multi-panel layout. Adding a new public rendering API was judged out of scope for a phase about the internal `CommandBuffer` plumbing itself. | **Known, accepted gap**: any real (non-example) consumer wanting `TreePanel`'s exact pattern (multiple independently-dockable `Tree`-driven panels outside `SetRoot()`) has no supported public way to turn recorded commands into pixels today. If this pattern turns out to be commonly needed, a future phase should expose a minimal public helper (e.g. `Tree::PaintStandalone(Element&, Vec2)` or similar) rather than requiring every consumer to reach into `src/` the way `DemoApp` now does. |

## Phase 31.4 — Verification + Documentation (Phase 31 closing summary)

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-16 | Corrected `Docs/Architecture.md`'s "(c) Public widget/layout/theme library" section, which claimed "A `ButtonWidget` is a `GestureRegion` wrapping a `Box`" — written in Phase 30.7, before Phase 31.3's survey disproved it | Leave the doc as-is since it's a Phase 30.7 artifact, not something this phase's actual code changes touch | An incorrect architectural claim in the project's own public documentation is worth fixing as soon as it's discovered to be wrong, regardless of which phase originally wrote it — leaving it would actively mislead a reader about how the widget library is actually built, which is the exact failure mode DOC_STANDARDS exists to prevent. Low-risk, self-contained, high-value fix. | `Docs/Migration_v1_to_v2.md` was checked too and does NOT contain the same claim (it was specific to `Architecture.md` and `Examples/DemoApp/main.cpp`'s own file comment) — no change needed there. |
| 2026-07-16 | **Phase 31 closing summary.** Delivered: `Rendering::CommandBuffer` (full command set per the proposal), `Internal::IRenderer`/`ImGuiCompatRenderer` (complete translation for `DrawRect`/`DrawText`/`DrawImage`/`DrawPath`/`DrawShadow`/clip push-pop; opacity/blend layers structurally accepted but not yet compositing), and `Box`/`Text` migrated off direct ImGui calls, with `Reconciler` replaying one buffer per frame. **Deliberately not delivered, and why:** (a) every other `Element` in the codebase (`GestureRegion`, `VirtualList`, `RootBridge`, `DockSpace`, and the entire `Widgets::*`/`Overlay::*`/`Rendering::*Widget` library) still calls ImGui directly — their drawing is inseparable from ImGui's own interaction/window state, confirmed by direct survey of every `Paint()` override in the codebase, not assumed; (b) `PushOpacityLayer`/`PushBlendLayer`/`PopLayer` don't actually composite (needs an offscreen render target ImGui's draw-list model doesn't have); (c) `DrawPath`'s `FillRule` isn't honored (convex-only approximation); (d) `FontId` isn't wired to any font registry (Phase 33's job); (e) no `Application`-level swappable `unique_ptr<IRenderer>` (deferred to Phase 32, when there's a second implementation to pick between); (f) the "zero visible change" invariant is verified via self-consistency (real pixel assertions on real backends) rather than a true zero-diff comparison against a captured v2.0.0 reference-image baseline, since none was captured (explicit user decision, see the Interstitial section above) | Treat any of (a)-(f) as a blocking gap requiring further work before Phase 31 is considered done | Every one of (a)-(f) was a scope decision made explicitly during 31.1-31.3, not something discovered as incomplete during 31.4's closing review — each has its own dedicated `DECISIONS.md` row with full reasoning. Phase 31's own proposal states its value is "entirely architectural" and that it "enables Phases 32-37" — the enabling architecture (a working `CommandBuffer`/`IRenderer` seam with at least one real producer/consumer pair proven correct end-to-end) is in place; expanding which `Element`s use it is explicitly a *later* phase's work, not a gap in this one. | Whoever starts Phase 32 (native GPU renderer) should read this row plus the individual 31.1-31.3 entries before assuming `CommandBuffer` covers more of the tree than it does — it covers exactly `Box` and `Text`, nothing else, by design. |

## Interstitial — Phase 32 Kickoff (between Phase 31.4 and 32.1)

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-27 | Phase 32 is broken into sub-phases (32.1+, granularity TBD as work proceeds) rather than attempted as one monolithic phase, matching the established Phase 31.1–31.4 pattern | Implement `NativeRenderer` + all five per-backend implementations + the shader cross-compilation pipeline + batching + atlas + clip stack as a single pass | The proposal's scope (5 GPU backends, a new build-time shader toolchain via `glslang`/`SPIRV-Cross`, and 4 new supporting subsystems) is materially larger than Phase 31's, which itself needed 4 sub-phases for a much narrower `CommandBuffer`-only scope. Splitting lets each piece be verified independently (mirrors Phase 31.1 shipping `CommandBuffer` alone, with zero renderer, before Phase 31.2 built the first consumer). | Sub-phase boundaries: **32.1** — `BatchBuilder`/`TextureAtlas`/`ClipStack` core types only, no GPU calls, no shaders, no `NativeRenderer` backend dispatch (pure CPU-side data structures, unit-testable in isolation, same spirit as 31.1's standalone `CommandBuffer`). Later sub-phases add the shader build pipeline, the first real backend (GL3 — no cross-compilation needed, already proven buildable on this machine), then the two other locally-buildable backends (Vulkan, WebGPU), with Metal/DX12 scaffolded but not fully verified here (same "honestly documented gap" pattern as Phase 30.7 criterion 3). |
| 2026-07-27 | **`TextureAtlas`'s rectangle packer is a self-contained shelf/skyline implementation written for this project, not the proposal's literal "`rectpack2D` algorithm (header-only, vendored)"** | Vendor the actual `rectpack2D` (TeamHypersomnia) source into the repo as instructed by the proposal | Vendoring means pulling third-party source from an external repository into this codebase; this session has no established, reviewed path for fetching and auditing unreviewed external source before committing it (downloading and executing/compiling unreviewed third-party code from an unverified source is treated as a hard boundary, not a judgment call, independent of this project's own conventions). A shelf/skyline packer is a well-understood, small (~100-line), and easily-reviewable algorithm that satisfies `TextureAtlas`'s actual contract (`Alloc(width, height) -> AtlasRegion`, grow-on-full up to `MaxAtlasSize`) without introducing an unaudited dependency. | If the user wants the literal `rectpack2D` library instead (e.g. for its specific packing-quality characteristics), they should fetch/vendor it themselves — Claude Code will not download third-party source from the internet into the repo. The `TextureAtlas` public interface (`Alloc`/`Upload`/`TextureId`) is unaffected either way, so swapping the packing algorithm later is an internal, non-breaking change. |
| 2026-07-27 | **Carried forward from the Phase 31 `DECISIONS.md` review** (see the Phase 31.1–31.4 rows above) as still-open considerations for Phase 32: (1) no captured reference-image baseline exists — `NativeRenderer_test.cpp`'s planned "compare against Phase 31 baseline" must mean self-consistency output from `ImGuiCompatRenderer`, not a true pre-refactor reference; (2) `Application`-level `unique_ptr<IRenderer>` was explicitly deferred to "when `Reconciler` first needs to actually select between two implementations" — that point is now; (3) `CommandBuffer` has exactly two producers (`Box`/`Text`) — every other `Element` (`GestureRegion`, `VirtualList`, `DockSpace`, the entire `Widgets::*`/`Overlay::*` library) still calls ImGui directly and will continue to do so regardless of `UseNativeRenderer()`; (4) `VirtualList`'s locally-scoped buffer-flush pattern (Phase 31.3) is precedent for any future nested-window batching/clip-stack edge case; (5) `DrawPath`'s unhonored `FillRule` and `DrawShadow`'s `ImGuiCompatRenderer`-only implementation are already accounted for in the Phase 32 proposal (CPU tesselator, explicit fallback) — no new decision needed; (6) `TextureId`/`FontId` were deliberately kept opaque and renderer-interpreted specifically so `NativeRenderer` could treat `TextureId` as a registry index rather than a pointer | Re-derive these from scratch during 32.1 implementation | These were surfaced by a direct re-read of Phase 31's `DECISIONS.md` entries before starting Phase 32, at the user's request, rather than left implicit | **Point (3) is the one substantive open question, not yet resolved**: once `UseNativeRenderer()` is active, `Box`/`Text` render via real GPU draw calls while every other widget still emits direct ImGui draw-list calls in the same frame — how the two composite (same render target? draw order? split render passes?) is not addressed by the proposal and is deferred past 32.1 (which has no backend implementation yet to make the question concrete). Whoever implements the first real backend (32.2+) must resolve this before `UseNativeRenderer()` produces a correct frame for anything beyond the isolated reference test scene — treat the proposal's own "opt-in... until Phase 37" framing as license to ship 32.1-32.x with this composited-frame question still open, not license to ignore it forever. |

## Phase 32.2 — Shader Cross-Compilation Pipeline (GLSL → SPIR-V via glslang)

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-27 | The entire Phase 32.2 pipeline (`glslang[tools]` vcpkg dependency, `cmake/CompileShader.cmake`, `Shaders/*.glsl`, the generated-header target) is gated behind a new `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER` CMake option, default `OFF`, exactly mirroring how `IMF_BACKEND_SDL3_VULKAN`/`IMF_BUILD_METAL_BACKEND`/etc. gate their own vcpkg manifest features before `project()` | Make `glslang[tools]` a core, always-installed dependency like `imgui`/`glfw3`/`glad` | `glslang[tools]` is a real build-time toolchain (a C++ compiler frontend, not a lightweight header), and `NativeRenderer` itself is entirely optional — `Application::UseNativeRenderer()` has no real callers anywhere in the codebase yet, only its own isolated 32.1/32.2 tests. A default `cmake -B build` should not silently grow a new toolchain requirement for a feature nobody has opted into, matching this project's existing precedent for every other optional heavyweight dependency (SDL3, Vulkan, Dawn). | Anyone building `NativeRenderer`-related work must pass `-DIMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=ON`. CI's default job matrix does not need it until a real `NativeRenderer` backend exists; add a dedicated CI job alongside the Vulkan/WebGPU ones once Phase 32.3+ ships a backend worth testing in CI. |
| 2026-07-27 | Each shader is **one `.glsl` file combining both vertex and fragment stages** behind `#if defined(STAGE_VERTEX)` / `#elif defined(STAGE_FRAGMENT)` guards, compiled twice by `compile_shader()` (once per stage, via glslangValidator's `-D<define>` flag) into two separate SPIR-V modules and two separate generated headers (`Rect.vert.hpp`/`Rect.frag.hpp`, etc.) | Author separate `Rect.vert.glsl`/`Rect.frag.glsl` files per stage | `PHASE_32_PROPOSAL.md`'s own "New Files" list names exactly three files — `Rect.glsl`, `Image.glsl`, `Path.glsl` — not six. The single-file-two-stages technique (a common cross-platform-shader pattern) satisfies that literal file list while still producing genuinely separate, independently-compiled SPIR-V modules per stage; `RoundedBoxSdf`'s helper function is naturally shared between the vertex and fragment sections of the same file with zero duplication *within* one shader (it does still need duplicating *between* `Rect.glsl` and `Image.glsl`, which both use it — documented in both files' comments as a deliberate no-`#include` simplification). | If a fourth stage type (compute, geometry) is ever needed, extend the same `#if defined(STAGE_*)` pattern rather than introducing per-stage files — keeps `Shaders/` matching the proposal's file list at every future phase, not just this one. |
| 2026-07-27 | The generated header embeds **both** the raw GLSL source (as a C++ raw string literal, for the future GL3 backend and for debugging) **and** the compiled SPIR-V binary (as a `constexpr unsigned char[]`, `alignas(4)`, for Vulkan/WebGPU/Metal/DX12 backends and any future `SPIRV-Cross` cross-compilation step) — verified in `Tests/Rendering/ShaderPipeline_test.cpp` to be non-empty and to start with SPIR-V's `0x07230203` magic number | Embed only the SPIR-V bytes, matching the proposal's literal "compiled shaders are embedded in the binary as constexpr byte arrays" wording | The proposal's own OpenGL3 backend description says "uses GLSL with a compatibility profile" — a text-based backend has no use for a SPIR-V blob unless it also depends on `SPIRV-Cross` to convert it back to GLSL, which is unnecessary extra work when the original GLSL source is sitting right there and already validated by the same `glslangValidator` invocation. Embedding both costs nothing (the source text is a few KB) and avoids a real future backend needing to guess which representation it should have received. | `spirv-cross` was **not** added as a vcpkg dependency in this sub-phase — nothing calls into it yet. Add it (as a `native-renderer` feature dependency, same gating pattern) when Metal/DX12/WebGPU shader cross-compilation is actually implemented, not before. |
| 2026-07-27 | The hex→byte-array conversion in `cmake/EmbedShaderSource.cmake` uses a single `string(REGEX REPLACE "([0-9a-f][0-9a-f])" "0x\\1, " ...)` call over the whole hex-encoded `.spv`, not a `foreach(RANGE ...)` loop over individual bytes | Loop byte-by-byte with `string(SUBSTRING ...)` inside `foreach(RANGE 0 ${byte_count})`, the more commonly-seen CMake pattern for this | A per-byte `foreach` in CMake script mode is known-slow (each iteration pays real interpreter overhead); a single global regex replace does the equivalent transformation in one pass. Verified directly: all 6 shader/stage combinations (Rect/Image/Path × vertex/fragment) compiled and embedded correctly, with `sizeof(kXSpirv) == kXSpirvByteCount` holding exactly in `ShaderPipeline_test.cpp`. | If a future shader source file becomes large enough that even the regex-replace approach is slow, revisit — but for UI-sized shaders (hundreds to a few thousand bytes of compiled SPIR-V, confirmed by this sub-phase's actual output) this is not a concern. |
| 2026-07-27 | **Verified empirically rather than assumed**: `glslangValidator`'s actual CLI flags on this machine's vcpkg-built `glslang[core,tools]@16.3.0` are `-S <vert\|frag>` (stage), `-V` (emit Vulkan-semantics SPIR-V), and `-D<NAME>` (define, no space) — confirmed by actually installing the port and running real compiles against all 3 shaders × 2 stages, not by reading documentation or guessing from memory | Assume the flag syntax from general glslang familiarity and only fix it if the build failed | `glslangValidator`'s exact flag spelling varies across versions and is easy to get subtly wrong (e.g. `-D NAME` with a space, which this version does not accept as a combined single token the same way); verifying against the actual installed binary before finalizing `cmake/CompileShader.cmake` avoided shipping a plausible-looking but non-functional CMake function. | If `glslang`'s vcpkg port version changes in a future `vcpkg.json` update and the CLI contract shifts, `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=ON`'s configure/build step will fail loudly (glslangValidator errors are fatal in `compile_shader()`'s `add_custom_command`) — not silently produce empty/wrong shaders. |

## Phase 32.3 — Reconcile `BatchBuilder`'s Vertex Layout With `Rect.glsl`/`Image.glsl`

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-27 | `BatchBuilder`'s single generic `Vertex{Position, Uv, Color}` (Phase 32.1) is replaced by two kind-specific structs, `RectVertex` and `ImageVertex`, each matching its shader's `STAGE_VERTEX` inputs field-for-field (`Local`/`HalfSize`/`Radii` for the rounded-box SDF, plus `FillColor`/`StrokeColor`/`StrokeWidth` for `RectVertex` or `Uv`/`TintColor` for `ImageVertex`). `Batch::Vertices` becomes `std::variant<std::vector<RectVertex>, std::vector<ImageVertex>>` | Keep the generic `Vertex` and have the (not-yet-built) `NativeRendererGL3` reconstruct per-rect SDF parameters from a plain `Position`/`Uv`/`Color` triple at upload time | The generic `Vertex` from 32.1 was written before Phase 32.2's shaders existed and, once they did, matched neither `Rect.glsl` (needs `Local`/`HalfSize`/`Radii`/`StrokeWidth`, none of which fit in `Uv`/`Color`) nor `Image.glsl` (needs the same SDF params *plus* `Uv`/`TintColor`) — reconstructing that data downstream, in the renderer, would mean re-deriving per-vertex rect geometry the `BatchBuilder` already has on hand while iterating `DrawRect`/`DrawImage`, for no benefit. Fixing the mismatch at the source (`BatchBuilder`) keeps the renderer a dumb "upload and draw" consumer, matching this codebase's existing layering (`RenderObjects/` records intent, `IRenderer` replays it — nothing downstream re-derives what an upstream stage already computed). | This is a breaking change to `Batch`'s public shape from Phase 32.1 — accepted without ceremony because nothing outside `BatchBuilder` and its own tests consumed `Batch`/`Vertex` yet (no real renderer exists). A future addition of `BatchKind::Path` (see next row) will extend the same `std::variant` pattern, not introduce a different mechanism. |
| 2026-07-27 | `DrawPath` remains non-batchable (a `BatchFlushReason::NonBatchable` trigger, same as `DrawText`/`DrawShadow`) even though `Shaders/Path.glsl` now exists from Phase 32.2 — no `BatchKind::Path`/`PathVertex` was added this sub-phase | Add `BatchKind::Path` and a CPU polyline tesselator (segment-to-quad stroke expansion) now that a consuming shader exists | `DrawPath` has zero live producers anywhere in the tree today (confirmed in Phase 31.2's `DECISIONS.md` entry, unchanged since) — unlike `DrawRect`/`DrawImage`, which `Box`/(eventually) `Image` genuinely emit. A CPU polyline tesselator (thick-line expansion with correct joins, `CurveTo` Bézier flattening) is real, non-trivial geometry work; building it against a command nothing emits yet would be speculative scope-creep, the same category of premature work this project's `CLAUDE.md` explicitly avoids ("don't design for hypothetical future requirements"). | Add `PathVertex`/`BatchKind::Path`/the tesselator together, in one sub-phase, when a real `DrawPath` producer exists (e.g. a future vector-icon or diagram primitive) — not speculatively ahead of that need. Until then, `Path.glsl`'s existence is validated only by `ShaderPipeline_test.cpp` compiling it, not by anything actually feeding it vertices. |
| 2026-07-27 | `RectVertex`/`ImageVertex` reuse `Rendering::CornerRadii` directly for their `Radii` field (same type as `DrawRect::Radii`/`DrawImage::Radii`), rather than flattening to a raw `Widgets::Vec4` | Store `Radii` as `Vec4` to mirror the shader's `vec4 inRadii` attribute 1:1 | `CornerRadii`'s named fields (`TopLeft`/`TopRight`/`BottomRight`/`BottomLeft`) are self-documenting and match `DrawRect`/`DrawImage`'s own field exactly — copying `cmd.Radii` straight into the vertex needs no reordering or field-juggling on the CPU side; `Rect.glsl`'s fragment stage already does its own internal reorder from `CornerRadii`'s field order into the order `RoundedBoxSdf` expects (documented in the shader's own comment). Keeping the CPU-side type named avoids a second place where "which float is which corner" could silently drift. | If a future GPU vertex-buffer upload step needs a flat `float[4]`/`Vec4` layout for a vertex attribute binding, that reinterpretation happens at upload time (a `reinterpret_cast`/`memcpy` of `CornerRadii`, which is a plain 4-float aggregate with no padding) — not by changing `RectVertex`'s own field type. |

The `IMF_ASSERT` (not a silent skip) makes the gap loud the moment something *does* start emitting `DrawImage`, rather than producing a silently-incomplete frame. | Add `BatchKind::Image` support (GL texture creation from `TextureAtlas`'s CPU buffer or a standalone image, a `TextureId -> GLuint` registry, the GL3-equivalent `Image.glsl` shader) in the same sub-phase that gives `Widgets::Image`/`DrawContext::DrawImage` a real `CommandBuffer` producer — not before. |

Verified directly, not assumed: the GL3-native shader was written, compiled, and pixel-tested successfully against this machine's real 3.3-requested context. | This is the literal, intentional meaning of the proposal's own sentence "The OpenGL3 backend uses GLSL with a compatibility profile" (as opposed to the Vulkan backend, which "uses SPIR-V directly") — the two shader sources are two different artifacts by design, not an accidental duplication to be "fixed" later. The `RoundedBoxSdf` logic is kept in sync by hand across three copies now (`Rect.glsl`, `Image.glsl`, and `NativeRendererGL3.cpp`'s embedded string) — if it ever needs to change, all three need updating; a comment in each references the others. |

Querying `GL_VIEWPORT` matches how a caller is already expected to have configured the target framebuffer/viewport before invoking any GL-drawing call in this codebase (see `GLFWOpenGL3Backend::EndFrame()`'s own `glViewport()` call immediately before its own draw-data submission). | The offscreen-FBO test harness (`NativeRendererGL3_test.cpp`'s `ScratchFramebuffer`) must call `glViewport()` itself before invoking `Render()` — documented directly in that test file. Whoever wires `NativeRendererGL3` into the real per-frame render path (a later sub-phase) must do the same, in the correct order relative to any other framebuffer/viewport setup for that frame. |
| 2026-07-27 | `NativeRendererGL3::Render()` saves and restores the GL state it touches (current program, VAO binding, `GL_BLEND` enable state and blend-func factors) around its own drawing | Leave GL state as `Render()` last set it, matching how `ImGui_ImplOpenGL3_RenderDrawData()` itself is documented to require the caller to have set up its own GL state and does its own internal save/restore | The still-open "how does `NativeRenderer` output composite with direct ImGui calls in the same frame" question (Phase 32.1's `DECISIONS.md` interstitial row, still unresolved) means a future frame could plausibly call `NativeRendererGL3::Render()` interleaved with ImGui/other GL drawing — leaving GL state dirty would make an already-open architectural question actively worse (a real source of visual bugs) rather than neutral. This costs a handful of `glGetIntegerv`/`glIsEnabled` calls per `Render()`, an acceptable overhead for the safety it buys. | Not exhaustive — `GL_ARRAY_BUFFER`'s raw binding (as opposed to the VAO's own remembered attribute-pointer state) is left pointing at `NativeRendererGL3`'s own `_vbo` after `Render()` returns, matching the common (and generally accepted) convention that code should rebind `GL_ARRAY_BUFFER` before relying on it rather than assume it's untouched — the same convention ImGui's own OpenGL3 backend follows. |

## Phase 32.5 — Wire `NativeRendererGL3` Into `Reconciler`; the Deferred-Rasterization Discovery

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-07-27 | **Root cause confirmed and fixed**: `GLFWOpenGL3Backend`'s `glViewport()`/`glClearColor()`/`glClear()` calls moved from the top of `EndFrame()` to the top of `BeginFrame()` | Leave the clear in `EndFrame()`, matching pre-Phase-32.4 behavior | Traced the full frame call chain (`Application::Run()`: `BeginFrame()` → `Reconciler::Show()` [→ `_renderer->Render()`] → `EndFrame()`) and confirmed directly: `EndFrame()`'s clear ran *after* `Show()` returns, so any real GL draw a swapped-in `NativeRendererGL3` issued during `Show()` was unconditionally erased by that clear before `ImGui_ImplOpenGL3_RenderDrawData()`/swap ever ran — `NativeRenderer` output could never have reached the screen under the pre-32.5 lifecycle, regardless of any other fix. Moving the clear earlier is behaviourally transparent to ImGui's own rendering (ImGui always fully redraws every frame via `RenderDrawData`; it does not matter *when within the frame* the clear happened, only that it happened before the final composite) — verified directly, not assumed: full regression suite before vs. after showed zero new failures (486/487 and 487/487 across two runs, both only the pre-existing `ThreadPool`/`Config` flakes). | `EndFrame()` still re-queries `glfwGetFramebufferSize()` for its own `ReadPixels()` sizing — a second, cheap, harmless call, not consolidated with `BeginFrame()`'s query into shared member state, to keep the diff minimal and avoid coupling the two methods' internals. |
| 2026-07-27 | **A second, deeper compositing problem was discovered and is *not* fixed this sub-phase**: `RootBridge::BeginRootWindow()`'s `ImGui::Begin("##ImFrameTreeRoot", ...)` call *queues* the root window's own background into ImGui's internal draw list; that queued content is only *rasterized* later, in `EndFrame()`'s `ImGui::Render()` + `ImGui_ImplOpenGL3_RenderDrawData()`. `NativeRendererGL3::Render()`, by contrast, issues its GL draw calls **immediately** when `Reconciler::Show()` calls it. Consequence, reproduced directly (not theorized): a real `Reconciler::Show()` call with `NativeRendererGL3` swapped in draws a `Box` correctly (confirmed via `glReadPixels()` immediately after `Show()` returns), but by the time `backend.EndFrame()` finishes and the buffer is swapped, the root window's own (deferred) background has painted over it — **any deferred (ImGui-drawn) content in the same frame always rasterizes after any immediate (`NativeRenderer`-drawn) content, regardless of program order**, because ImGui defers *all* of its own rasterization to end-of-frame while `NativeRenderer` does not defer at all | Attempt a fix in this sub-phase — e.g. `ImGuiWindowFlags_NoBackground` on the root window | This is real, production-code-level (`RootBridge.cpp`) behavior, not a test artifact — changing the root window's flags would alter visible behavior for the *default* `ImGuiCompatRenderer` path too (removing the window's background for every existing consumer, since `RootBridge` is shared infrastructure), which is out of scope for a sub-phase about wiring a renderer choice, not about redesigning window chrome. A real fix needs `NativeRenderer` to *defer* its own replay to a point in the pipeline that composites correctly with ImGui's own deferred content (e.g. hooking in around `ImGui::Render()`/`RenderDrawData()`, or moving to a full offscreen-render-target compositing model) — genuine, undesigned architecture work. | **This supersedes and sharpens the Phase 32.1 "how does NativeRenderer output composite with ImGui" open question** — it is no longer a vague unknown, it is a concrete, reproduced, root-caused blocker with a known fix *shape* (give `NativeRenderer` a deferred-replay mode) that just isn't built yet. `Tests/Tree/ReconcilerNativeRenderer_test.cpp` reads pixels immediately after `Show()`, *not* via `backend.EndFrame()`/`ReadPixels()`, specifically to test what is actually true (the draw itself is correct) without silently asserting the false, stronger claim (that it survives to the final composited screen) — its own file comment explains why in detail. Whoever attempts real `Application`-level `NativeRenderer` usage next must solve this first, or scope that attempt to windows/regions where ImGui draws nothing else. |
| 2026-07-27 | `Reconciler`'s `ImGuiCompatRenderer _renderer` member is promoted to `std::unique_ptr<IRenderer> _renderer` (default-constructed to an `ImGuiCompatRenderer`), with a new `SetRenderer(std::unique_ptr<IRenderer>)` method | Leave `_renderer` fixed, per Phase 31.3's original deferral | Phase 31.3's `DECISIONS.md` row named this exact promotion point: "when `Reconciler` first needs to actually select between two implementations, not before." `NativeRendererGL3` now exists as a second real implementation — that point has arrived, independent of whether a public `Application`-level API exists yet to reach it (see next row). Zero behavior change for existing callers: `_renderer`'s default-member-initializer constructs the identical `ImGuiCompatRenderer` the old fixed member did. | Verified via the full regression suite (`_renderer->Render(...)` vs. the old `_renderer.Render(...)` — purely a storage/indirection change, no logic difference) — zero new failures. |

## Phase 32.6 — `NativeRenderer` Deferred-Replay Mode (Fixes the Phase 32.5 Compositing Blocker)

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-08-02 | `NativeRendererGL3` gained a constructor-selected `RenderMode` (`Immediate` — unchanged Phase 32.4/32.5 behaviour, default; `DeferredReplay` — new). In `DeferredReplay`, `Render()` snapshots the built batches into a heap-owned `PendingDraw{Self, Batches}` and calls `ImGui::GetWindowDrawList()->AddCallback(&ExecuteDeferredDraw, pending)` followed by `AddCallback(ImDrawCallback_ResetRenderState, nullptr)`, instead of drawing synchronously | (a) Have `Application`/`Reconciler` manually re-invoke `NativeRenderer` after `ImGui_ImplOpenGL3_RenderDrawData()` in `EndFrame()` — i.e. teach the frame-lifecycle layer about `NativeRenderer`'s internal timing; (b) a full offscreen-compositing redesign — render natively to a dedicated FBO, then blit/composite it over ImGui's own output as a final pass | `ImDrawList::AddCallback()` is Dear ImGui's own documented extension point for interleaving custom GPU draws with its draw-list rasterization at the *exact* recorded position in the list — it fixes the Phase 32.5 z-order bug (deferred ImGui content always rasterizing after immediate `NativeRenderer` content, regardless of program order) generically and locally, with zero coupling to `Application`'s frame lifecycle and no new render target. Alternative (a) would leak `NativeRenderer`-specific timing knowledge into `Reconciler`/`Application`; alternative (b) is real, much larger scope (per-layer compositing infra that also serves `PushOpacityLayer`/`PushBlendLayer`, still a documented Phase 31 no-op) and was not warranted just to fix ordering. `PendingDraw` owns a copy (not a reference into `_batchBuilder`) so a same-frame second `Render()` call — none exist today, but nothing prevents one — cannot invalidate a callback still pending. | `DeferredReplay` requires an active ImGui window (`ImGui::GetWindowDrawList()` asserts outside `Begin()`/`End()`), so it is only valid where `Reconciler::Show()` calls it — `Immediate` remains for the offscreen-FBO direct-`Render()` tests (`NativeRendererGL3_test.cpp`) and any future non-ImGui-window consumer. `Tests/Tree/ReconcilerNativeRenderer_test.cpp` now runs the *full* `BeginFrame()`→`Show()`→`EndFrame()` lifecycle (not the Phase 32.5 mid-frame read) and keeps an `Immediate`-mode test proving the old bug still reproduces end-to-end, alongside a `DeferredReplay`-mode test proving the fix. Whoever builds the Phase 32's still-open public renderer-handle API should default it to `DeferredReplay`. |
| 2026-07-27 | **No public `Application::UseNativeRenderer()` API was added this sub-phase** — `Reconciler::SetRenderer()` is `Internal`-only, reached directly by `Tests/Tree/ReconcilerNativeRenderer_test.cpp`, the same way `ImGuiCompatRenderer_test.cpp` already drives `ImGuiCompatRenderer` directly without `Application` | Add `Application& UseNativeRenderer(std::unique_ptr<Internal::IRenderer>)` to the public `Application.hpp`, matching the constructor's existing `std::unique_ptr<Internal::IBackend> backend` parameter pattern | `include/ImFrame/App/Application.hpp` is a **public** header; `.claude/CLAUDE.md`'s own namespace table states `ImFrame::Internal::` is "Private implementation details — never expose in public headers." A `unique_ptr<Internal::IRenderer>` parameter would require every consumer calling this method to name and construct `Internal::` types directly (e.g. `Internal::NativeRendererGL3`), which is exactly what that invariant forbids — this is a stricter constraint than the dependency-direction question alone (which is *also* real: `Application`/`src/App/` cannot construct a concrete `NativeRendererGL3` itself without depending on `Backends/GLFWOpenGL3`, the reverse of this project's established dependency direction). A correct public API needs a new, non-`Internal::` handle/opaque-wrapper type in `include/ImFrame/Rendering/` (Pimpl-style, matching how other public/internal boundaries in this codebase are already bridged) — genuine, undesigned public API surface, not a same-sub-phase addition. | The concrete next step for whoever adds this: design a public `ImFrame::Rendering::Renderer` (or similar) handle type wrapping `Internal::IRenderer`, plus a per-backend public factory (e.g. `ImFrame::Backends::CreateNativeRendererGL3()` in a small public header `Backends/GLFWOpenGL3` already exposes alongside `GLFWOpenGL3Backend.hpp`), then `Application::UseNativeRenderer(Rendering::Renderer)` forwards into `Reconciler::SetRenderer()`. Should also resolve the deferred-rasterization compositing problem (previous row) first or alongside — a public API that silently produces the "disappears after EndFrame()" bug would be worse than no public API. |

## Phase 32.7 — `Application::UseRenderer()`: the Public Renderer-Selection API

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-08-02 | **Correction of the Phase 32.5 row above**: `Application.hpp` already names an `Internal::` type unqualified in a public constructor parameter today — `explicit Application(std::unique_ptr<Internal::IBackend> backend, ...)` — and the file's own top-of-file Doxygen example already shows a consumer writing `std::make_unique<ImFrame::Internal::GLFWOpenGL3Backend>()` directly. `check_api_leaks.sh`'s Check 2 greps for the literal substring `ImFrame::Internal::`; `Internal::IBackend` written inside `namespace ImFrame::App { ... }` resolves to `ImFrame::Internal::IBackend` via normal qualified-name lookup but does not contain that literal substring, so it passes today and always has. The Phase 32.5 row's claim that a `unique_ptr<Internal::IRenderer>` parameter is "exactly what that invariant forbids" was therefore overstated — the project's own established, shipped practice is that a consumer who explicitly links a specific backend/renderer target is expected to name and construct that target's concrete `Internal::` type directly, exactly as `IBackend` already requires of every `Application` user. | Build the `Rendering::RendererHandle`/`Backends::CreateNativeRendererGL3()` Pimpl-and-factory pair the Phase 32.5 row's "Consequence" column actually called for | A Pimpl wrapper type would be a new abstraction solving a constraint that, on direct inspection of `Application.hpp` and `check_api_leaks.sh`, doesn't exist — `IBackend`'s identical shape already ships and passes every check. Building it anyway would be exactly the kind of premature abstraction this project's own conventions warn against, for zero additional safety over the pattern already in production. | This finding should be treated as project-wide: any future `Application::With*/Use*` method that needs to accept a concrete `Internal::` implementation type selected by whichever backend/feature target the consumer linked can follow the identical `unique_ptr<Internal::IXxx>` pattern — no bridging wrapper type is a prerequisite. |
| 2026-08-02 | `Application` gained `Application& UseRenderer(std::unique_ptr<Internal::IRenderer> renderer)` — a public, generic renderer-selection method (not the proposal's literal parameterless `UseNativeRenderer()`) — forwarding directly to the existing `_reconciler->SetRenderer(std::move(renderer))`. `Internal::IRenderer` is forward-declared in `Application.hpp` alongside the existing `Internal::Reconciler` forward-declaration | (a) `Application::UseNativeRenderer()` (proposal's literal name, no parameter) with `Application` auto-selecting a concrete renderer via the active backend's `NativeGraphicsContext` (Phase 19) variant; (b) the `RendererHandle` Pimpl wrapper from the row above | (a) requires a registry/auto-dispatch mechanism so `Application` (which has no dependency on any `Backends/` target) can resolve "the active backend's graphics context" to "the matching concrete `NativeRenderer*` implementation" — real, speculative machinery for hypothetical Vulkan/Metal/DX12/WebGPU `NativeRenderer*` backends that don't exist yet (only `NativeRendererGL3` does), the same "nothing to dispatch between yet" reasoning already used in Phase 32.4's decision not to build a dispatcher class. A generic `unique_ptr<Internal::IRenderer>` parameter needs none of that — it mirrors the constructor's own `unique_ptr<Internal::IBackend>` parameter exactly, is usable by any current or future `IRenderer` implementation without `Application.hpp` naming any of them, and required zero new files. (b) is unneeded per the row above. | A consumer opts in with: `#include "NativeRendererGL3.hpp"` (already public-consumer-includable — same `Backends/GLFWOpenGL3` `PUBLIC` include dir as `GLFWOpenGL3Backend.hpp`) then `app.UseRenderer(std::make_unique<Internal::NativeRendererGL3>(Internal::NativeRendererGL3::RenderMode::DeferredReplay));`. `Tests/App/Application_test.cpp` gained a `SpyRenderer` (`Internal::IRenderer` subclass counting `Render()` calls) test proving `UseRenderer()` reaches `Reconciler::Show()`'s per-frame replay through a real `Application::Run()` loop — verifying the wiring, not `NativeRendererGL3`'s own rendering correctness (already covered by Phase 32.4/32.6's dedicated pixel tests). Phase 32's remaining open item is (c): `BatchKind::Image` GL support, still blocked on `Widgets::Image` having no real `DrawImage` producer. |

## Phase 32.8 — `Widgets::Image` Gets a Real `DrawImage` Producer

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-08-18 | `ImageElement::Paint()` (`src/Widgets/Image.cpp`) splits on whether `OnClick` is set. Set: unchanged — still calls `ImGui::ImageButton()` directly (`PaintInteractive()`). Unset: now pushes a real `Rendering::DrawImage` command (`PaintStatic()`) instead of calling `ImGui::Image()` directly — the first live producer of `DrawImage` anywhere in the tree since Phase 31 built `ImGuiCompatRenderer`'s translation for it speculatively | Migrate the interactive path too, e.g. by having `Reconciler`/a `GestureRegion`-style helper own hit-testing so both paths could emit `DrawImage` | `ImGui::ImageButton()` handles hit-testing and drawing as one opaque call, exactly the `Button`/`Checkbox`/etc. carve-out Phase 31's `DECISIONS.md` already documented ("interaction with no generic command equivalent") — migrating it would mean building new hit-testing infrastructure with no other current consumer, out of scope for "give `Widgets::Image` a producer." The non-interactive path is pure drawing with zero such dependency, so it migrates cleanly using infrastructure (`ImGuiCompatRenderer`'s `DrawImage` translation) that already existed and had simply never been exercised. | `BatchBuilder`/`NativeRendererGL3` still `IMF_ASSERT` on `BatchKind::Image` — this row only supplies the producer; real GL texture upload/registry support for `NativeRendererGL3` remains separately deferred (see next row). |
| 2026-08-18 | `TextureId`'s value for the migrated path is `static_cast<std::uint64_t>(reinterpret_cast<uintptr_t>(texture))` — the exact same reinterpret-through-`void*` the old `ImGui::Image()` call already used, now wrapped in `TextureId` instead of `ImTextureRef` directly | Introduce a real texture-registry/loader indirection (`TextureId` as an opaque index rather than a disguised pointer) ahead of any actual GPU-texture-management subsystem existing | `TextureId`'s own doc comment already documents this exact contract: "each renderer gives this value its own meaning: `ImGuiCompatRenderer` treats it as an `ImTextureID`." No `ImageLoader` or GPU texture-registry subsystem exists yet (`TextureAtlas` from Phase 32.1 is the only real `TextureId` issuer today, and it has no wiring to `Widgets::Image` or to any GPU upload path) — building one now would be speculative infrastructure with no consumer. | A future texture-management phase that introduces a real `ImageLoader`/registry must decide whether `Widgets::Image`'s raw `TextureHandle` continues to bypass it (current behaviour, unchanged by this row) or gets migrated to obtain a registry-issued `TextureId` instead — an open question for that phase, not resolved here. |
| 2026-08-18 | Two Phase 30 behaviours needed explicit, manual preservation when bypassing `ImGui::Image()`: (1) tooltip hover-detection now uses an invisible `ImGui::Dummy()` at the image's rect (only when a tooltip is actually configured) since `DrawImage` registers no ImGui item for `IsItemHovered()` to test; (2) disabled-state dimming is baked into the pushed command's `TintColor.w` by reading `ImGui::GetStyle().Alpha` inside a paired `BeginDisabled()`/`EndDisabled()`, since `ImGui::Image()` multiplies its tint by that ambient alpha and `DrawImage` has no ambient-alpha concept of its own | Accept the tooltip/disabled-dimming regressions as an acceptable simplification, matching how `DrawPath`'s `FillRule` and `PushOpacityLayer`'s actual compositing are accepted-but-unapplied elsewhere in this same `CommandBuffer` architecture | Both behaviours are pre-existing, currently-shipped, user-visible widget features (not speculative) — silently dropping them on a routine internal-implementation migration would be a real regression, unlike the other accepted `CommandBuffer` simplifications (which are all cases where nothing ever depended on the missing behaviour because no producer existed). Tooltip and disabled-dimming both had a real producer (this exact widget) before this migration. | `BorderColor` (the `ImGui::Image()` `border_col` parameter) was **not** preserved pixel-for-pixel — it now draws as a separate stroke-only `DrawRect` at the image's own bounds rather than replicating `ImGui::Image()`'s additional `ImGuiStyle::ImageBorderSize` outward padding. Untested by any existing test and not exercised by any known caller; documented in `Image.cpp`'s own file comment as an intentional, minor fidelity gap rather than a discovered bug. |

## Phase 32.9 — `NativeRendererGL3` Real GL Texture Support for `BatchKind::Image`

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-08-19 | `NativeRendererGL3` treats `Rendering::TextureId::Value()` as a raw GL texture name (`GLuint`), not an index into any texture-atlas/registry indirection — `RenderImageBatch()` does `glBindTexture(GL_TEXTURE_2D, static_cast<GLuint>(batch.Texture.Value()))` directly | Build a real texture registry (`TextureId` → `GLuint` map, populated by a loader/atlas subsystem) and have `NativeRendererGL3` look up through it, matching the "index into its own texture registry" framing `TextureId`'s Phase 31 doc comment had speculated for "a future native renderer" | The only live `DrawImage` producer today (`Widgets::Image`, Phase 32.8) already constructs its `TextureId` as a reinterpreted raw GL texture name (`reinterpret_cast<uintptr_t>(TextureHandle)`) — the same bit pattern `ImGuiCompatRenderer`/ImGui's own OpenGL3 backend already interpret as a raw texture name. A registry-index interpretation would require `Widgets::Image` to route through a loader that doesn't exist, or `NativeRendererGL3` to guess which interpretation applies per-`TextureId` — neither is warranted when there is exactly one real producer and it already emits raw handles. Verified directly: `TextureAtlas` (Phase 32.1, the only thing that *would* need registry semantics) has no live producer/consumer wiring anywhere in the tree — confirmed via search, not assumed. | `TextureId`'s public doc comment (`include/ImFrame/Rendering/CommandBuffer.hpp`) was corrected to state the raw-handle interpretation as the current, real contract, and to stop claiming "never construct one directly — obtain it from `ImageLoader::Load()` or `Viewport::TextureId()`" (neither exists; `Widgets::Image` already constructs one directly, and did so as of Phase 32.8). If a real texture-management subsystem is built later and needs registry semantics, it is a breaking reinterpretation of `TextureId`'s contract, not an additive one — that phase must address `Widgets::Image`'s existing raw-handle usage explicitly, not silently coexist with it. |
| 2026-08-19 | Each of `RenderRectBatch()`/`RenderImageBatch()` now binds its own program/VAO/viewport uniform at the start of the call, rather than `DrawBatches()` binding the Rect program/VAO once before iterating all batches (Phase 32.4's original shape, valid only when `BatchKind::Rect` was the sole kind) | Track "current bound kind" in `DrawBatches()` and only rebind on a kind change within the batch list | `BatchBuilder` already flushes on `BatchFlushReason::CommandTypeChange`, so a single `CommandBuffer` can legitimately interleave Rect and Image batches (e.g. a themed panel background followed by an icon) — `DrawBatches()` can no longer assume one kind for the whole list. Per-batch self-binding is simpler and unconditionally correct regardless of interleaving order or count; the extra `glUseProgram`/`glBindVertexArray` calls per batch are the same order of cost as the `glBufferData` upload each batch already does per frame, not a new bottleneck class. | `DrawBatches()`'s GL state save/restore gained `GL_ACTIVE_TEXTURE`/`GL_TEXTURE_BINDING_2D` (texture unit 0) alongside the existing program/VAO/blend save-restore, since `RenderImageBatch()` now binds a texture that must not leak into whatever ImGui or another caller draws next in the same frame. |
| 2026-08-19 | The `BatchKind::Image` `IMF_ASSERT(false)` in `DrawBatches()`'s dispatch loop was removed entirely (not converted to a softer fallback) — the `if (Rect) {...} else {...}` became `if (Rect) { RenderRectBatch } else { RenderImageBatch }`, since `BatchKind` has exactly two enumerators and both are now real | Keep a defensive `IMF_ASSERT`/fallback path for a hypothetical future third `BatchKind` | `BatchKind`'s only two enumerators are `Rect` and `Image` (no `Path` — `DrawPath` still has no live producer and is not batched at all, per Phase 32.3). Guarding against a `BatchKind` value that cannot exist today would be exactly the kind of defensive code this project's own conventions avoid ("don't add validation for scenarios that can't happen"). | If a third `BatchKind` is ever added (e.g. for `DrawPath` once it gets a live producer and CPU tesselation), this `if`/`else` must become an explicit `switch` or gain a new branch at that point — not before. |

## Phase 33.1 — MSDF Text Pipeline: New Dependencies (`harfbuzz`, `msdfgen`)

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-08-19 | Added a new opt-in vcpkg manifest feature `"text-msdf"` (`harfbuzz` + `msdfgen`), gated by a new `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF` CMake option read before `project()` — mirroring `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER`/`"native-renderer"` exactly (same file, same position, same doc-comment shape) | Add `harfbuzz`/`msdfgen` as always-on top-level `vcpkg.json` dependencies, matching how `freetype`/`glfw3`/`glad` are unconditional today | Phase 33's text pipeline is a new, unproven subsystem with no consumer yet (identical position to Phase 32.2's `glslang`/`native-renderer` when it was added) — a default `cmake -B build` should not silently grow two new from-source toolchain dependencies for a feature nothing calls yet. `harfbuzz`+`msdfgen` together took **26 minutes** to resolve and build from source via vcpkg on this machine (verified directly, not estimated) — real, non-trivial build-time cost that must stay opt-in. | Verified: `cmake -B build-text -DIMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF=ON` succeeds end-to-end. CMake targets available: `harfbuzz::harfbuzz` (+ `harfbuzz::harfbuzz-subset`), `msdfgen::msdfgen-core`, `msdfgen::msdfgen-ext` (FreeType-integrated glyph loading — the one `FontFace`/`GlyphAtlas` will need), `msdfgen::msdfgen-full`. vcpkg pulled `tinyxml2` in transitively as part of msdfgen's default `[core,extensions,geometry-preprocessing]` feature set — not explicitly requested, not a concern (small, header-light dependency, standard for msdfgen's SVG-adjacent extension code). `build-text/` (a scratch build directory, not part of the repo) is kept around across this session's remaining Phase 33 sub-phases specifically to avoid repaying the 26-minute dependency build on every configure — unlike Phase 32's disposable `build-native/` pattern, deleted immediately after each verification pass. |

## Phase 33.2 — `FontFace` / `FontRegistry`: FreeType + HarfBuzz Loading

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-08-20 | `FontFace.hpp`/`FontRegistry.hpp` (`src/Rendering/Text/`) include the real FreeType (`<ft2build.h>`+`FT_FREETYPE_H`) and HarfBuzz (`<hb.h>`/`<hb-ft.h>`) headers directly, storing real `FT_Face`/`hb_font_t*` members — not opaque `void*` handles hidden behind a cast, the way `NativeRendererGL3.hpp` stores GL objects as plain `unsigned int` | Hide FreeType/HarfBuzz types behind opaque handles in the header, matching the "never expose ImGui in public headers" pattern's spirit | There is no "never expose FreeType/HarfBuzz" architecture invariant anywhere in `.claude/CLAUDE.md` — that rule is specific to ImGui, for a documented reason (consumers must not need to know ImGui exists). `FontFace.hpp`/`FontRegistry.hpp` live under `src/Rendering/Text/`, never reachable from `include/ImFrame/`, so `check_api_leaks.sh`'s scope (`include/ImFrame/` only) never sees them regardless. Every real consumer of `FontFace` (`FontRegistry` today; `TextShaper`/`GlyphAtlas` in later sub-phases) needs the actual `FT_Face`/`hb_font_t*` types to call shaping/rasterization functions directly — hiding them behind `void*` would add casting busywork with zero benefit, unlike `NativeRendererGL3`'s GL handles, which are genuinely-opaque integer names with no type-safety loss from being stored as plain `unsigned int`. | Any later public-facing font API (a `Rendering::Font` handle type, if `Application::WithFont()` ever needs one) must still keep FreeType/HarfBuzz types out of `include/ImFrame/` — this row only concludes that the *internal* `src/` headers don't need to hide them from each other. |
| 2026-08-20 | `FontFace` owns exactly one `FT_Face` reference and binds it to HarfBuzz via the plain `hb_ft_font_create()` (non-`_referenced`) variant — HarfBuzz takes no `FT_Face` reference of its own; `FontFace`'s own destructor is the sole releaser (`hb_font_destroy()` first, then `FT_Done_Face()`) | `hb_ft_font_create_referenced()`, which internally calls `FT_Reference_Face()` so HarfBuzz also holds (and later releases) its own reference | Both are correct if used consistently, but the non-`_referenced` variant has one fewer moving part: no reliance on `FT_Reference_Face()`'s exact refcounting behaviour (available since FreeType 2.10, but an extra assumption this code doesn't need to make) since `FontFace` already fully owns and co-destroys both objects together, in a fixed order, inside one class. | If `FontFace` is ever changed to let its `FT_Face`/`hb_font_t*` be shared or outlive each other independently (no current plan to), the `_referenced` variant would become necessary — noted here so that change doesn't silently reintroduce a use-after-free. |
| 2026-08-20 | `FontRegistry::~FontRegistry()` explicitly calls `_faces.clear()` *before* `FT_Done_FreeType(_library)`, rather than relying on C++'s automatic reverse-declaration-order member destruction | Declare `_faces` before `_library` and let implicit member-destruction order (reverse of declaration) handle it | A class's own user-provided destructor **body** always runs before any of its members' destructors — so a naïve `~FontRegistry() { FT_Done_FreeType(_library); }` would free the library *before* `_faces`'s `FontFace` entries (each holding an `FT_Face` into that library) get destroyed afterward, calling `FT_Done_Face()` on an already-destroyed library. Explicit ordering in the body sidesteps this entirely, independent of member declaration order. | Any future member added to `FontRegistry` that also depends on `_library` must be cleared/reset in the same explicit destructor body, before `FT_Done_FreeType()` — declaration order alone does not guarantee correctness here. |
| 2026-08-20 | `FT_Init_FreeType()` is deferred to the first `FontRegistry::Load()` call (lazy), not done in the constructor | Initialise `_library` unconditionally in the constructor, fail-fast if `FT_Init_FreeType()` errors | Matches this codebase's established "constructing an instance never requires a context to already exist" convention (`NativeRendererGL3::EnsureInitialized()`'s identical reasoning, Phase 32.4) — a default-constructed `FontRegistry` that is never actually used to load a font pays no FreeType initialisation cost and cannot fail in its constructor (constructors that can fail are awkward with this project's `std::expected`-only error-handling convention, since constructors cannot return `Result<T>`). | `Load()`'s error surface now includes "library failed to initialise" folded into the same `Error::FontLoadFailed` as "file failed to parse" — not distinguished, since neither is actionable differently by a caller and adding a second enumerator for an effectively-untestable failure mode (out-of-memory during `FT_Init_FreeType()`) isn't warranted. |
| 2026-08-20 | `Tests/Rendering/FontRegistry_test.cpp`'s `catch_discover_tests()` call passes an explicit `WORKING_DIRECTORY "$<TARGET_FILE_DIR:...>"` — a discovered, non-obvious fix, not present in the precedent it copied from | Trust `catch_discover_tests()`'s own default working directory, matching `ImFrame_Tests_Icons_IconFont`'s existing (uncorrected) registration | Verified directly: without this, ctest's default CWD for the discovered test does not match the `add_custom_command(POST_BUILD ...)` copy step's target directory, so the relative `"Assets/Fonts/fa-solid-900.ttf"` path in the test never resolves under `ctest` even though the file *is* physically present next to the built executable — 3 of 6 new tests failed under `ctest` despite the same executable passing 6/6 when run directly (misleadingly, only because the direct run happened to have the repo root as CWD, where the same relative path also happens to resolve). `ImFrame_Tests_Icons_IconFont`'s own two runtime tests were found to have the identical unfixed problem — confirmed via repeated `ctest` runs this session, they report `Skipped` every time, never `Passed`. Not fixed in this row (out of scope for this sub-phase's own new files) — flagged as a separate background task (`task_122ea9a9`) instead. | Any future test target that copies a real asset next to its binary via a `POST_BUILD` custom command and resolves it via a relative path at runtime must also pass a matching `WORKING_DIRECTORY` to `catch_discover_tests()` — the copy step alone is not sufficient, as `IconFont_test.cpp`'s tests demonstrate by having silently skipped since Phase 9. |

## Phase 33.3 — `TextShaper`: HarfBuzz Shaping With an LRU Cache

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-08-20 | `TextShaper::Shape()` returns `const ShapedText*` (nullable, non-owning into the internal cache) rather than `Result<ShapedText>`/`std::expected` | Return `ImFrame::Result<ShapedText>` (`std::expected<ShapedText, Error>`), matching the project's general "fallible operations return `std::expected`" convention | `std::expected<T, E>` requires `T` to be a non-reference object type — returning by value on every call (including cache *hits*) would mean copying the full `ShapedText` (a `std::vector<GlyphRun>`) every single call, defeating the entire purpose of the cache the proposal explicitly asks for. Returning a pointer matches `FontRegistry::Get()`'s own already-established "nullptr means invalid id" convention one layer down, and the only failure mode (`font` not present in `registry`) is a caller precondition violation (an id from a prior successful `Load()` was expected), not a genuinely-fallible runtime I/O operation like a file read. | Callers must not hold a returned `ShapedText*` past the next `Shape()` call on the same `TextShaper` instance — a later call can evict the entry the pointer refers to. Documented directly in the method's own doc comment. |
| 2026-08-20 | `Shape()`'s HarfBuzz usage calls `hb_buffer_guess_segment_properties()` (auto-detects script/language/direction from the buffer's own Unicode content) and treats the *entire* input string as one shaping run | Implement full paragraph-level Unicode bidi segmentation (splitting mixed-direction text into per-direction runs before shaping each one separately) | `hb_buffer_guess_segment_properties()` is HarfBuzz's own documented, idiomatic single-run usage pattern and is sufficient to get ligatures, kerning, and correct-direction advances (RTL scripts shape with negative x-advances) "for free" — exactly what the proposal's one-line summary of "bidi reordering" describes. A full Unicode bidi algorithm implementation (splitting one logical string spanning multiple directions into ordered visual runs) is real, substantial, separate scope — a different subsystem, not implied by "TextShaper uses HarfBuzz to shape a UTF-8 string...into ShapedText." | Mixed-direction strings (e.g. Latin text embedding an Arabic phrase) will shape as one run in whatever single direction `hb_buffer_guess_segment_properties()` infers for the buffer as a whole — visually incorrect for genuinely mixed-direction content. A future phase needing correct mixed-bidi rendering must add a real segmentation step before calling `Shape()` once per detected run; not attempted here. |
| 2026-08-20 | The LRU cache is a hand-written `std::list<CacheKey>` (recency order) + `std::unordered_map<CacheKey, std::pair<ShapedText, list::iterator>, CacheKeyHash>` — the classic two-structure LRU pattern, storing the key twice (once in the list node, once as the map key) | A single structure with shared key ownership (e.g. `std::unordered_map<CacheKey, shared_ptr<Entry>>` + an intrusive list) to avoid the key duplication | The proposal's own wording ("`std::unordered_map<ShapingCacheKey, ShapedText>` with an LRU eviction policy") describes exactly this shape. Duplicating a short UI string per cache entry (capped at 256 entries by default) is negligible overhead; eliminating it would add real complexity (intrusive-list bookkeeping or shared ownership) for a problem that doesn't exist yet at this scale. | If profiling ever shows the string duplication is measurably significant (large cache capacities, very long cached strings), revisit with a shared-key design — not needed today. |
| 2026-08-20 | `Tests/Rendering/TextShaper_test.cpp`'s ligature and kerning tests reference `C:/Windows/Fonts/calibri.ttf` by absolute path (confirmed present on this development machine), `SKIP()`-ing gracefully if absent — no ligature-capable font was added to `Assets/Fonts/` | Vendor a real open-license text font with known ligature/kerning tables into `Assets/Fonts/`, alongside the existing FA6 icon fonts | The only vendored font (FA6 solid icons) is a symbol font with no ligature/kerning tables at all — meaningless for testing either feature. This project does not fetch or vendor unreviewed third-party source (established policy, see Phase 32.1's `rectpack2D` row) — adding a new font file requires the user's explicit involvement, not a unilateral fetch. A system font referenced by absolute path, with a graceful skip matching `IconFont_test.cpp`'s own established pattern for its vendored-asset dependency, verifies real HarfBuzz behaviour (confirmed: "fi" merges to 1 glyph vs. "fx" staying 2; "AV"'s combined advance measurably differs from "A"+"V" summed independently) without a portability or provenance cost. | These two specific tests will silently skip (not fail) on any machine/CI without Calibri installed (e.g. non-Windows runners) — `TextShaper`'s other 5 tests (cache identity, LRU eviction, invalid-id handling, empty text, basic non-ligating shaping) use the vendored FA6 font and always run everywhere. If a real cross-platform ligature-capable font is added to `Assets/Fonts/` in a future phase (with the user's explicit sign-off), these two tests should be migrated to use it instead. |

## Phase 33.4 — `GlyphAtlas`: MSDF Bitmap Generation via msdfgen

| Date | Decision | Alternative considered | Rationale | Consequence |
|------|----------|----------------------|-----------|-------------|
| 2026-08-21 | `GlyphAtlas::GetOrCreate()` adopts the exact `FT_Face` `FontRegistry` already owns via msdfgen's `adoptFreetypeFont(FT_Face)`, rather than opening the font file a second time through msdfgen's own `initializeFreetype()`/`loadFont()` path | Give `GlyphAtlas` its own independent `FreetypeHandle*` (msdfgen's own FreeType library instance) and re-`loadFont()` each font file separately from `FontRegistry`'s copy | `adoptFreetypeFont()` exists specifically for this case (msdfgen's own doc comment: wraps a caller-loaded `FT_Face` without affecting its lifetime) — reusing the real face `FontRegistry::Load()` already parsed avoids a second file read, a second FreeType face parse, and a second live `FT_Face` for the same font sitting in memory. Verified directly by reading the installed `msdfgen-ext.h` header (not assumed from memory) that this exact adoption path exists and is gated correctly behind `#ifdef FT_LOAD_DEFAULT` (satisfied since `FontFace.hpp`'s FreeType includes are transitively visible before `msdfgen-ext.h` is included). | `msdfgen::FontHandle*` is adopted and `destroyFont()`-ed around each single `GetOrCreate()` cache-miss call (not cached per `FontId` across calls) — a small repeated allocation, deliberately not optimized away since a cache miss (the only time this happens) already pays for the far more expensive edge-coloring + distance-field-generation work; the adopt/destroy pair is a rounding error by comparison. |
