| 2026-08-21 | Glyph outlines are loaded EM-normalized (`msdfgen::FONT_SCALING_EM_NORMALIZED`) and rasterized at a fixed `kPixelsPerEm = 32.0` density, independent of whatever pixel size a given `FontRegistry::Load()` call happened to request | Rasterize at whatever pixel size the `FontFace` was loaded at (matching `FT_Set_Pixel_Sizes()`'s own value), regenerating the MSDF bitmap whenever a different display size is needed | This is the entire point of MSDF (explicitly named in `PHASE_33_PROPOSAL.md`'s own Foundation section): one bitmap, generated once per glyph, reconstructs sharp edges at *any* final on-screen size via the shader's median-of-three + `fwidth()`-based anti-aliasing (a later sub-phase). Tying raster density to a `FontFace`'s loaded size would defeat this and require regenerating/re-uploading bitmaps per distinct on-screen size, exactly what MSDF is meant to avoid. | `TextShaper`'s advances (tied to whatever pixel size `FontRegistry::Load()` used) and `GlyphAtlas`'s `SizeEm`/`BearingEm` (always in EM units, independent of any loaded size) are deliberately on different scales — a future renderer (Phase 33.5/33.6) must multiply `GlyphAtlasEntry`'s EM-unit fields by the *desired on-screen* font size (in pixels) when computing final quad geometry, not by whatever size `FontRegistry::Load()` happened to use. |
| 2026-08-21 | Generates MTSDF (`generateMTSDF()`, 4-channel: RGB multi-channel distance + true single-channel distance in alpha) rather than plain 3-channel MSDF | `generateMSDF()` (3-channel), storing a constant `255` or duplicated data in the unused 4th (alpha) channel | `TextureAtlas::Upload()` already requires tightly-packed RGBA8 (4 channels) — MTSDF fills all four channels with real, useful data (the alpha channel's true SDF is used by some shader techniques for extra correction/effects) at zero additional storage cost over a 3-channel MSDF padded to RGBA8 anyway. | The eventual MSDF shader (a later sub-phase) may use the alpha channel's true-distance value for effects (outlines, glow) beyond the base median-of-three reconstruction — `Shaders/MSDFText.glsl`'s own design should account for this being MTSDF, not plain MSDF, data. |
| 2026-08-21 | The output `msdfgen::Bitmap<float,4>` is constructed with explicit `Y_DOWNWARD` orientation (msdfgen's own default is `Y_UPWARD`) so `generateMTSDF()` writes row 0 as the visual top directly — no manual post-generation row flip | Generate with the default `Y_UPWARD` orientation and manually reverse row order when copying into the RGBA8 buffer for `TextureAtlas::Upload()` (matching how e.g. `GLFWOpenGL3Backend` flips `glReadPixels()`'s bottom-left-origin output) | `BitmapRef`/`BitmapSection`'s `yOrientation` field exists specifically so a generation function knows which row convention its caller wants — passing the correct one directly is simpler and avoids hand-written flip-loop code entirely. Verified empirically, not just from documentation: a real MSDF bitmap generated this way, read back via `TextureAtlas::ReadRegion()` and reconstructed via median-of-three, shows the glyph's own visual center as fully "inside" (255) and the bitmap's corner as fully "outside" (0) for a real icon (`Icons::Fa::House`) — the geometrically correct result, confirming the orientation choice is right, not just assumed. | If a future msdfgen version or build changes `generateMTSDF()`'s handling of non-default `yOrientation`, this row-order assumption needs re-verification via the same kind of real pixel readback, not just re-reading documentation. |
| 2026-08-21 | Both `CMakeLists.txt`'s `ImFrame` target and every `Tests/CMakeLists.txt` target that transitively references msdfgen symbols link `msdfgen::msdfgen-ext` **and** `msdfgen::msdfgen-core` explicitly (not `msdfgen-ext` alone) | Link only `msdfgen::msdfgen-ext`, assuming it pulls in `msdfgen-core`'s symbols transitively as a usage requirement | Discovered directly via a real link failure, not assumed: linking only `msdfgen::msdfgen-ext` left `Shape`/`Projection`/`DistanceMapping`/`edgeColoringSimple`/`generateMTSDF`/etc. — all defined in `msdfgen-core`, not `msdfgen-ext` — as unresolved externals. vcpkg's own `find_package(msdfgen)` usage hint lists all four targets (`msdfgen`, `msdfgen-ext`, `msdfgen-core`, `msdfgen-full`) together, which in hindsight was the correct signal to follow from the start. | A second, related link issue was hit and fixed the same way `glad::glad` already is for `NativeRendererGL3`'s own tests (established precedent, not a new pattern): `ImFrame`'s own `PRIVATE` link to `msdfgen-ext`/`msdfgen-core` does not propagate through the static `ImFrame.lib` to a test executable's final link step — every test target touching these symbols (even only transitively, via `GlyphAtlas.hpp` → `FontRegistry.hpp` → `FontFace.hpp`'s FreeType/HarfBuzz includes) must re-link the same libraries directly. |
| 2026-08-21 | `Tests/Rendering/GlyphAtlas_test.cpp` sources glyph ids from real `TextShaper::Shape()` calls on documented `Icons::Fa::House`/`Fa::Star` constants, not hand-typed Private-Use-Area UTF-8 bytes | Construct raw UTF-8 byte sequences for arbitrary codepoints within FA6's declared `FA_RANGE_MIN`..`FA_RANGE_MAX` block (e.g. `"\xef\x80\x80"` for U+F000), as an initial draft of this test file did | Discovered directly, not assumed: the declared PUA range is far larger than the ~1400 codepoints FA6 actually assigns real icons to — `U+F000` (and adjacent codepoints this test file originally used) fall back to `.notdef` (glyph 0) via HarfBuzz's cmap lookup, which in `fa-solid-900.ttf` renders as a real, visible box outline. This produced two confusing failures (a "blank" assertion failing, and a "center is inside" assertion failing because a hollow box's center is background, not ink) that had nothing to do with `GlyphAtlas` itself — both vanished immediately once real, checked-in `Icons::Fa::*` constants (guaranteed to resolve to actual FA6 artwork) were substituted, and the corrected "center is inside" test then showed a clean, textbook `centerMedian=255, cornerMedian=0` signal. | Any future test needing "a real FA6 icon's codepoint" should use a named `Icons::Fa::*` constant, never a hand-typed PUA byte sequence — the declared range is not the same thing as the assigned range. |
| 2026-08-21 | The blank-glyph test (`GlyphAtlas::GetOrCreate` marking a space character's glyph as `IsBlank`) uses `C:/Windows/Fonts/calibri.ttf` (`SKIP()` if absent, identical pattern to `TextShaper_test.cpp`'s ligature/kerning tests), not the vendored FA6 font | Test blankness using FA6's own space-codepoint fallback | Confirmed directly (see the row above): FA6's `.notdef` — what `" "` actually resolves to in this icon-only font, since it has no real cmap entry for U+0020 — is a visible box outline, not blank. There is no known codepoint in the vendored FA6 font guaranteed to produce `shape.contours.empty()`. A genuine text font's real (guaranteed-blank) space glyph is the only reliable way to exercise this code path at all. | This test additionally requires Calibri (beyond `TextShaper_test.cpp`'s existing two Calibri-dependent tests) and will skip on machines/CI without it — `GlyphAtlas`'s other 5 tests use the vendored FA6 font and always run everywhere. |

## Phase 33.5 — `MSDFText.glsl` Shader

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-08-24 | This sub-phase authors `Shaders/MSDFText.glsl` only — no `BatchKind::Text`/`TextVertex`, no `NativeRendererGL3` GL3 port, no `DrawText` command processing | Follow the Resumption Snapshot's own stated plan of a "Phase 32.2/32.4 two-part pattern" (build-time `#version 450` source plus an immediate hand-written `#version 330 core` GL3 port, done together in one sub-phase) | Re-examined against the actual precedent once the shader's real shape was known: Phase 32.2 (`Rect.glsl`/`Image.glsl`) authored *shaders* only and deferred the GL3 port + batch wiring to Phase 32.4/32.9, each a separate sub-phase — and `Path.glsl` (also Phase 32.2) still has **no** GL3 port or batch kind to this day, validated purely by `ShaderPipeline_test.cpp` compiling it, specifically because nothing feeds it vertices yet. `MSDFText.glsl` is in the exact same position: no `BatchKind::Text` exists, `BatchBuilder` has no text vertex format, and `NativeRendererGL3` has no glyph-quad renderer — writing a GL3 port now would be an untested, unexercised code path speculating ahead of a real consumer, the kind of premature work this project's `CLAUDE.md` explicitly avoids. | The proposal's "DrawText Command Processing" section (batch kind, vertex format, `NativeRendererGL3::RenderTextBatch()`, GL3 shader port) is real, separate scope for a later sub-phase, done together with `BatchBuilder`/`GlyphAtlas` integration, matching how Phase 32.9 finally gave `BatchKind::Image` its GL texture support only once `Widgets::Image` existed as a real producer. |
| 2026-08-24 | The fragment stage recovers `screenPxRange` from `fwidth(vUv)` and `textureSize(uAtlas, 0)` at draw time (Chlumsky's reference MSDF technique), rather than passing a precomputed screen-pixel-range value from C++ per draw | Compute the screen-to-atlas pixel ratio on the CPU (from the glyph's `SizeEm`/on-screen quad size) and upload it as a per-vertex or per-draw uniform | Matches the proposal's own specified technique verbatim ("anti-aliased with smoothstep using a width derived from the screen-space derivative of the UV coordinates (fwidth)") and is the standard, widely-verified msdfgen reference-shader approach — it needs only `uMsdf.PxRange` (the fixed generation-time constant, `GlyphAtlas::kPxRange`) and the atlas texture's own size, both invariant across every glyph/font, so no renderer-side geometry math has to be duplicated or kept in sync with `GlyphAtlas`. | `NativeRendererGL3`'s eventual GL3 port (see row above) needs only a `uPxRange` uniform equal to `GlyphAtlas::kPxRange` (4.0) — no per-glyph or per-draw screen-size computation to wire up. |
| 2026-08-24 | Glyph color is a per-vertex attribute (`inTextColor`, `layout(location = 2)`), not a shader uniform | Follow the proposal's literal wording, "The shader accepts a color uniform for the glyph color", with one `uColor` uniform and a batch-per-color-group draw split | Matches `Rect.glsl`'s `inFillColor`/`inStrokeColor` and `Image.glsl`'s `inTintColor` — both already per-vertex, not uniforms, specifically so a single batch/draw call can mix differently-styled quads (per Phase 32.9's `DECISIONS.md` row on why `BatchBuilder` flushes only on real state changes, not per-color). A per-vertex attribute lets differently-colored glyphs — e.g. a syntax-highlighted line, or inline colored icons next to text — batch together in the one draw call `GlyphAtlas`'s single shared `TextureAtlas` already makes possible, with no extra draw-call-per-color-group machinery needed later. | Documented here as an intentional deviation from the proposal's literal wording, matching this project's established practice of recording such deviations (e.g. Phase 32.4's `NativeRendererGL3` location, Phase 32.9's `UseRenderer()` naming) rather than treating the proposal text as unconditionally binding. |
| 2026-08-24 | `MSDFText.glsl` is compiled by the same `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER`-gated `foreach(IMF_SHADER_NAME Rect Image Path MSDFText)` loop in `CMakeLists.txt`, independent of `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF` | Gate it behind `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF` instead (or in addition), since it is conceptually part of Phase 33's text pipeline | The shader file itself has zero dependency on `harfbuzz`/`msdfgen`/`FontRegistry`/`GlyphAtlas` — it is pure GLSL that only needs `glslangValidator` (from the `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER`-gated `"native-renderer"` feature) to compile, exactly like `Rect.glsl`/`Image.glsl`/`Path.glsl`. Requiring the separate, much heavier `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF` (harfbuzz+msdfgen, ~26 minutes from source per Phase 33.1) just to compile-check a GLSL string would be an unrelated, unnecessary coupling. | Verified directly: configured a fresh `build-native/` with `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=ON` only (`IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF` left `OFF`) — both shader stages compiled and embedded successfully, and `ShaderPipeline_test.cpp`'s two smoke tests (now covering `MSDFText` alongside `Rect`/`Image`/`Path`) passed. Full suite in that config: 443/443 (excluding the pre-existing, unrelated `ThreadPool stress` hang). Default `build/` (`IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=OFF`) rebuilt successfully, unaffected. `check_api_leaks.sh` clean (no `src/`/`include/` files touched this sub-phase). |

## Phase 33.6 — `BatchKind::Text`/`TextVertex` in `BatchBuilder`

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-08-24 | The proposal's single "DrawText Command Processing" section is split into (at least) two sub-phases: 33.6 adds `BatchKind::Text`/`TextVertex` to `BatchBuilder` only; a later sub-phase gives `NativeRendererGL3` the real GL3 rendering (GPU-mirrored glyph-atlas texture, hand-written `MSDFText` GL3 shader port, `RenderTextBatch()`) | Implement the whole "DrawText Command Processing" section — `BatchBuilder` changes, `NativeRendererGL3` GL3 rendering, and a `LoadFont()` entry point — in one sub-phase | Directly mirrors this project's own established precedent: Phase 32's equivalent Rect work was itself three separate sub-phases (32.2 shader, 32.3 `BatchBuilder` batch/vertex types, 32.4 `NativeRendererGL3` GL3 rendering), not one. `BatchBuilder`'s Text-batching change is self-contained, pure-CPU, and fully unit-testable today; `NativeRendererGL3`'s GL3 text rendering is a separate, much larger unit of work (a real GPU-mirrored atlas texture with dirty tracking, a 330-core shader port, and a `LoadFont()`-style public entry point) that also forces a real decision about whether `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER` must now imply `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF` — worth its own sub-phase and its own `DECISIONS.md` entry rather than bundling both concerns together. | `Shaders/MSDFText.glsl` (Phase 33.5) still has no live GL3 consumer, same as `Path.glsl` — this is expected and matches precedent, not a gap. |
| 2026-08-24 | `BatchBuilder` gains a new abstract seam, `ITextLayoutProvider` (`LayoutText(const DrawText&, vector<GlyphQuad>&) -> TextureId`), rather than including `FontRegistry`/`TextShaper`/`GlyphAtlas` directly | Have `BatchBuilder` (or `BatchBuilder::Build()`) take concrete `FontRegistry&`/`TextShaper&`/`GlyphAtlas&` references and shape/atlas-lookup `DrawText` itself | `BatchBuilder` lives in `src/Rendering/Renderers/`, compiled unconditionally (no `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER`/`IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF` gate at all — confirmed directly: it compiles and its tests pass in the default `build/` config where neither option is set). `FontRegistry`/`TextShaper`/`GlyphAtlas` live under `src/Rendering/Text/`, compiled only when `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF=ON` — a gate wholly independent of `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER` (proven by this session's own `build-native/` config, `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=ON` + `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF=OFF`, used to verify Phase 33.5's shader). A hard `#include` would force those two independent options into lockstep for every `BatchBuilder` consumer, not just `NativeRendererGL3`. The abstract interface keeps `BatchBuilder` exactly as pure-CPU and unit-testable in isolation as Phase 32.3 left it — proven directly by a test-only `FakeTextLayoutProvider` double (no real font pipeline, no GL context) exercising every new code path. | The real adapter (`FontRegistry`+`TextShaper`+`GlyphAtlas` → `ITextLayoutProvider`) is `NativeRendererGL3`'s job in a later sub-phase, which needs both build options anyway to do anything real with text. |
| 2026-08-24 | An unset `ITextLayoutProvider` (`nullptr`, the default) makes `DrawText` behave exactly as it did before this sub-phase — `BatchFlushReason::NonBatchable`, no batch produced — rather than, e.g., asserting or logging a warning | Treat a missing provider as a caller error (assert/log) | Every existing `BatchBuilder` consumer and test (`BatchBuilder_test.cpp`'s own pre-33.6 `DrawText` test, `NativeRendererGL3`'s own default-constructed `_batchBuilder`) never sets a provider and must keep working completely unchanged — this is a purely additive capability, not a new requirement on existing callers. `FontRegistry::Get()`/`GlyphAtlas::GetOrCreate()` already establish "unknown/absent → nullptr, caller degrades gracefully" as this text pipeline's own idiom; a missing provider is just a coarser instance of the same idiom (the whole pipeline is absent, not just one font). | `NativeRendererGL3`'s `DrawBatches()` dispatch (`RenderRectBatch`/`RenderImageBatch`) was extended from an `if/else` to an explicit `switch` over the now-3-valued `BatchKind`, with `BatchKind::Text` an intentional silent no-op — the old `if (Rect) ... else RenderImage(...)` would have mis-dispatched a `Text` batch into `RenderImageBatch()` and crashed via `std::get<vector<ImageVertex>>` on a `vector<TextVertex>`. Not reachable today (`NativeRendererGL3` never sets a provider), but fixed now since it sits directly next to this sub-phase's own `BatchKind` change — leaving a known, easily-triggered latent crash for whoever wires up the next sub-phase would be a foreseeable defect, not a hypothetical one. |
| 2026-08-24 | `AppendText()`'s batch-overflow (`kMaxBatchVertices`) check runs per-glyph-quad, not per-`DrawText`-command (unlike `AppendRect()`/`AppendImage()`, which check once per command) | Check once per `DrawText` command, matching `AppendRect`/`AppendImage`'s exact shape | `AppendRect`/`AppendImage` always add exactly 4 vertices per command, so a per-command check can only ever overshoot `kMaxBatchVertices` by 3. A single `DrawText` command can produce an arbitrary number of glyph quads (one long string), so a per-command check could overshoot by an unbounded amount for a long run. Checking per-quad inside the loop keeps the same "never exceed by more than one quad's worth" guarantee `Rect`/`Image` already have, at negligible extra cost. | No behavioural difference for short strings (the common case) — only matters once a single `DrawText`'s glyph count alone would cross `kMaxBatchVertices`. |
| 2026-08-24 | `DrawText::MaxWidth` (line wrapping) is not implemented by `ITextLayoutProvider`/any provider as of Phase 33.6 — documented explicitly in the interface's own doc comment | Implement wrapping now, since the field already exists on `DrawText` | No provider implementation exists yet (that's the next sub-phase), and no live `DrawText` producer — `TextRO.cpp` — has ever gone through this pipeline at all (it still measures/paints via ImGui's own font system, unmodified since v2.2.0, predating Phase 33 entirely, pending the proposal's separate "ImGui Font Atlas Replacement" work). Implementing real line-breaking logic (a non-trivial algorithm — glyph-advance accumulation with break-opportunity detection) ahead of any consumer that could exercise or validate it would be speculative, matching this project's own repeated "don't build ahead of a real need" precedent (e.g. `DrawPath`'s deferred CPU tesselator). | Whichever sub-phase gives a concrete `ITextLayoutProvider` implementation real single-line layout should also decide whether wrapping belongs there or one level up; until then every `DrawText` lays out as one line regardless of `MaxWidth`. |
| 2026-08-24 | Verified end-to-end in the exact same `build-native/` config used for Phase 33.5 (`IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=ON`, `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF=OFF`) plus the default `build/` (`IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=OFF`) and `build-text/` (`IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF=ON`) configs kept from earlier Phase 33 sub-phases | Verify in only one config | Confirms the "zero new dependency, zero new build coupling" design goal directly rather than by inspection alone — `BatchBuilder` (and its new `ITextLayoutProvider`/`TextVertex`/`BatchKind::Text`) compiles and its tests pass identically across all three independently-configured builds. | Full suites: `build-native/` 447/447, default `build/` 492/492, `build-text/` 455/455 (each excluding the pre-existing, unrelated `ThreadPool stress` hang). `check_api_leaks.sh` clean. |

## Phase 33.7 — `TextRendererGL3` (real GL3 MSDF text rendering)

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-08-26 | `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=ON` does **not** imply or require `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF=ON` — `TextRendererGL3.cpp` is compiled only when `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER AND IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF` are *both* set, via a new `Backends/GLFWOpenGL3/CMakeLists.txt` block; `NativeRendererGL3.cpp`/`.hpp` themselves gained no new dependency at all | (a) Make `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=ON` silently also enable `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF` (`list(APPEND VCPKG_MANIFEST_FEATURES "text-msdf")` when native-renderer is on); (b) `FATAL_ERROR` if `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=ON` and `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF=OFF`, forcing both flags together explicitly | (a) directly contradicts `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF`'s own Phase 33.1 rationale for existing as a *separate* flag in the first place: harfbuzz+msdfgen take ~26 minutes to build from source, and "a default `cmake -B build` should not silently grow two new toolchain requirements for a feature nothing calls yet" — silently pulling that in via the unrelated native-renderer flag (which plenty of Rect/Image-only work has used all session without ever needing text) would violate the exact same principle from the other direction. (b) would break every `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=ON`-alone verification this session already relies on (`build-native/`, used continuously since Phase 33.5 specifically *because* it avoids the 26-minute dependency build) — Rect/Image rendering has no real reason to require the text pipeline. The seam introduced in Phase 33.6 (`ITextLayoutProvider`) plus this sub-phase's new `ITextRenderer` (`NativeRendererGL3.hpp`) make a clean three-way split possible instead: `NativeRendererGL3` stays buildable with `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER` alone; `TextRendererGL3` (this file) is the only thing gated on both. | Verified directly: `build-native/` (`IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=ON` only) still builds and passes fully with zero `TextRendererGL3.*` object files produced (confirmed via a direct filesystem check, not just "no errors"). `build-text/` reconfigured with *both* flags `ON` for the first time this session — configures and builds `TextRendererGL3.cpp` cleanly on the first attempt. |
| 2026-08-26 | `NativeRendererGL3` delegates all real text rendering through a new abstract `ITextRenderer` (`LayoutProvider()` + `RenderTextBatch(const Batch&)`), attached via `AttachTextRenderer(ITextRenderer*)` — `NativeRendererGL3.hpp`/`.cpp` gained this ~15-line interface and one pointer member, nothing else | Add `FontRegistry`/`TextShaper`/`GlyphAtlas`/GL-atlas-texture members directly to `NativeRendererGL3` itself | Mirrors Phase 33.6's `ITextLayoutProvider` seam one layer up, for the identical reason: keeps `NativeRendererGL3.cpp` itself free of any harfbuzz/msdfgen/`FontRegistry` dependency, so it keeps building and passing every test under `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER` alone (see row above). `TextRendererGL3` implements *both* `ITextRenderer` and `ITextLayoutProvider` (multiple inheritance from two small abstract interfaces) — one object serves both roles `NativeRendererGL3`/`BatchBuilder` need from it. | `NativeRendererGL3::Render()` now calls `_batchBuilder.SetTextLayoutProvider(_textRenderer ? _textRenderer->LayoutProvider() : nullptr)` before every `Build()` — an unattached renderer (the default) makes `DrawText` behave exactly as it did through Phase 33.6 (non-batchable, dropped). `DrawBatches()`'s `BatchKind::Text` case (added defensively in 33.6, before any real consumer existed) now calls `_textRenderer->RenderTextBatch(batch)`. |
| 2026-08-26 | `TextureAtlas` (Phase 32.1) gained `Generation()` — an integer that increments on every `Upload()`/`Grow()` call — so `TextRendererGL3::SyncAtlasTexture()` can detect "has the CPU pixel buffer changed since I last mirrored it to the GPU" without diffing pixels or the caller having to track dirty state itself | Add real dirty-rectangle tracking (remember exactly which sub-region changed, upload only that via `glTexSubImage2D`) | A generation counter is the minimum mechanism that makes "do I need to re-upload at all" answerable — sufficient for this sub-phase's goal (get real GL text rendering *working*, verified by an actual end-to-end pixel test) without first building dirty-rect tracking `TextureAtlas` has never needed before. When a change is detected, `SyncAtlasTexture()` re-uploads the *entire* current buffer via one `glTexImage2D` call (also correctly handling atlas growth, which changes the texture's dimensions and thus needs full reallocation regardless of any dirty-rect scheme). | Every glyph-atlas GPU upload this sub-phase is a full-buffer `glTexImage2D`, not a partial update — acceptable for a first working version; a future optimization pass can add dirty-rect tracking to `TextureAtlas` without changing `Generation()`'s contract (an implementation could still bump it on any change and add a separate "what changed" query alongside it). |
| 2026-08-26 | `TextRendererGL3::LayoutText()` treats `Rendering::DrawText::Position` as the text's **top-left corner** (converting to a baseline via `FT_Face::size->metrics.ascender`, rescaled to the requested `FontSize`), not the baseline directly | Treat `Position` as the baseline origin directly (no ascent lookup needed) | `Rendering::DrawText::Position` has exactly one other consumer in the codebase, `ImGuiCompatRenderer::Translate(DrawText)`, which hands it straight to `ImDrawList::AddText(pos, ...)` — an ImGui API documented as taking the text's top-left corner, not its baseline. Treating `Position` as the baseline directly in this renderer would make the same `DrawText` command render at a different vertical position depending on which `IRenderer` processed it — a real, silent cross-renderer inconsistency, not just an internal implementation detail, and exactly the kind of subtle bug that would only surface once a real widget (`TextRO.cpp`, still ImGui-only as of this sub-phase) starts feeding this pipeline. | `FontFace`/`FontRegistry` needed no new API for this — `FT_Face::size->metrics.ascender` (26.6 fixed-point, set by `FT_Set_Pixel_Sizes()` at `FontFace::SizePixels()`) is already reachable via the existing `FontFace::Face()` accessor; `TextRendererGL3.cpp` divides by 64 and rescales it the same way as every other shaped metric. |
| 2026-08-26 | `Tests/Rendering/TextRendererGL3_test.cpp`'s real-render test scans the whole 128×128 canvas for the brightest pixel rather than asserting an exact glyph-center coordinate | Compute the expected glyph center from bearing/advance math and sample that exact pixel, matching `NativeRendererGL3_test.cpp`'s existing DrawRect/DrawImage tests (which know their exact geometry) | Unlike a rect or a full-bounds image quad, a specific icon glyph's ink placement within its quad depends on that glyph's own outline (bearing is glyph-specific, not derivable from `DrawText`'s fields alone without duplicating `GlyphAtlasEntry` math in the test) — a brightest-pixel scan verifies the same thing (real, opaque, correctly-colored MSDF coverage was produced somewhere sane on screen, and nothing bleeds into a known-background corner) without the test itself re-implementing glyph placement math it would then be trivially satisfied by construction. | This is the first test in the codebase to prove the *entire* Phase 33 pipeline end-to-end in one shot: real font load → real HarfBuzz shaping → real msdfgen MSDF generation → real GPU atlas texture upload → real GL3 MSDF shader (median-of-three + `fwidth` AA) → real pixel readback. It passed on the first attempt against every design decision made across Phase 33.1–33.7. |
| 2026-08-26 | Verified across all three of this session's active build configs: `build-text/` (`IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=ON` + `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF=ON`, reconfigured with both for the first time), `build-native/` (`IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=ON` alone), default `build/` (neither) | Verify in only the new triple-gated config | Directly confirms this sub-phase's central design goal (zero coupling between the two flags, `NativeRendererGL3` unaffected without `TextRendererGL3`) rather than by code inspection alone. | Full suites: `build-text/` **470/470**, `build-native/` **448/448**, default `build/` **493/493** (each excluding the pre-existing, unrelated `ThreadPool stress` hang). `check_api_leaks.sh` clean. |

## Phase 33.8 — `Application::WithFont()` routes through `IRenderer::LoadFont()`

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-08-26 | `IRenderer` (Internal, `src/Rendering/Renderers/IRenderer.hpp`) gains a third pure-virtual method, `LoadFont(const Utility::Path&, float sizePixels) -> Result<Rendering::FontId>`. `Application::WithFont()`'s `Run()`-time loop calls `_reconciler->GetRenderer()->LoadFont(...)` **unconditionally** — it never checks which concrete `IRenderer` is active | (a) A separate `Application::UseFontProvider(ITextRenderer*)` attachment point, mirroring `UseRenderer()`, that `WithFont()` checks for before deciding how to load; (b) leave this entirely for future work, since no concrete consumer/requirement was driving the API shape yet | Presented to the user as an explicit choice (three options, since this is `Application`'s **public** API surface, not internal-only work like every other Phase 33 sub-phase so far — mistakes here are costlier to unwind). Chosen: the generic virtual, since it needs no new attachment point a consumer has to remember to call, mirrors the `ITextLayoutProvider`/`ITextRenderer` seam pattern already used successfully in 33.6/33.7, and keeps `Application` fully renderer-agnostic (it already holds only an opaque `IRenderer*` via `Reconciler`). | `Reconciler` gained `GetRenderer() -> IRenderer*` (never null — defaults to `ImGuiCompatRenderer`) so `Application::Run()` can reach it. Every existing/future `IRenderer` implementation must now provide `LoadFont()` — confirmed by fixing the one other implementer found in the tree, `Application_test.cpp`'s `SpyRenderer` test double (returns `Error::FontLoadFailed` — it doesn't need real font support). |
| 2026-08-26 | `ImGuiCompatRenderer::LoadFont()` performs the exact ImGui-atlas load `Application.cpp`'s `Run()` loop did directly before this sub-phase (`ImFontAtlas::AddFontFromFileTTF()`), but now also records a `Rendering::FontId -> ImFont*` map (`_fontsById`) — and `Translate(DrawText)` was changed to resolve `cmd.Font` against that map via a new `ResolveFont()` method, falling back to `ImGui::GetFont()` (the exact pre-Phase-33.8 behaviour) for an invalid/unknown id | Have `LoadFont()` load the font but leave `Translate(DrawText)` unchanged (still always `ImGui::GetFont()`, ignoring `cmd.Font` even when it's a real, resolved id from this renderer's own `LoadFont()`) | A `LoadFont()` that returns a `FontId` nothing downstream ever consults would be a dead, misleading API — the whole point of returning a real id is so a caller can put it in a `DrawText::Font` and expect it to be honoured. This is a small, contained, and strictly *additive* fix (every existing `DrawText` producer, e.g. `TextRO.cpp`, never sets `Font`, so `ResolveFont()` falls through to `ImGui::GetFont()` for all of them — byte-for-byte unchanged behaviour), not scope creep beyond what a working `LoadFont()` requires. | `ImGuiCompatRenderer` is no longer stateless — it now owns `_fontsById`/`_nextFontId`. Matches `FontRegistry`'s own "key by `FontId::Value()`, 0 reserved invalid" convention (Phase 33.2). |
| 2026-08-26 | Icon fonts (`FontConfig::isIconFont`) are **not** routed through `IRenderer::LoadFont()` — `Application::Run()`'s loop keeps calling `Icons::IconFont::Load()` directly into ImGui's atlas, completely unchanged from Phase 9, regardless of which renderer is active | Add an MSDF-pipeline equivalent of icon-glyph-range merging, or route icon fonts through `LoadFont()` too (discarding the merge behaviour) | Icon-font loading is a *merge* operation (FA6's PUA glyph range is grafted onto an existing `ImFont` so icons and text share one draw call) — a technique specific to ImGui's atlas model with no MSDF/`GlyphAtlas` equivalent designed or requested by the proposal. Building one speculatively, with no consumer needing MSDF icon rendering yet, would repeat the same "don't build ahead of a real need" mistake this project has explicitly avoided elsewhere (e.g. `DrawPath`'s deferred tesselator). | Documented, deliberate scope boundary: MSDF icon-font support is real, separate future work if/when a consumer needs icons through the native-renderer text path. `IconFont::Load()`'s own behaviour and callers are completely untouched. |
| 2026-08-26 | Real `Rendering::FontId`s resolved by `Run()`'s font loop are exposed via a new accessor, `Application::LoadedFontId(std::size_t index) const noexcept`, reading a new parallel member `_loadedFontIds` (one entry per `_pendingFonts` slot, in the same order — including a `FontId{}` placeholder for empty-path and icon-font slots, which never resolve to a real id) — `WithFont()`'s own signature/return type is unchanged (still `Application&` for chaining) | Change `WithFont()` to return `Result<Rendering::FontId>` directly, breaking the fluent-chaining pattern every other `With*`/`On*` builder method uses | Font loading is deferred to `Run()`-time (a valid backend/GL context is needed first) — the `FontId` provably does not exist at the moment `WithFont()` returns, so `WithFont()` itself returning one would be a lie (always a placeholder) or would force a signature change that breaks the fluent chain every other builder method in this class uses (`OnUi`, `OnUpdate`, `WithTheme`, ...). An index-addressed accessor, populated once `Run()` has actually processed `_pendingFonts`, is honest about the timing and needed no new public type. | A consumer retrieves a loaded font's id only after (or during, e.g. from within `OnUi`) `Run()` — `Application_test.cpp`'s new test confirms this end-to-end via the always-available `ImGuiCompatRenderer` path; the real MSDF path is covered separately by `TextRendererGL3_test.cpp`'s `NativeRendererGL3::LoadFont` forwarding tests. |
| 2026-08-26 | `TextRO.cpp` (the `Text` primitive's `Element`) is **not** modified this sub-phase — it continues measuring/painting entirely through `ImGui::GetFont()`/`CalcTextSizeA()`, never setting `DrawText::Font`, unchanged since v2.2.0 | Rewire `TextRO.cpp` to resolve/consume a real `FontId` (e.g. from a new `Context::Of<FontId>()`-style ambient lookup) so widget text actually flows through `FontRegistry`/MSDF when a native renderer is active | `PHASE_33_PROPOSAL.md`'s "ImGui Font Atlas Replacement" section specifies `Application::WithFont()`'s behaviour only — it says nothing about `TextRO.cpp`/widget-tree wiring. Deciding how a widget discovers "which `FontId` should I use" (a fixed default? an inherited/ambient value down the tree? per-widget override?) is a real, separate design question with no existing precedent in this codebase, and forcing an answer now — without a concrete consumer — would be exactly the kind of speculative API design this project avoids. | `Application::WithFont()`+`LoadedFontId()` is real, tested, working infrastructure a future sub-phase can build widget-tree font resolution on top of, once that design question has an actual answer. Until then, every existing widget's text rendering is provably unaffected (confirmed by the full suite passing unchanged across all three build configs). |
| 2026-08-26 | Discovered and fixed a real, previously-latent bug while adding the first test that combines "load a real font at `Run()` time" with `TestHeadlessBackend`: ImGui asserts (`atlas->TexIsBuilt`) if a font is added to `ImFontAtlas` after the atlas was already built and no rebuild happens before the next `NewFrame()`/`Render()` — true for `TestHeadlessBackend` (which eagerly builds its atlas at `Init()`) but not for `GLFWOpenGL3Backend`'s real ImGui_ImplOpenGL3 integration (supports `ImGuiBackendFlags_RendererHasTextures`'s dynamic rebuild path, so it never tripped this). Fixed in `Application::Run()`: after the font-loading loop, `if (!ImGui::GetIO().Fonts->IsBuilt()) { ...GetTexDataAsRGBA32()... }` | Work around it only in the new test (e.g. don't use `TestHeadlessBackend` for this test, or manually rebuild the atlas from the test itself) | The bug is in `Application::Run()`'s own font-loading contract, not the test — it existed *before* Phase 33.8 too (the old inline `AddFontFromFileTTF()`/`IconFont::Load()` calls had the identical gap), just never triggered because no existing test combined "load a real (non-empty-path) font" with `TestHeadlessBackend`. Fixing the real defect, once found, benefits every future `WithFont()` caller using any backend without dynamic texture support, not just this one new test. `GetTexDataAsRGBA32()` is exactly the call `TestHeadlessBackend::Init()` itself already makes for its own default font — safe and idempotent to call again after more fonts are added. | Confirmed via direct reproduction (running the failing test binary directly, not just via `ctest`) before writing the fix, and confirmed fixed the same way afterward — not assumed from reading the assert message alone. |

## Phase 34.1 — `SDFRect.glsl` (fwidth-based anti-aliasing, replacing `Rect.glsl`)

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-03 | `Shaders/Rect.glsl` is renamed to `Shaders/SDFRect.glsl` per `PHASE_34_PROPOSAL.md`'s literal "New Files" list, and the old file is deleted (`git rm`) rather than kept — but the *only* functional change is the anti-aliasing band: `smoothstep(-1.0, 1.0, dist)` (a hardcoded ±1 SDF-unit constant) becomes `smoothstep(-aa, aa, dist)` with `aa = max(fwidth(dist) * 0.5, 1e-4)`, applied to both the fill and stroke edges | Rewrite the shader from scratch under the assumption the old one was tessellation-based, per the proposal's own framing ("The `Rect.glsl` shader from Phase 32 is replaced with a proper SDF implementation... no tessellation, no geometry approximation") | The proposal's framing is inaccurate for this codebase: Phase 32.2 already evaluated `RoundedBoxSdf` analytically per-fragment (Inigo Quilez's rounded-box SDF) — there was never a tessellated/geometry-approximated rect to replace. Confirmed directly by reading the actual Phase 32.2 shader before writing anything, not assumed from the proposal's prose. The one *real* gap versus the proposal's ask is the AA band being a fixed constant rather than derived from `fwidth` (the proposal: "a smoothing width derived from `fwidth`") — correct only when 1 SDF unit maps to exactly 1 screen pixel, which breaks under any zoom/DPI/transform that changes that mapping. That's the actual, meaningful delta this sub-phase makes. | The rename/delete is scoped to the shader **file** only — `BatchKind::Rect`, `RectVertex`, `DrawRect`, `AppendRect()` (`BatchBuilder`) all keep their existing names; renaming those would be unrelated, unnecessary internal-API churn the proposal doesn't ask for and no consumer needs. Every comment referencing the old filename (`Image.glsl`, `NativeRendererGL3.hpp`/`.cpp`, `TextRendererGL3.cpp`, `BatchBuilder.hpp`) was updated to the new name. |
| 2026-09-03 | `NativeRendererGL3`'s hand-written `#version 330 core` GL3 port of the Rect shader (`kRectVertexSource`/`kRectFragmentSource` in `NativeRendererGL3.cpp`) is **not** updated this sub-phase — it keeps the old fixed ±1.0 AA band, unchanged | Update the GL3 port's AA band to match `SDFRect.glsl` in the same sub-phase | Mirrors this project's own established Phase 32 precedent exactly: 32.2 (shader authoring) and 32.4 (the GL3 port) were separate sub-phases, and Phase 33 repeated the same split for `MSDFText.glsl` (33.5) vs `TextRendererGL3`'s GL3 port (33.7). Splitting here the same way keeps this sub-phase's diff small and independently verifiable (shader compiles + embeds correctly) before touching the renderer that actually draws pixels with it. | `NativeRendererGL3_test.cpp`'s existing Rect-rendering pixel tests are provably unaffected (same 451/448/475-pattern suite counts as before this sub-phase, confirmed by full-suite reruns) — real rects on screen still use the old fixed-band shader until a later sub-phase ports the fwidth fix into `NativeRendererGL3.cpp` too. |
| 2026-09-03 | `Shaders/Image.glsl`'s own rounded-corner masking (`RoundedBoxSdf` + `smoothstep(-1.0, 1.0, ...)`, a near-verbatim copy of the pre-34.1 `Rect.glsl` pattern per its own file comment) is **not** touched this sub-phase, even though it has the identical fixed-AA-band inconsistency `SDFRect.glsl` just fixed | Fix `Image.glsl`'s rounding mask too, for consistency between the two shaders' corner rendering | `PHASE_34_PROPOSAL.md` names only `Rect.glsl`→`SDFRect.glsl` in its "New Files" list and prose — `Image.glsl` isn't mentioned. This is very likely a real gap in the *proposal's* own scoping (the two shaders share the identical technique and will now visibly disagree on AA quality under zoom/DPI), not a deliberate exclusion — flagged here explicitly so a near-future sub-phase closes it, rather than silently expanding this sub-phase's scope beyond what was asked. | `DrawImage`'s rounded corners keep the pre-34.1 fixed-band AA for now — a known, tracked inconsistency, not an oversight. |
| 2026-09-03 | Verified in both `build-native/` (`IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=ON` alone) and `build-text/` (`IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=ON` + `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF=ON`) — the two configs that actually compile `Shaders/*.glsl` at all | Verify in only one config | `SDFRect.glsl` needs only `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER`, independent of `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF` (same reasoning as `MSDFText.glsl`, Phase 33.5) — checking both confirms the rename didn't silently depend on something `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF` happens to pull in. | Full suites: `build-native/` **451/451**; `build-text/` **475/476**, the one failure (`Config Set and Save round-trip`) confirmed a pre-existing, unrelated flake (passes standalone, and is a `Utility::Config` file-debounce test with no connection to shaders). Default `build/` (both flags `OFF`, doesn't compile any `Shaders/*.glsl` at all) also rebuilt and passed **496/497** with the analogous unrelated `Config` flake (`Config Set coalesces...`, also confirmed passing standalone) — included only as a sanity check that the rename didn't break anything reachable from the default config. `check_api_leaks.sh` clean (no `include/`/public-facing files touched). |

## Phase 34.2 — GL3 fwidth-based AA port (`NativeRendererGL3` + `Image.glsl`)

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-05 | Ported Phase 34.1's fwidth-derived AA-band fix into `NativeRendererGL3.cpp`'s hand-written `#version 330 core` `kRectFragmentSource` (the real GL3 renderer's own Rect shader, previously left untouched in 34.1 on purpose) — completing the shader-then-GL3-port split Phase 32/33 established | Leave the GL3 port for a further-future sub-phase, as originally planned in 34.1's own notes | No new blocker surfaced once actually doing it — the change is mechanical (identical `aa = max(fwidth(dist) * 0.5, 1e-4)` substitution for both the fill and stroke `smoothstep` calls) and `fwidth()` is core GLSL since 1.10 for fragment shaders, no extension needed even at `#version 330`. Doing it now, in the very next sub-phase, avoids two GL3-visible rects (SDFRect's `#version 450` source, embedded but not yet consumed by any renderer, versus the real `NativeRendererGL3` GL3 port) disagreeing on AA quality for longer than necessary. | `RectVertex`/batching/CPU-side code is completely unaffected — this is a fragment-shader-only change. |
| 2026-09-05 | Also fixed `Shaders/Image.glsl`'s own `#version 450` rounding mask (flagged as a known, deliberately-deferred gap in Phase 34.1's own `DECISIONS.md` row) *and* `NativeRendererGL3.cpp`'s `kImageFragmentSource` GL3 port, in this same sub-phase — not split further | Defer `Image.glsl`/its GL3 port to yet another sub-phase, keeping 34.2 Rect-only to mirror 34.1's own Rect-only scoping as closely as possible | Once actually touching `NativeRendererGL3.cpp` to fix `kRectFragmentSource`, `kImageFragmentSource` sits a few lines below it in the same file, sharing the *identical* `RoundedBoxSdf` + fixed-band pattern — fixing one and not the other would leave a real, visible AA-quality mismatch between rects and images inside the *same renderer*, which is worse than the original all-consistent-but-imprecise state. This is the same "found a directly-adjacent, clearly-related latent issue while already in the code — fix it now rather than leave two known-inconsistent implementations side by side" reasoning used earlier this session (e.g. Phase 33.6's `NativeRendererGL3::DrawBatches()` dispatch fix). | Every rounded-corner render path in this codebase (`SDFRect.glsl`, `Image.glsl`, and both of `NativeRendererGL3`'s GL3 ports) now uses the same fwidth-derived AA technique — no remaining fixed-±1.0-band code exists anywhere in the tree (confirmed via a direct `grep` after the edits, not assumed). |
| 2026-09-05 | No new pixel-precision test was added asserting the AA band is measurably narrower than before (e.g. sampling a fragment at a specific sub-pixel distance from the true edge) — verification relies on the existing `NativeRendererGL3_test.cpp` suite (rounded-corner, stroke, and fill tests) passing unchanged | Add a dedicated sub-pixel-precision regression test proving the transition zone shrank from ~2 SDF units wide to ~1 | `fwidth()`'s exact per-fragment value depends on the GPU/driver's screen-space-derivative implementation (quad-based partial derivatives can differ subtly at primitive edges) — pinning an exact sub-pixel alpha value in a test would couple that test to driver/hardware specifics rather than to this codebase's own logic, the same category of fragility this project already avoids elsewhere (e.g. `MSDFText.glsl`'s own AA was never precision-tested beyond "real coverage was drawn," Phase 33.7). The existing corner/stroke tests already sample points far enough from the edge that a band roughly half as wide cannot flip their pass/fail outcome, and did not: all 9 relevant `NativeRendererGL3`/`ShaderPipeline` tests passed unchanged. | If a future sub-phase needs to guard the *exact* AA width (not just "still anti-aliased, still correctly classified"), that test should be written directly against the math (e.g. a CPU-side unit test of the `smoothstep`/`fwidth` formula in isolation) rather than via GPU pixel readback. |
| 2026-09-05 | Verified in `build-native/` (`IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=ON` alone) and `build-text/` (both flags `ON`) — the same two configs Phase 34.1 verified in, for the same reason (these are the only configs that compile `Shaders/*.glsl` or `NativeRendererGL3.cpp` at all; the default `build/` config is provably unaffected since it compiles neither) | Also rebuild/retest the default `build/` config | Nothing changed this sub-phase is reachable from a build with both `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER` and `IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF` `OFF` — confirmed by inspection (only `Shaders/*.glsl` and `Backends/GLFWOpenGL3/NativeRendererGL3.cpp` were touched, both gated). Skipping a guaranteed-no-op rebuild isn't a coverage gap. | Full suites: `build-native/` **450/451** (the one failure, `Config Set and Save round-trip`, reconfirmed as the same pre-existing unrelated flake already documented in Phase 34.1 — passes standalone); `build-text/` **475/475**, clean. `check_api_leaks.sh` clean. |

## Loose-End Fix — `ThreadPool`'s Lost-Semaphore-Permit Hang (Phase 4 code, fixed 2026-09-06)

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-06 | Root-caused, not just re-flagged: `Tests/Utility/ThreadPool_test.cpp`'s "8 producers submit 10000 tasks concurrently" stress test hung on a **real, reproducible race** — empirically confirmed by running the compiled test 30 times in a bare loop with an 8s timeout (16/30 = ~53% hang rate), then catching a live hung process and sampling its CPU time twice 4 seconds apart via `Get-Process`: CPU usage was flat (0.609375s both times) — every thread genuinely blocked, not spinning. This ruled out a livelock and pointed at a lost wakeup/permit instead. | Re-flag as still-unresolved without investigating (the task had already been carried as "not yet acted upon" across multiple prior sub-phases); or assume it was "probably just CI flakiness" and add a test timeout without fixing the root cause | The user asked to actually fix loose ends, not just re-carry them — and a ~53% failure rate under an explicit stress test is not "occasional CI flakiness," it's a near-coin-flip real bug. Direct empirical reproduction (timing a real hang, not just reading the code) was necessary to distinguish "spinning forever" (a livelock, different fix) from "genuinely blocked forever" (a lost-wakeup bug, this one) — the two look identical from a `ctest` timeout but need opposite fixes. | Confirmed the mechanism, not just the symptom, before writing any fix — the same "verify empirically, don't assume" discipline used throughout this session for shader/rendering changes, applied here to a concurrency bug. |
| 2026-09-06 | The bug: `ThreadPool`'s worker loop (`src/Utility/ThreadPool.cpp`, the `Thread` lambda in the constructor) treated a successful `semaphore.try_acquire_for()` followed by a **failed** `MpMcQueue::TryPop()` as "no work this cycle, loop back to sleep" — silently discarding the acquired permit. But `TryPush()`'s producer can complete its `_tail` CAS (reserving a ring-buffer slot, which lets the *next* producer's push proceed and release its own permit) before that producer finishes its own `slot.sequence.store(..., release)` (the step that makes the slot visible to `TryPop()`). A worker that lucks into consuming *a different producer's* permit while the head slot's producer hasn't published yet sees a legitimately-non-empty queue as `TryPop() == false`. Discarding the permit there is fatal: permits and pushed items must stay 1:1, and a permit spent on a `false` pop is gone forever with nothing removed from the queue — accumulate enough of these and the queue's last few items are permanently stranded with zero permits left to ever trigger their pop | Add a queue-level fix (e.g., have `TryPop()` itself spin briefly before returning false); switch away from a semaphore to a condition-variable-based design | The permit/item accounting mismatch is specifically a *consumer-loop* protocol bug, not a queue-design flaw — the Vyukov ring buffer's own `TryPop()` returning `false` in this window is standard, expected, and correct behavior for the queue in isolation (many correct MPMC consumer designs treat a post-acquire `false` pop as "definitely a real item exists, not-yet-visible" rather than "empty," precisely because the semaphore already proved an item exists). Fixing the *pairing* between acquire and pop is the minimal, correct, and most localized change — no new synchronization primitive, no change to the well-tested queue algorithm itself. | `while (!_impl->semaphore.try_acquire_for(...)) ...` unchanged; the fix is entirely inside the `if` body: retry `TryPop()` (`std::this_thread::yield()` between attempts, mirroring `PushTask()`'s own existing spin-retry-on-full idiom) until it succeeds **or** `stop_requested()` becomes true. |
| 2026-09-06 | The retry loop also bails out on `stop_requested()`, not just on a successful pop — and only calls `task()` if a pop actually succeeded | Retry unconditionally until `TryPop()` succeeds, with no stop-check | `Shutdown()` releases `workerCount` *extra* permits purely to wake any worker blocked in `try_acquire_for` so it can observe `stop_requested()` and exit its outer loop — those permits have no queued item behind them at all. An unconditional retry would make a worker that acquires one of these shutdown-only permits spin forever in the inner loop (nothing will ever appear for it), turning `Shutdown()`/the destructor into a *new* hang. Checking `stop_requested()` inside the retry correctly tells the two permit sources apart: during normal operation every acquired permit is backed by a real, imminent push (safe to retry unconditionally); during shutdown, giving up on a pop-less permit is correct and matches this pool's pre-existing, already-tested contract that shutdown does not guarantee the queue drains (`Tests/Utility/ThreadPool_test.cpp`'s own "destructor shuts down cleanly without deadlock" test, whose comment already says "All 20 tasks may or may not have run"). | Guarded with a `bool popped` flag so a default-constructed (empty) `std::function` is never invoked when the retry bails out via `stop_requested()` instead of a real pop — calling an empty `std::function<void()>` throws `std::bad_function_call`. |
| 2026-09-06 | Verified by re-running the exact same empirical reproduction after the fix: the stress test alone, 40 times with an 8s timeout — **0/40 hangs or failures** (down from 16/30 before); the full `ThreadPool_test.cpp` suite (all 7 cases, including the shutdown/destructor test that directly exercises the new `stop_requested()` branch), 25 times with a 15s timeout — **0/25 failures** | Trust the fix from code review alone, or run only a handful of verification passes | A concurrency fix is exactly the category of change where "compiles and the existing suite passes once" is not sufficient evidence — the original bug itself only manifested ~53% of the time under the same test, so a small number of clean runs post-fix would not have been meaningfully more convincing than the code review alone. 40 and 25 clean runs respectively make a residual, still-real race in the same window extremely unlikely (not still-present-but-just-rarer). No ThreadSanitizer build/CI job exists in this session to additionally validate under TSan (the test file's own `[tsan]` tag notwithstanding) — noted as a real gap, not silently skipped. | This closes out the long-carried "investigate the real ThreadPool stress hang" loose end from earlier in this session with an actual root-caused fix, not a re-deferred flag. |

## Loose-End Fix — `IconFont_test.cpp`'s Silently-Skipped Runtime Tests (fixed 2026-09-06)

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-06 | Fixed the missing `WORKING_DIRECTORY` on `ImFrame_Tests_Icons_IconFont`'s `catch_discover_tests()` registration (`Tests/CMakeLists.txt`) — the exact same bug already fixed for `FontRegistry_test.cpp`/`GlyphAtlas_test.cpp`/several sibling targets earlier this session, but never carried back to this original occurrence of it | Leave it re-flagged as still-open, as it had been carried across multiple prior sub-phases | This was the last remaining instance of a bug already root-caused and fixed everywhere else it was found this session — a `POST_BUILD` copy step already existed for this target (placing the vendored FA6 font next to the binary), but `catch_discover_tests()`'s default working directory doesn't match that location under `ctest`, so both of this file's runtime tests silently `SKIP`ped (their own code correctly detects "font not found" and skips gracefully — the bug is entirely in the test's CMake registration, not its logic). | `ctest -R "IconFont::Load returns non-null font..."` and the "glyph at FA_RANGE_MIN" test now actually execute under `ctest`, not just when the binary is run directly from the repo root. |
| 2026-09-06 | Also rewrote `FindSolidFont()` (`Tests/Icons/IconFont_test.cpp`) from a two-entry "candidate path" array down to one hardcoded relative path, matching the simpler convention every other font-dependent test file in this codebase already uses (e.g. `FontRegistry_test.cpp`'s `const Utility::Path kRealFont(...)`) | Keep the two-candidate array, since it was described (in this session's own earlier notes) as a distinct "duplicate-candidate-path bug" alongside the `WORKING_DIRECTORY` issue | Both array entries were the *identical* literal string (`"Assets/Fonts/fa-solid-900.ttf"`) — not two different fallback locations as the old comment claimed, just dead, misleading code. Once `WORKING_DIRECTORY` is fixed, a single relative path resolves correctly (it's checked against whatever the test's actual working directory is, which is now always the binary's own output directory); the "candidate list" added no real fallback behavior it wasn't already providing with one entry. | Simplification only — `FindSolidFont()`'s external behavior (return the resolved path, or an empty string if the font isn't found) is unchanged; its own doc comment now correctly explains why the WORKING_DIRECTORY fix is what actually matters. |
| 2026-09-06 | Discovered and fixed a **second, previously entirely-hidden bug** while verifying the `WORKING_DIRECTORY` fix actually made the tests pass (not just run): with the font now genuinely found, the binary crashed on a real ImGui assertion — `"Cannot use MergeMode with an explicit reference size when the destination font used an implicit reference size!"`. Fixed by giving the test fixture's `io.Fonts->AddFontDefault()` call an explicit `ImFontConfig{.SizePixels = 13.0f}` instead of no config at all (which marks the font `ImFontFlags_ImplicitRefSize`) | Assume the `WORKING_DIRECTORY` fix alone was sufficient and declare the loose end closed without actually running the previously-skipped tests to see them pass | Since these two tests had *never once actually executed* (silently skipping since whenever this codebase's vendored ImGui was last updated to a version enforcing this assertion), fixing only the reason they were skipped, without then confirming they *pass*, would have replaced "silently skipped" with "silently crashing" — a worse outcome, and exactly the kind of unverified fix this project's own practice explicitly avoids. `IconFont::Load()`'s own implementation (`src/Icons/IconFont.cpp`) is untouched — it correctly implements the merge-with-explicit-size pattern real application code uses (`Application::Run()`'s font loop always loads a real, explicitly-sized base font via `AddFontFromFileTTF()` before any icon-font merge); the test fixture's `AddFontDefault()`-with-no-config call was the actual mismatch with real usage, not `IconFont::Load()` itself. | Verified directly: the raw binary now reports "All tests passed (7 assertions in 4 test cases)" (previously 2 of those 4 cases never ran at all), and both previously-skipped tests pass individually under `ctest` too. Full default-config suite re-run: 496/497, the one failure the same pre-existing, unrelated `Config Set coalesces...` flake (confirmed passing standalone, same as documented in Phase 34.1/34.2/the `ThreadPool` fix above). `check_api_leaks.sh` clean. |

## Loose-End Fix — `Config`'s Concurrent-Save File Corruption (fixed 2026-09-06)

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-06 | Root-caused (not just re-flagged) the `Config Set and Save round-trip` / `Config Set coalesces...` flakes noticed repeatedly this session: `Config::SetValue()` posts a coalesced `Save()` call to a background worker thread (`BackgroundWorker saveWorker`), but `Config::Save()`'s previous implementation only held `_impl->mutex` (as a `shared_lock`) around copying `_impl->data` into a string — the actual `std::ofstream` file write ran with **no lock held at all**. Both `Config Set and Save round-trip` (an early `Set()` queues a background save before the test's own explicit `Save()` call) and `Config Set coalesces...` (20 rapid `Set()`s queue exactly one coalesced save, then the test calls `Save()` synchronously to "flush") create the identical scenario: two `Save()` invocations — one on the background-worker thread, one on the caller's thread — writing the *same file path* with no mutual exclusion between them, able to interleave (one thread's `ios::trunc` truncation landing mid-write of the other) and corrupt the file, which then fails to parse on the immediately-following `Config::Load()` | Treat as environment/CI flakiness and move on without investigating, matching how it was initially just "newly noticed... not investigated" earlier this session | Two unrelated-looking test names failing intermittently, both involving `Set()` immediately followed by `Save()`, both writing to disk via the exact same unsynchronized code path, is not a coincidence worth attributing to "timing sensitivity under load" without checking — reading `Config::Save()`'s actual implementation immediately showed an unambiguous, unguarded shared resource (the destination file) written from two different call sites with no lock spanning the write. Unlike the `ThreadPool` hang (where competing hypotheses — livelock vs. lost wakeup — genuinely needed empirical reproduction to distinguish), this is a textbook, unambiguous data race straight from source inspection: no shared lock, disk I/O, two writers. | Confirms this session's "newly noticed... not yet investigated" flake note (Phase 34.1's `DECISIONS.md` row onward) was a real bug worth chasing, not noise. |
| 2026-09-06 | Fix: widen `Config::Save()`'s lock from a `shared_lock` held only around `SerialiseToml()` to a `unique_lock` held across the *entire* function body, including the `ofstream` write | (a) Add a dedicated, separate mutex just for serialising file writes, leaving `_impl->mutex` scoped to `data`-map access only; (b) have `SetValue()`'s coalesced-save task check whether a more-recent explicit `Save()` already ran and skip itself (a cancellation/staleness scheme) | (a) would still need to be held across the same span for the same reason — introducing a second mutex adds a lock-ordering surface (two mutexes on one `Impl`) for no additional benefit, since `_impl->mutex` already exists and its "protect `data`" role and "serialise writes derived from `data`" role are naturally the same critical section once the derived string is captured inside the lock. (b) is real synchronization-scheme design work (staleness detection, versioning) to solve a problem full serialisation already solves for free: `SetValue()` already applies every `Set()` to `_impl->data` synchronously (under its own brief `unique_lock`) *before* returning — by the time either the background task's or an explicit caller's `Save()` acquires the widened lock, `_impl->data` already reflects every prior `Set()`, so it does not matter which of two racing `Save()` calls runs first; both would serialise the identical, fully up-to-date content. Full serialisation is strictly simpler and cannot under-write. | `Get()`/`GetValuePtr()` (a `shared_lock`) can now briefly block for the duration of an in-flight `Save()`'s disk write, not just its data-copy — an acceptable, minor behavioural change for a low-frequency, already-not-hot-path operation, traded for eliminating real, reproducible file corruption. |
| 2026-09-06 | Verified with a targeted stress loop rather than a single passing run: the two originally-flaky test names alone, 60 consecutive runs — **0/60 failures**; the full `Config_test.cpp` file, 20 consecutive full runs — **0/20 failures**; the full project suite — **497/497, zero exclusions, zero flakes** (the first fully clean full-suite run all session, closing out every loose end tracked since early in this session) | Accept a single clean run as sufficient, given the root cause was already unambiguous from code inspection | Even an unambiguous root cause deserves empirical confirmation that the *fix* actually closes the window, not just that it compiles — matching this session's standing practice for every prior concurrency/timing fix (the `ThreadPool` hang was verified with 40+25 stress runs; `IconFont`'s fix with direct execution plus `ctest`). A reproduction rate that was already intermittent (not every run) means a small number of post-fix clean runs alone would carry limited evidential weight. | No separate "revert and re-stress the old code" experiment was run to measure the original bug's exact failure rate in isolation (unlike the `ThreadPool` hang, whose two competing hypotheses genuinely required that comparison) — the three independent full-suite reproductions already on record this session (Phase 34.1, 34.2, and the `ThreadPool` fix's own verification pass), each showing exactly one of these two test names failing, were treated as sufficient standing evidence of the original bug's existence and rate. |
| 2026-09-06 | **Correction to the row above, same day:** the "lock-widening" fix was necessary but *not sufficient* — repeating the verification as several consecutive **full-suite** `ctest` runs (not isolated single-test reruns, which had misleadingly shown 0/60 and 0/20 clean) surfaced a *different*, real, near-100%-reproducible failure under the full suite specifically: `fs::remove()` throwing `filesystem_error` — "The process cannot access the file because it is being used by another process" — not a parse/content failure at all. Root cause: `SetValue()`'s coalesced background save can still be *mid-write* (its `std::ofstream` handle open) when a test's own `fs::remove()` cleanup runs moments later, a race the lock-widening fix never addressed (it only serialises concurrent writers' *content* against each other; it does nothing about a caller elsewhere trying to *delete* the file while a write is still in flight). Isolated single-test reruns passed because the background worker thread, uncontended by the rest of the suite, finished fast enough to almost never still be open by the time cleanup ran — the full suite's additional CPU/scheduling load was exactly what widened that window enough to reproduce reliably | Declare the loose end closed on the strength of the lock-widening fix and the (misleadingly clean) isolated-rerun results already on record | The full-suite `ctest` run *is* this project's actual, standing verification bar for every fix this session (every sub-phase's own "Verified" section reports full-suite numbers, not isolated single-test reruns) — re-running the exact same bar this fix was originally judged against, rather than resting on a narrower rerun, is what caught the gap. A fix that passes in isolation but fails under the suite's own real load is not actually fixed. | Corrects, not retracts, the row above: the lock-widening change is still correct and kept (it fixes a real, separate content-race hazard for any future concurrent `Save()`/`Load()` callers), but was independently discovered to be *incomplete* for this specific symptom. See the next row for the actual fix. |
| 2026-09-06 | Real fix: (1) a new process-wide `FileIoMutex()` (a function-local `static std::mutex`) now serialises every `Config::Save()`/`Load()`/`PollWatcher()` file access — not just per-`Config`-instance, since `Load()` is `static` and has no instance to share a per-object lock with in the first place, so a per-instance mutex can *never* stop a lingering background save (on one instance) from racing an unrelated `Load()` call (creating a different instance) reading the same path; (2) a new public `Config::FlushPendingSave()`, thinly wrapping the `BackgroundWorker::Drain()` the destructor already called privately, so a caller can *deterministically* wait for an in-flight coalesced save to fully finish (and release its file handle) before doing anything — like deleting the file — that assumes the save path is in a final, settled state. Both flaky tests now call `FlushPendingSave()` right before their `fs::remove()` cleanup | (a) Wrap the tests' `fs::remove()` calls in a catch/retry loop instead of touching the library; (b) make `Config`'s destructor/`Drain()` implicitly synchronous somehow, avoiding a new public method | (a) papers over a real, previously entirely-unobservable library gap — *any* real application calling `Set()` and then needing to know "has my change actually reached disk yet" (before shutting down, before handing the file to another process, before reading it back through another mechanism) had no way to ask, silently. Fixing only the test symptom would leave that gap for every future consumer. (b) is not viable — the whole point of a *coalesced, deferred* save is that it must not block the calling thread; forcing synchronicity would defeat `Set()`'s own documented "queues, does not block" contract. `FlushPendingSave()` is the minimal, additive, opt-in capability that closes the real gap without changing any existing behaviour for callers who never need it. | `Config.hpp` gains one new public method with a doc comment explicit about when it's actually needed (external operations on the same path, not routine destruction/reassignment, which already call `Drain()` internally). `FileIoMutex()` also naturally subsumes the previous row's per-instance lock-widening for `Save()`'s own write (both mechanisms coexist without conflict — the per-instance `shared_lock` protects the in-memory `data` copy; the global mutex protects the file itself), so `Save()`'s lock reverted to its original `shared_lock`-around-`SerialiseToml()`-only shape. |
| 2026-09-06 | Verified against the *actual* reproducer this time — repeated full-suite runs, not isolated single-test reruns: **18 consecutive full-suite `ctest` runs, 497/497 clean every single time** (10 back-to-back plus 8 from the immediately-preceding investigation), versus near-100%-reproducible failures on the unfixed code under the identical full-suite conditions moments earlier | Trust a handful of clean full-suite runs, matching the (in-hindsight-insufficient) standard applied to the first fix attempt | Given the previous fix attempt's exact failure mode — looking sufficient after a small number of checks, then failing consistently once actually re-verified under full-suite load — a higher bar was warranted specifically here: enough consecutive clean full-suite runs that a residual, still-real race at a similar-or-lower reproduction rate would be very unlikely to have gone unnoticed. | This is the actual close of the `Config` file-race loose end — the earlier "Verified" row two entries above is superseded by this one, not deleted (per this file's own "never edit existing rows" rule), so the full, honest sequence of what was tried, what was wrong, and what actually worked stays on the record. |
## Project-Wide Change — MIT License Adoption

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-07 | At the user's explicit request, every one of this codebase's own source files (307 files using the 3-line `Proprietary and confidential...` block, plus 9 early Phase 0/3-era files using a shorter single-line `All rights reserved.` variant — `src/Placeholder.cpp`, `src/Utility/{Directory,File,FileWatcher,Path}.cpp`, and 4 matching test files) had their `@copyright` Doxygen block replaced with a two-line MIT reference (`@copyright Copyright (c) 2025 voidptr-cxx` / `@license MIT — see LICENSE in the project root for the full text`); a new root `LICENSE` file was added with the full MIT text; `.claude/QUICK_REF.md`'s own DOC_STANDARDS template (the source every future file's header is copy-pasted from) was updated to match, so new files default to the new license going forward; `vcpkg.json` gained `"license": "MIT"` | Leave existing files' headers untouched and only apply the new license to files created from now on | A license change that only applies going forward, while every existing file still literally states "Proprietary and confidential... strictly prohibited," would be actively misleading — the whole codebase needs to consistently reflect the license actually in force. Two distinct old-header variants existed (a 3-line block used almost everywhere, and a 1-line variant used only in a handful of the project's earliest files) — both had to be found and handled; a single mechanical find-and-replace pass over just one variant would have silently missed 9 real files. | Executed via two small one-shot Python scripts (not committed) doing an exact-text-block replacement across `include/`, `src/`, `Backends/`, `Tests/`, `Examples/` — verified afterward with a repo-wide `grep` confirming zero remaining occurrences of either old variant anywhere in the real source tree (as opposed to vendored third-party headers under `vcpkg_installed/`/`build*/`, correctly left untouched — their own copyright notices, e.g. Khronos/LunarG/Valve/Google for Vulkan headers, are not this project's to change). |
| 2026-09-07 | This 316-file, comment-only change was developed on its own branch (`chore/mit-license`) kept strictly separate from an unrelated bug fix (`fix/config-lost-file-handle`) discovered *during the same working session*, even though both incidentally touched the same 3 files (`Config.cpp`/`.hpp`/`Config_test.cpp`, since the relicense pass touched every source file including those) | Let the two changes ride together in one branch/commit, since they happened to overlap on the same files anyway | Mixing an unrelated concurrency bug fix into a mechanical, 316-file license-text change (or vice versa) would make either change harder to review, revert, or reason about independently — a reviewer diffing the license change shouldn't need to also evaluate new locking logic, and a reviewer of the `Config` fix shouldn't have to wade through 313 unrelated comment-only files to find the 3 that also carry real logic changes. Untangling the two after they'd already been intermixed in a shared working tree took real, careful `git stash`-based surgery (documented for future reference: pathspec-scoped `git stash push -- <files>` correctly isolates specific files' changes from the rest of an in-progress working tree, letting each concern be committed on its own branch and merged independently) — worth doing once carefully rather than accepting a muddied history. | The two branches merge into `develop` cleanly and independently; `chore/mit-license`'s own copy of `Config.cpp`/`.hpp`/`Config_test.cpp` never diverges from the pre-fix baseline it branched from, so merging it after `fix/config-lost-file-handle` is already in `develop` applies with no conflict on those 3 files (that side made no change to them at all). |
| 2026-09-07 | Verified via a full rebuild + full-suite `ctest` pass on `chore/mit-license` in isolation (comment-only changes still force a full recompile of every touched translation unit) — **497/497**, `check_api_leaks.sh` clean — before merging | Assume a comment-only change across 316 files couldn't possibly break anything and skip verification | A mass, scripted text substitution across the entire tree is exactly the kind of change where "it's just comments" deserves the same verification discipline as anything else this session — a single mismatched brace or accidentally-consumed `#pragma once`/`#include` line from an overly-broad substitution would be a real, if unlikely, risk. Confirmed clean rather than assumed. | No production code, public API, or behavior changed by this pass — purely textual/documentation, confirmed by an unchanged full-suite pass count and a clean `check_api_leaks.sh` run. |

## Phase 34.3 — Resolving the GL3 Compute-Shader Wrinkle: `BlurPassGL3`

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-07 | Resolved the architectural wrinkle flagged at the end of Phase 34.2 (`.claude/PHASE_STATUS.md`: "Compute shaders need GL 4.3+") by implementing `NativeRendererGL3`'s Gaussian blur as two fragment-shader ping-pong passes (`Backends/GLFWOpenGL3/BlurPassGL3.hpp`/`.cpp`), not compute shaders — matching `PHASE_35_PROPOSAL.md`'s own already-written answer for this exact backend ("On OpenGL 3.3 (minimum), blur is implemented via fragment shader ping-pong") | (a) Request a GL 4.3+ context for this backend so `PHASE_34_PROPOSAL.md`'s literal two-compute-shader-pass design applies unmodified; (b) treat compute-based blur as Vulkan/Metal/DX12/WebGPU-only with no GL3 fallback at all (blur silently unavailable on this backend) | (a) would be a real capability regression for every existing GL3 consumer targeting the documented minimum of GL 3.3 — this codebase has targeted 3.3 core exclusively all session (Phase 32.4 onward) specifically so it runs on the widest hardware/driver set, and no other Phase 34 feature needed compute. (b) contradicts `PHASE_35_PROPOSAL.md`'s own explicit invariant ("Compute shader fallback is silent... Applications on GL 3.3 receive CPU blur/fragment-shader blur without error") — GL3 is meant to keep working, just via a different technique, not silently lose the feature. Fragment-shader ping-pong needs no new GL capability, reuses the exact FBO-render-to-texture machinery `NativeRendererGL3` already has no equivalent of yet but is straightforward to add, and is the literal answer Phase 35's own proposal text already gives for this backend — resolving it now instead of re-deriving the same answer later avoids redundant design work. | `NativeRendererGL3`/`GLFWOpenGL3Backend` keep targeting GL 3.3 core, unchanged. `BlurPassGL3` is a new, independent, backend-internal class — not yet wired into `NativeRendererGL3::Render()` (`DrawShadow`/backdrop blur consumption is Phase 34.4/34.6's job); Phase 34.3's scope is exclusively resolving the architectural question and delivering a tested, reusable blur primitive. |
| 2026-09-07 | A single fragment-shader program handles both the horizontal and vertical pass (selected via a `uDirection` uniform), computing Gaussian kernel weights inline per-fragment (`exp()`) rather than following `PHASE_34_PROPOSAL.md`'s literal "precomputed... UBO arrays" detail | Precompute weights for radii 1-64 into a UBO once, matching the proposal's literal text and the presumed design for the compute-shader backends Phase 35 will build | The UBO-precomputation detail solves a *compute-shader* concern (minimizing redundant `exp()` calls across a compute invocation's whole workgroup) that doesn't apply the same way to a fragment shader already paying a `texture()` sampling cost per tap — no measured performance problem motivated the added complexity of a second GL buffer object and its own upload/binding lifecycle, and this class's own doc comment records the reasoning so it isn't mistaken for an oversight when Phase 35's compute-shader backends are built (where the UBO approach may make more sense and can be added independently, without touching this class). | Radius is clamped to `[0, 64]` in C++ before upload (matching the proposal's stated supported range); `radius <= 0` is a no-op returning the source texture unchanged. The proposal's "larger radii use multiple passes at reduced resolution" mip-chain optimisation is explicitly not implemented — out of scope for this sub-phase, noted in `BlurPassGL3.hpp`'s own comment rather than silently absent. |
