| 2026-09-07 | `BlurPassGL3` blurs the source texture's raw RGBA linearly, with no premultiply/unmultiply step | Unmultiply alpha before blurring and re-premultiply after, the generally-correct approach for blurring arbitrary straight-alpha RGBA content (avoids color fringing at transparent boundaries) | Verified `NativeRendererGL3`'s own `DrawRect`/`DrawImage` fragment shaders already write a *premultiplied-by-coverage* result (`color = vFillColor * fillAlpha`, so RGB and alpha both go to zero together at a fully-transparent AA edge) — there is no unmultiplied-alpha color fringing to correct for in the specific case this class is used for (blurring `NativeRendererGL3`'s own rendered output), so the extra unmultiply/premultiply round-trip would be unneeded complexity for zero behavioural benefit here. Recorded explicitly in `BlurPassGL3.hpp`'s comment so this isn't mistaken for a missed case if this class is ever reused to blur a different, straight-alpha texture source. | Confirmed empirically by `Tests/Rendering/BlurPassGL3_test.cpp`'s impulse-response test: a single fully-opaque white pixel on a fully-transparent background blurs into a smooth falloff with no dark/light fringing at the zero-alpha boundary. |
| 2026-09-07 | Lives in `Backends/GLFWOpenGL3/` as a plain concrete class (no `IBlurPass` abstraction), gated on `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER` alone in both `Backends/GLFWOpenGL3/CMakeLists.txt` and `Tests/CMakeLists.txt` — the same placement and gating `NativeRendererGL3.cpp` itself already uses, and *not* the seam-interface pattern `ITextRenderer` uses for `TextRendererGL3` | Introduce an abstract `IBlurPass` interface now, anticipating Phase 35's other backends will each need their own blur implementation | `ITextRenderer` exists specifically because `TextRendererGL3` is gated behind a *second*, independent build flag (`IMF_BUILD_TEXT_MSDF`) that must not be implied by `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER` alone (Phase 33.7) — `BlurPassGL3` has no such second flag; it's unconditionally available whenever the native GL3 renderer is. Introducing an abstraction with exactly one implementation and no conditional-compilation reason for the split would be speculative — Phase 35's other backends (Vulkan/Metal/DX12/WebGPU) will each own their compute-shader-based blur pass directly inside their own backend directory, the same way `BlurPassGL3` does here; a shared interface can be introduced later if and when a real caller needs to hold a backend-agnostic blur handle, which nothing does yet. | Verified: rebuilt with `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER=ON`, full suite **511/511** (497 baseline + 7 pre-existing `NativeRendererGL3` tests + 3 new `BlurPassGL3` tests, all of which only ran because this was the first full-suite run this session with `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER` turned on), `check_api_leaks.sh` clean. `Tests/Rendering/BlurPassGL3_test.cpp`'s impulse-response test confirms real 2D Gaussian spread (peak dims, energy visibly reaches nearby texels, nothing reaches texels well outside the kernel radius) and a no-op fast path for `radius <= 0`. |

## Phase 34.4 — `DrawShadow` Rendering on `NativeRendererGL3`

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-10 | `Rendering::DrawShadow` gets its own `BatchKind::Shadow` in `BatchBuilder` (a new `ShadowVertex` record carrying the command's fields, always as a single-item batch that closes immediately) rather than staying `BatchFlushReason::NonBatchable` (silently dropped) the way it had been since Phase 32.3 | (a) Keep `DrawShadow` non-batchable in `BatchBuilder` and have `NativeRendererGL3::Render()` walk the raw `Rendering::CommandBuffer` directly instead of relying on `_batchBuilder.Batches()` for shadow commands; (b) add a bespoke non-`Batch`-shaped side channel (e.g. a separate `std::vector<Rendering::DrawShadow>` alongside `Batches()`) | (a) would mean `NativeRendererGL3::Render()` needs two different data flows — `_batchBuilder.Batches()` for everything else, plus a second pass over `buffer` for shadows — and get their relative draw order right by re-deriving it, when `BatchBuilder` already tracks encounter order for exactly this purpose. (b) loses the "batches preserve draw order, closed batches are a flat ordered list" invariant `NativeRendererGL3::DrawBatches()`'s single loop already relies on for every other kind. Reusing the existing `Batch`/`BatchVertices` shape (a `std::variant` of vectors) for a record that isn't literal GPU vertex data is a minor abuse of the type's name, but keeps one ordered list and one render loop instead of two. | `BatchFlushReason::NonBatchable` now only fires for `DrawPath` or a `DrawText` with no usable layout provider — its doc comment updated accordingly. `OpenVertexCount()`/`FlushOpen()`'s switches over `BatchKind` gained an (unreachable, but required for `-Wswitch` exhaustiveness) `Shadow` case, since `AppendShadow()` never calls `BeginBatch()` — it builds and appends its one-item batch directly. |
| 2026-09-10 | `NativeRendererGL3::RenderShadowBatch()` implements the shadow as: render a solid-white rounded-rect silhouette (reusing the existing `_rectProgram`/`RenderRectBatch()`) into a small offscreen texture padded by the blur radius on every side, blur it via `BlurPassGL3` (Phase 34.3), then composite the result — tinted by `ShadowColor`, at the shape's position plus `Offset` — back into the caller's framebuffer via the existing `_imageProgram`/`RenderImageBatch()`, with `Radii` all zero (no extra masking; the shape's rounding is already baked into the blurred silhouette's own alpha) | Write a third, shadow-specific shader pair (vertex+fragment) rather than reusing the existing Rect/Image programs | The silhouette pass needs exactly what `_rectProgram` already does (rounded-box SDF fill with AA) with `FillColor` forced to opaque white; the composite pass needs exactly what `_imageProgram` already does (sample a texture, multiply by a tint color) with `Radii` forced to zero. Building small helper functions (`BuildRectQuadVertices`/`BuildImageQuadVertices`) that assemble a one-off `Batch` and calling the existing `RenderRectBatch()`/`RenderImageBatch()` directly reuses two already-tested shaders and their entire upload/draw path, instead of introducing and maintaining a third GLSL pair (plus its own hand-written `#version 330 core` GL3 port, per this codebase's established `#version 450`-source/GL3-port duplication pattern) for logic that decomposes cleanly into two already-solved problems. | A future `NativeRendererGL3::Stats()` (mentioned in `BatchBuilder.hpp`'s own `BatchStats` doc comment) would see a `Shadow` batch's cost show up as two real draw calls (silhouette + composite) plus one `BlurPassGL3::Apply()` (itself two more draw calls) — four GPU draw calls per on-screen shadow, not one; worth knowing if/when per-frame draw-call budgets matter (Phase 35's cross-backend conformance work or a later perf pass). |
| 2026-09-10 | The silhouette's `Rendering::CornerRadii` are taken directly from `DrawShadow::Radii`, unchanged by `Spread` — CSS box-shadow semantics (which grow the corner radius by the spread amount) are not replicated | Grow each corner radius by `Spread` to match CSS `box-shadow`'s precise behavior | No test, proposal text, or existing consumer in this codebase specifies exact CSS-parity semantics for `Spread`+`Radii` interaction — `PHASE_34_PROPOSAL.md`'s own `DrawShadow` section only says spread/blur/offset/color, not a radius-growth formula. Implementing the simpler "radii unchanged" interpretation now, and documented here explicitly, avoids guessing at unspecified behavior; revisit if a real design need for exact CSS parity surfaces later. | `Widgets::` call sites that set both `Spread` and `Radii` on the same `DrawShadow` (none exist yet) will see slightly less-rounded corners on the spread-grown silhouette than literal CSS `box-shadow` would produce — a documented approximation, not a bug, should this come up in review later. |
| 2026-09-10 | Verified with a real-pixel test (`Tests/Rendering/NativeRendererGL3_test.cpp`): a `DrawShadow` behind an opaque `DrawRect` of the same position/size shows shadow color at a point inside the shadow's offset footprint but outside the rect's own edge, shows the rect's own color where the rect occludes it, and shows nothing at a point outside both — plus two `BatchBuilder`-level tests confirming `DrawShadow` produces its own always-singleton `Shadow` batch and never merges with a neighbouring shadow. Full suite **514/514**, `check_api_leaks.sh` clean. | Trust the BatchBuilder-level unit tests alone, without a real-GL-pixel-level test of the actual composited output | The BatchBuilder tests confirm the command reaches `NativeRendererGL3` correctly shaped, but say nothing about whether the silhouette-render-blur-composite pipeline actually produces a visually-correct shadow (right position, right color, right occlusion order) — that needs real GL rendering and pixel readback, matching every other real-rendering claim already verified this way in this codebase (`NativeRendererGL3_test.cpp`'s existing Rect/Image tests, `BlurPassGL3_test.cpp`'s impulse-response test). | `Tests/Rendering/NativeRendererGL3_test.cpp` gained one new `TEST_CASE`; `Tests/Rendering/BatchBuilder_test.cpp` gained two. No new CMake target needed — both files' existing targets already build/link everything required. |

## Phase 34.5 — Layer Compositing on `NativeRendererGL3`

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-10 | Expanded the public `Rendering::BlendMode` enum (`CommandBuffer.hpp`) from 4 values (`Normal`, `Multiply`, `Screen`, `Additive`) to the 12 standard CSS/PDF `mix-blend-mode` values `PHASE_34_PROPOSAL.md` actually lists (`Normal`, `Multiply`, `Screen`, `Overlay`, `Darken`, `Lighten`, `ColorDodge`, `ColorBurn`, `HardLight`, `SoftLight`, `Difference`, `Exclusion`), dropping `Additive` | Keep `Additive` alongside the 12 real modes; leave the enum as-is and implement only a subset | A `graphify` query confirmed `BlendMode` has exactly one reference anywhere in the tree (`PushBlendLayer::Mode`) with no producer yet — a breaking change here has zero real consumers to break. Matching the proposal's literal list, not an unlisted extra mode, avoids a permanent unexplained discrepancy between the enum and its own governing proposal. | Enumerator order is load-bearing — the blend fragment shader dispatches on `static_cast<int>(mode)` matching declaration order exactly (documented on the enum: "must not be reordered"). Phase 35's other backends inherit this same convention. |
| 2026-09-10 | `PushOpacityLayer`/`PushBlendLayer`/`PopLayer` each get their own `BatchKind::Layer` marker in `BatchBuilder` (a `LayerVertex` record with a `LayerOp` discriminator, always a single-item batch — the same shape `ShadowVertex` established in Phase 34.4) | Have `NativeRendererGL3::Render()` walk the raw `CommandBuffer` a second time, in parallel with `_batchBuilder.Batches()`, just for layer commands | Reusing the existing "flat, encounter-ordered `Batch` list, one render-loop switch" architecture needed exactly one new `switch` arm, not a second traversal to keep in sync. Unlike `Shadow`, a `Layer` marker can't render anything alone — it only marks where a *range* of subsequent batches redirects to an offscreen target — so the actual bookkeeping (`NativeRendererGL3::_layerStack`) is real new runtime state, not something the renderer-agnostic `BatchBuilder` needs to know about. | `OpenVertexCount()`/`FlushOpen()`'s exhaustive switches gained an (unreachable, `-Wswitch`-required) `Layer` case, identical in shape to `Shadow`'s. |
| 2026-09-10 | `PushLayer()` saves the current framebuffer/viewport into a `LayerFrame` pushed onto `_layerStack`, then binds a pooled, size-matched offscreen target (indexed by nesting depth, resized on demand) cleared to transparent; `PopLayer()` pops the frame, rebinds the saved parent framebuffer/viewport, then composites | Size each layer's offscreen target to a bounding rect of its actual content | `PushOpacityLayer`/`PushBlendLayer` carry no position/size field — a layer covers the *entire* current render target. Full-viewport sizing also makes every layer in a stack the same size, so `RenderRectBatch()`/`RenderImageBatch()`/`RenderShadowBatch()` need zero changes inside a layer — they already just draw into whatever framebuffer/viewport is current. | A well-formed buffer always pairs Push with Pop, leaving the framebuffer/viewport exactly where `DrawBatches()` started before its own trailing restore runs. A malformed buffer is caught via `IMF_ASSERT(_layerStack.empty())` at the end of `DrawBatches()` — a caller bug, not a runtime condition to recover from. |
| 2026-09-10 | `CompositeOpacityLayer()` uses `glBlendFunc(GL_ONE, GL_ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA)` — correct premultiplied-alpha "over" — instead of `DrawBatches()`'s own default `(GL_SRC_ALPHA, GL_ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA)` | Reuse the existing default blend func for consistency | This renderer's `Rect`/`Image` shaders already emit premultiplied output (`color = vFillColor * fillAlpha`). The non-premultiplied blend formula double-applies alpha (`src.a²` instead of `src.a`) — already a latent, imperceptible ~1px AA-edge inaccuracy elsewhere in this renderer (pre-existing, not fixed here), but a large, directly-observable error the moment a deliberate `Opacity < 1` is involved, which is this feature's entire point. Fixed only for this new path, not `DrawBatches()`'s general default, to keep blast radius to genuinely new code. | Verified exactly against `PHASE_34_PROPOSAL.md`'s own spec ("`PushOpacityLayer(0.5)` halves pixel alpha values") — an opaque red rect at `Opacity=0.5` composites to `(0.5,0,0,0.5)` premultiplied, matching hand-derived math. The pre-existing AA-edge inaccuracy elsewhere is now a documented, deliberately-deferred finding for a future rendering-accuracy pass. |
| 2026-09-10 | `CompositeBlendLayer()` copies the parent target's current content into `_backdropTex` via `glCopyTexSubImage2D` (the same technique Phase 34.6's backdrop blur will use), then draws a fullscreen quad with a two-texture shader (`_blendProgram`) that samples both textures, computes the selected mode's formula in GLSL, and writes the fully composited result with `GL_BLEND` disabled | Try to express blend modes via fixed-function `glBlendFunc`/`glBlendEquation` alone | A real blend mode (e.g. `Multiply(Cb,Cs)=Cb×Cs`) is a nonlinear function of *both* source and destination — fixed-function blending can only combine them through fixed linear factors or a fixed equation, neither of which expresses "multiply the two colors." A fragment shader can't read the framebuffer's own current pixel under GL 3.3 core (no `ARB_framebuffer_fetch`), so copy-to-texture is the only portable option. | One program handles all 12 modes via a `uMode` uniform (a GLSL `if`-chain), not 12 compiled variants — consistent with `BlurPassGL3`'s "one program, uniform-selected variant" precedent (Phase 34.3). |
| 2026-09-10 | Both textures the blend shader reads are unpremultiplied before applying each mode's classic CSS formula, then recomposited via the W3C "simple alpha compositing" formula (`resultRgb=(1-αb)·αs·Cs + αb·αs·Blend(Cb,Cs) + (1-αs)·αb·Cb`, `resultAlpha=αs+αb-αs·αb`) | Apply each blend formula directly to the (already premultiplied) texture samples | CSS blend formulas are defined for straight, 0-1 colors — applying them to premultiplied values would compound alpha's effect twice wherever alpha isn't 1. Confirmed numerically: `Multiply(0.8,0.5)=0.4` and `Screen(0.8,0.5)=0.9`, both matching the real-pixel tests' measured output exactly. | Guarded against divide-by-zero at α=0 (unpremultiplied color defaults to `vec3(0)`), matching the compositing formula's own terms, which already zero out a fully-transparent side's contribution independently. |
| 2026-09-10 | **Real bug found while writing this sub-phase's own tests:** `NativeRendererGL3.cpp`'s `BuildImageQuadVertices()` (introduced in Phase 34.4) had its UV table backwards specifically for sampling a texture *this renderer itself rendered* — `Rect`/`Image`'s vertex shader negates Y, so pixel-space Y=0 ends up at the texture's raw *bottom* row, the opposite of `texture()`'s default UV=(0,0)-reads-bottom-row convention. Phase 34.4's own shadow test never caught this because its content (a full, edge-to-edge, symmetric silhouette) looks identical flipped or not; Phase 34.5's off-center opacity-layer test does not, and failed with an exact zero-alpha result (the flipped position fell entirely outside the drawn rect) | Trust the shadow composite's passing test as sufficient prior verification of the shared helper | A "passes" result from a test whose content can't *reveal* a bug class (here: vertical symmetry hiding an axis flip) isn't proof the code is correct — the same lesson this session already learned for concurrency bugs (isolated verification can be misleadingly clean). Debugged via temporary instrumentation; the first debugging pass found a *different* bug in the instrumentation itself first (a probe FBO deleted while still bound reset `GL_FRAMEBUFFER_BINDING` to 0, corrupting the state being observed) — recognized and ruled out before the real UV-flip root cause underneath it. | `BuildImageQuadVertices()`'s UV table is now `{0,1},{1,1},{1,0},{0,0}` (was `{0,0},{1,0},{1,1},{0,1}`), commented so it isn't "corrected" back by a future reader. Re-verified the existing shadow test still passes unchanged (expected, given its symmetry) — no regression. `AppendImage()`'s own UV handling for real `DrawImage` commands is untouched, never affected. Backdrop blur (Phase 34.6) will also composite a renderer-produced texture — worth checking it reuses this same (now-fixed) helper rather than re-deriving the same bug. |
| 2026-09-10 | Verified: real-pixel tests for `PushOpacityLayer` (exact premultiplied-alpha halving) and both `PushBlendLayer(Multiply)`/`PushBlendLayer(Screen)` (exact numeric results, proving `uMode` genuinely dispatches), plus two `BatchBuilder`-level marker tests. Full suite **519/519**, `check_api_leaks.sh` clean. | Accept approximate/threshold-only pixel checks | This sub-phase's core deliverable is compositing *math* — a test only checking "the pixel changed" wouldn't have caught the double-alpha-attenuation bug, nor proven the blend shader's dispatch is real rather than one mode being coincidentally hardcoded. | `NativeRendererGL3_test.cpp` gained three `TEST_CASE`s; `BatchBuilder_test.cpp` gained two. No new CMake target needed. |

## Phase 34.6 — Backdrop Blur on `NativeRendererGL3` (Phase 34 complete)

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-11 | Added a new public command, `Rendering::DrawBackdropBlur` (`Position`/`Size`/`Radii`/`BlurRadius`/`TintColor`), gaining its own `BatchKind::BackdropBlur` marker in `BatchBuilder` (a `BackdropBlurVertex` record, single-item batch — the same shape `ShadowVertex` established) | Model it as a `Push`/`Pop` bracket pair like `PushOpacityLayer`, since both are "offscreen-target" effects | Unlike a layer, backdrop blur doesn't bracket a *range* of subsequent commands — it samples whatever's *already* rendered up to that point and replaces that one region in place; a widget's own content (background, text, icons) is expected to draw *after* it, on top. This is structurally identical to `DrawShadow` (a single, self-contained operation), not to `PushOpacityLayer`/`PushBlendLayer` (which need a matching `Pop` and runtime nesting state) — see `BatchBuilder.hpp`'s updated file comment. | `NativeRendererGL3::RenderBackdropBlurBatch()` needs no layer-stack-style bookkeeping — one method, no `Push`/`Pop` pairing to get wrong. |
| 2026-09-11 | `RenderBackdropBlurBatch()` copies a *padded* region (padded by `BlurRadius`, clamped to the current viewport's bounds) behind the requested rect via `glCopyTexSubImage2D`, blurs it with `_blurPass` (Phase 34.3), then composites back *only* the unpadded, requested `Position`/`Size` — cropped out of the padded blurred copy via `BuildImageQuadVertices()`'s new `uvMin`/`uvMax` sub-rect parameters (generalized from Phase 34.4/34.5's always-whole-texture default) | Composite the whole padded region (like `RenderShadowBatch()` does), letting the excess bleed and rely on the widget's own subsequent content to cover it | `PHASE_34_PROPOSAL.md`'s Backdrop Blur section describes it as replacing *the requested region*, not painting a halo around it the way a drop shadow legitimately should — a shadow is expected to extend past its shape's own bounds, backdrop blur (frosted glass) is not. Cropping is the behavior an actual widget author would expect: "blur exactly this rect," full stop. | `BuildImageQuadVertices()` gained `uvMin`/`uvMax` (default `{0,0}`/`{1,1}`, Phase 34.4/34.5's unchanged whole-texture behaviour) and `radii` (default all-zero, also unchanged) parameters — `Rendering::DrawBackdropBlur::Radii` now actually rounds the composited result, unlike `DrawShadow`'s composite (always unrounded; its own rounding is already baked into the blurred silhouette's alpha). |
| 2026-09-11 | **Real bug found while generalizing `BuildImageQuadVertices()` for cropping:** the new `uvMin`/`uvMax` V-flip was implemented as a min/max *swap* (`{uvMin.x,uvMax.y},{uvMax.x,uvMax.y},{uvMax.x,uvMin.y},{uvMin.x,uvMin.y}`), copied directly from the Phase 34.5 fix's own table — but that table's specific values only *coincidentally* implement the correct flip because the Phase 34.4/34.5 default case has `uvMin=(0,0)`/`uvMax=(1,1)`, where swapping min/max happens to equal the real formula (`1 - v`). For a genuine non-default sub-rect (this phase's crop), the two differ, and the swap produced a subtly-wrong (but non-crashing, non-empty) UV mapping | Trust the Phase 34.5 table's exact shape and generalize it mechanically without re-deriving why it worked | Caught empirically: the seam-blend test showed *zero* blend at the seam — worked through the real per-fragment math by hand (author-local `v=0.5` should sample UV.y=0.5 at the exact seam) and found the swap-based table gives a different, wrong UV.y for a non-default sub-rect. Re-derived from first principles (`v_tex(v_author) = 1 - v_author`'s two endpoints, evaluated separately at `uvMin.y` and `uvMax.y`, not swapped) and confirmed the general formula reduces to the exact Phase 34.5 table when `uvMin=(0,0)`/`uvMax=(1,1)`, so this is a correction, not a reversal, of that fix. | `BuildImageQuadVertices()`'s `uvs[]` table is now `{uvMin.x, 1-uvMin.y}, {uvMax.x, 1-uvMin.y}, {uvMax.x, 1-uvMax.y}, {uvMin.x, 1-uvMax.y}` — commented with the general derivation so a future non-default use doesn't reintroduce the swap mistake. |
| 2026-09-11 | **A second, more significant real bug found via the same debugging session — this one dating to Phase 34.3, not new code:** `BlurPassGL3::Apply()` captured "the caller's previous framebuffer" (to restore before returning) via `glGetIntegerv(GL_FRAMEBUFFER_BINDING, ...)` positioned *after* calling `EnsureInitialized()`/`EnsureTargets()` — but `EnsureTargets()`'s `CreateTarget()` binds each newly-created FBO and never rebinds away from it. On the *first* `Apply()` call on a given instance (or any call whose `width`/`height` differs from the last, forcing reallocation), this means `Apply()` captured one of its *own* just-created ping-pong FBOs as "the caller's previous framebuffer," then dutifully "restored" the caller to that internal, unrelated FBO when returning — silently corrupting the caller's own binding | Leave it, since `RenderShadowBatch()` (Phase 34.4) already works correctly in practice | It only "works" for `RenderShadowBatch()` because that method independently re-binds its *own* saved framebuffer/viewport immediately after calling `_blurPass.Apply()` (needed anyway, to move on to the composite draw) — a redundant rebind that happens to paper over this bug completely, so it shipped undetected through all of Phase 34.3/34.4's extensive verification. `RenderBackdropBlurBatch()` has no equivalent redundant rebind (nothing else in it needs one), so it inherited the corrupted framebuffer directly — the composite then drew into the wrong target entirely, which is what actually produced the "zero blend" symptom investigated above, not the UV-table bug (both were present simultaneously; the UV fix was necessary but not sufficient on its own, confirmed by re-testing after each fix independently). | Fixed by moving the entire "save previous state" block in `BlurPassGL3::Apply()` to *before* `EnsureInitialized()`/`EnsureTargets()` run. Verified: the exact same seam-blend and rate-limit tests that exposed both bugs now pass with correct, hand-derived pixel values; `RenderShadowBatch()`'s own existing tests still pass unchanged (its redundant rebind made it correct before and remains simply redundant-but-harmless now, not deleted since removing it isn't this phase's job). This is the second time this session that a Phase 34.4/34.5 test's "passing" result concealed a real bug reachable only by a caller that doesn't happen to redundantly compensate for it — worth remembering when trusting an existing test's green result as proof a shared helper is fully correct for a *new* caller's usage pattern. |
| 2026-09-11 | `SetMaxBackdropBlurPerFrame(int)` (default 4, per `PHASE_34_PROPOSAL.md`) with a per-`Render()`-call counter (`_backdropBlurCountThisFrame`, reset at the top of `Render()`) — "per frame" is defined as "per `Render()` call" for this renderer, matching how this renderer has no other concept of frame boundaries of its own | Track a wall-clock or external frame-counter concept of "frame" | `NativeRendererGL3` has no existing notion of "frame" beyond "one `Render()` call renders one `Rendering::CommandBuffer`, once" — inventing a separate frame-boundary concept just for this one feature would add a new stateful concern (would `Render()` calls ever legitimately happen more than once per real frame? Not today) for no present benefit. | Additional `DrawBackdropBlur` commands beyond the limit degrade gracefully (`IMF_DEBUG`-logged, region left showing its already-rendered unblurred content) — verified with a dedicated test: 5 non-overlapping backdrop-blur regions straddling the same colour seam, the first 4 show measurable blur bleed, the 5th shows the exact original unblurred pixel values. |
| 2026-09-11 | Verified: a colour-seam test (asymmetric, Y-orientation-sensitive content — learned from the Phase 34.5 UV-flip incident not to trust vertically-symmetric test content) confirms genuine blur at the seam, an unblurred pixel just outside the requested rect but inside the padded copy region (proving correct cropping), and untouched pixels far away; a rate-limit test confirms the default limit of 4 and graceful degradation past it. Full suite **522/522**, `check_api_leaks.sh` clean. This closes out Phase 34 (Effects Pipeline) in full — `PHASE_34_PROPOSAL.md`'s entire literal scope (SDF rects, Gaussian blur, box shadow, layer compositing, backdrop blur) is now implemented and tested on `NativeRendererGL3`. | Reuse the Phase 34.4/34.5 test content patterns (symmetric fills, single-color backgrounds) for speed | Both real bugs found this sub-phase were only reachable through content/call patterns the *existing* passing tests didn't exercise — deliberately designing this phase's own tests to violate the same assumptions (asymmetric content, a genuinely non-default UV crop, a fresh `BlurPassGL3` instance's first-ever `Apply()` call) is what surfaced them before Phase 35 would have inherited them silently. | Phase 35 (all five backend renderers) begins next — `NativeRendererGL3` is now feature-complete per `PHASE_34_PROPOSAL.md`, giving Phase 35's cross-backend conformance suite a real, fully-implemented reference to compare Vulkan/Metal/DX12/WebGPU against for every effect, not just SDF rects. |

## Phase 35.1 — `NativeRendererVulkan`: First SDF Rect via a Real Vulkan Pipeline

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-12 | Vulkan is the first of the four remaining backends (Vulkan/Metal/DX12/WebGPU) to get a `NativeRenderer` — user's own explicit choice, offered alongside DX12 and WebGPU | Start with DX12 (Windows-native, no SDK dependency) or WebGPU (already has the most existing test infra) | Metal is macOS-only and cannot be built or tested from this Windows session at all, ruling it out entirely for now. Vulkan is cross-platform, buildable/testable here, and `PHASE_35_PROPOSAL.md`'s own compute-shader description for it (`vkCmdDispatch`, transient `VkImage` via VMA) is the most fully-specified of the four — a reasonable reference implementation for DX12/WebGPU's own later sub-phases to follow. | Phase 35.2+ (Vulkan compute blur, batching, text, effects) continues on this same backend before DX12/WebGPU get their own first sub-phase — matches how GL3 itself was built to near-completion (Phases 32-34) before Phase 35 even started touching a second backend. |
| 2026-09-12 | Phase 35.1's scope is `BatchKind::Rect` only, via a real Vulkan 1.3 graphics pipeline (dynamic rendering, no `VkRenderPass`/`VkFramebuffer`) rendering into a caller-provided offscreen `VkImageView` — no batching-kind interleaving, no text, no effects, no live `SDL3VulkanBackend` frame-loop integration | Integrate `NativeRendererVulkan` into the real backend's per-frame command buffer and swap chain from the start, matching how a "complete" implementation would eventually look | Mirrors `NativeRendererGL3`'s own actual starting scope exactly (Phase 32.4) — prove the shader/pipeline/buffer mechanics work correctly in isolation first, in an environment with no other moving parts, before layering in the harder, still-unresolved architectural question of "how does `IRenderer::Render(CommandBuffer)`'s fixed, backend-agnostic signature interact with a *specific* Vulkan command buffer/frame-in-flight index" — a question worth answering deliberately in its own later sub-phase, not rushed as a side effect of getting the first triangle on screen. | `NativeRendererVulkan::Render()` currently allocates, records, submits, and *synchronously waits on* (`vkWaitForFences`) its own one-shot command buffer every call — a deliberate, documented stopgap identical in spirit to `ViewportFramebufferVulkan::EndRender()`'s own already-accepted `vkQueueWaitIdle()` CPU stall (that file's own comment: "Phase 24.x: replace... enabling true GPU-pipeline parallelism"). Replacing this with real per-frame semaphore sync is deferred to whichever later 35.x sub-phase actually wires this renderer into a live frame loop. |
| 2026-09-12 | `NativeRendererVulkan` is constructed with explicit, already-typed Vulkan handles (`VkDevice`, `VmaAllocator`, `VkQueue`, queue family index, `VkCommandPool`) via a new backend-internal `SDL3VulkanBackend::GetRendererHandles()` accessor — not routed through the existing public `Rendering::VulkanContext` (`include/ImFrame/Backends/BackendInfo.hpp`) | Add a `VmaAllocator` field to the public `VulkanContext` struct and construct through that instead | `VulkanContext` exists specifically for Phase 24 Viewport's *application-facing* use (an external embedder's own custom Vulkan rendering into an ImFrame-managed viewport) — a different consumer with different needs, deliberately `void*`-typed to avoid leaking Vulkan headers into the public API. `NativeRendererVulkan` is exactly as backend-internal as `NativeRendererGL3` itself (which needs no public context object at all — GL has an implicit "current context" Vulkan simply has no equivalent of); growing a public, application-facing struct with a field that's really just this one internal renderer's own implementation choice (a *different* Vulkan-based renderer might not use VMA at all) would conflate the two concerns. `GetRendererHandles()` returns real Vulkan types directly rather than `void*`, since `Backends/SDL3Vulkan/` is itself an internal-only header directory already, with no API-leak concern. | `SDL3VulkanBackend.hpp` gains one new small `struct VulkanRendererHandles` and accessor method — used by this sub-phase's own test today, and available to whichever later sub-phase wires `NativeRendererVulkan` into the backend's real frame loop. |
| 2026-09-12 | Consumes `Shaders/SDFRect.glsl`'s pre-compiled SPIR-V directly (`ImFrame::Internal::Shaders::kSDFRectVertexSpirv`/`kSDFRectFragmentSpirv`, embedded by Phase 32.2's `cmake/CompileShader.cmake` pipeline) via `vkCreateShaderModule` — the first real consumer of that pipeline's SPIR-V half; `NativeRendererGL3` only ever used the same generated headers' raw-GLSL-source half, hand-writing its own `#version 330` equivalents instead | Hand-write a separate Vulkan-specific GLSL source and compile it independently | Phase 32.2 built exactly this cross-compilation pipeline for exactly this future moment (its own file comment: "for Vulkan/WebGPU/DX12/Metal backends, once a later sub-phase feeds it to a real graphics API") — reusing it needed zero new build infrastructure, just linking the already-produced `ImFrame_Shaders` INTERFACE target (previously linked by nothing) and `#include`-ing the generated header. `RectVertex` (`BatchBuilder.hpp`) already matches `SDFRect.glsl`'s vertex inputs field-for-field (it was designed to), so the same CPU-side struct feeds the Vulkan vertex buffer via `offsetof()`-derived `VkVertexInputAttributeDescription`s with no new vertex type needed either. | `CMakeLists.txt`'s Phase 32.2 shader-pipeline comment (previously "No target links against ImFrame_Shaders yet") is now stale-corrected. `Backends/SDL3Vulkan/CMakeLists.txt` gained the same `IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER`-gated `target_sources()`/`target_link_libraries(... ImFrame_Shaders)` shape `Backends/GLFWOpenGL3/CMakeLists.txt` already established for `NativeRendererGL3.cpp`. |
| 2026-09-12 | Verified with a real-pixel test harness (`ScratchImage`, mirroring `NativeRendererGL3_test.cpp`'s own `ScratchFramebuffer`): a hand-rolled offscreen `COLOR_ATTACHMENT | TRANSFER_SRC` `VkImage`, transitioned/cleared up front, read back via a one-shot command buffer copying into a mapped staging buffer. Two tests ported directly from `NativeRendererGL3_test.cpp`'s own first two (`DrawRect` fill-position, rounded-corner masking) — both passed with exact expected pixel values on the first run (no Y-flip, barrier, or descriptor-set bugs, unlike Phase 34.5/34.6's own debugging detours). Full suite **524/524**, `check_api_leaks.sh` clean. | Trust the pipeline mechanics without a real-pixel test, given how carefully the barrier/layout logic was derived from `ViewportVulkan.cpp`'s own already-working precedent | This session has twice now found real bugs specifically because a "should be correct by inspection" GPU resource-state assumption wasn't checked against actual rendered output (Phase 34.5's UV-flip, Phase 34.6's `BlurPassGL3::Apply()` framebuffer-restore ordering) — there was no reason to expect a brand-new graphics API's first use to be exempt from that same risk, so it was verified the same way regardless of how carefully the code was derived from working precedent. | `Tests/Rendering/NativeRendererVulkan_test.cpp` is a new test target (`ImFrame_Tests_Rendering_NativeRendererVulkan`), gated on `IMF_BACKEND_SDL3_VULKAN AND IMF_BUILD_NATIVE_RENDERER`, `[vulkan]`-labelled with the same `TIMEOUT 60` used by the existing Vulkan backend tests (guards against a hang if no Vulkan-capable GPU/driver is present in CI). |

## Phase 35.2 — `NativeRendererVulkan`: `BatchKind::Image`

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-13 | `Rendering::TextureId::Value()` is reinterpreted as a raw `VkImageView` handle, sampled through one renderer-owned shared `VkSampler` (linear filter, clamp-to-edge) | A small internal texture registry mapping `TextureId` to `{VkImageView, VkSampler}` pairs, allowing per-texture sampler parameters | Matches `NativeRendererGL3`'s own "raw handle, reinterpreted directly, no registry indirection" convention for its own GL texture type exactly — no `DrawImage` command field exists for per-texture sampling parameters today, so a single shared sampler costs nothing. A `VkImageView` alone isn't self-contained the way a GL texture name is (Vulkan's descriptor model needs an explicit combined-image-sampler binding naming both), which is why a registry-free approach still needs *some* renderer-owned state (the shared sampler), unlike GL3's fully stateless equivalent. | Any later per-`DrawImage` sampling-parameter field (e.g. nearest-filter, repeat-wrap) would require a real texture registry at that point — not needed by anything in `PHASE_35_PROPOSAL.md` today. |
| 2026-09-13 | One fresh descriptor set is allocated and written per `Image` batch, from a pool reset every `Render()` call (`EnsureImageDescriptorCapacity()`), rather than reusing a single stable Image descriptor set the way `_rectDescriptorSet` is reused across all `Rect` batches | Reuse one Image descriptor set across all `Image` batches in a frame, rewriting its binding-1 image/sampler before each batch's draw call is recorded | A descriptor set's *content* is resolved by the GPU at command-buffer *execution* time, not at `vkCmdBindDescriptorSets` *recording* time. `Render()` records every batch into one command buffer before a single combined submission — reusing one set across multiple different-textured `Image` batches would make every draw in that submission sample whichever texture was written *last*, once the GPU actually executes them. This was reasoned through *before* implementation (unlike the two bugs below, found only through testing) and directly validated by a dedicated test (`DrawRect` + two differently-textured `DrawImage` batches in one frame, each sampling its own correct texture). | The pool-reset-per-`Render()` pattern is the template for any future per-batch-varying-resource need (e.g. per-batch push constants) in this or other Vulkan renderers. |
| 2026-09-13 | **Real, pre-existing bug found while debugging Phase 35.2's own test failures — dating to Phase 35.1, not new code:** `SDFRect.glsl` and `Image.glsl`'s shared vertex-shader formula, `gl_Position = vec4(ndc.x, -ndc.y, 0.0, 1.0)`, negates Y — copied verbatim from `NativeRendererGL3`'s own equivalent GLSL, where negation is *correct* (GL's NDC is bottom-up; this codebase's pixel-space convention is top-down, so GL3 must flip). Vulkan's NDC is natively top-down already (`y=-1` is the viewport's top with a standard positive-height viewport, no flip trick applied anywhere in `NativeRendererVulkan`) — negating a second time introduces a real, wrong reflection of every Y coordinate about the target's vertical center. | Leave it, since Phase 35.1's own `DrawRect` tests pass | Every Phase 35.1 test happens to be flip-*symmetric*: two rects both spanning the full render-target height, and a rounded-corner rect covering the whole target — a Y-reflection about the center is undetectable in both cases. Phase 35.2's own combined test (`DrawRect` + two `DrawImage` batches, all 32×32, at distinct non-center positions) was the first test in this renderer to use small, off-center, non-full-height geometry, and it immediately exposed the flip: found by isolating a "renders nothing" symptom down to correct GPU submission + correct vertex-buffer content, then dumping a coarse pixel grid across the whole target and recognizing each shape's observed position as `TargetHeight - Size - OriginalY` (a reflection), which matches doubling an already-correct single flip. | Fixed by removing the negation in both `SDFRect.glsl` and `Image.glsl` (`gl_Position = vec4(ndc.x, ndc.y, 0.0, 1.0)`), each now commented explaining why Vulkan's own shader must NOT copy GL3's flip. This is the *fourth* time this session a real bug was masked by test geometry that happened to be flip/symmetry-invariant (see Phase 34.5's UV-flip and Phase 34.6's `BlurPassGL3::Apply()` entries for the first two) — any future renderer's first non-symmetric test is worth treating as a real correctness check, not a formality. |
| 2026-09-13 | **Second, unrelated bug found via the validation layer during the same debugging session:** `Tests/Rendering/NativeRendererVulkan_test.cpp`'s `ScratchImage` (the hand-rolled offscreen render target) was created with usage `COLOR_ATTACHMENT_BIT \| TRANSFER_SRC_BIT` only, but its own `TransitionAndClear()` transitions it to `TRANSFER_DST_OPTIMAL` and calls `vkCmdClearColorImage` on it — both undefined behaviour per the Vulkan spec without `VK_IMAGE_USAGE_TRANSFER_DST_BIT`. This only surfaced once `VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation` was actually active: the project's Debug config enables it, but the debugging session had been running the Release build (where `#if !defined(NDEBUG)` in `SDL3VulkanBackend::CreateInstance()` skips requesting the layer entirely), silently masking a real, spec-violating pre-existing bug in the test helper. | Leave the Release-only debugging in place and keep guessing at the Vulkan-Y-flip bug from pixel output alone | Switching to a Debug build specifically to get validation-layer diagnostics is what surfaced this issue in the first place; it turned out to be a real but separate bug (masked in Phase 35.1 because the invalid clear "worked" by luck on this driver) rather than the actual root cause of the failing tests, which was the Y-flip above — fixing it did not fix the tests on its own, confirming the two were independent. | Fixed by adding `VK_IMAGE_USAGE_TRANSFER_DST_BIT` to `ScratchImage`'s image creation usage flags. Worth remembering for any future Vulkan debugging on this project: build and run the `Debug` config (not `Release`) to get real validation-layer diagnostics — `NDEBUG` silently disables the request for `VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation` entirely, so a Release-only debugging session gets none of its error/warning output. |
| 2026-09-13 | `RenderRectBatch()` now rebinds `_rectPipeline`/`_rectDescriptorSet` unconditionally at the top of every call (previously bound once before the batch loop in `Render()`) | Leave the single bind-before-loop in `Render()`, since Phase 35.1 only ever had one `BatchKind` to draw | Phase 35.2 interleaves `Rect` and `Image` batches within one command buffer; each batch must rebind its own pipeline/descriptor set regardless of what the previous batch (of a possibly different kind) left bound. Matches `NativeRendererGL3::RenderRectBatch()`'s own identical unconditional-rebind convention. | Any future `BatchKind` added to this renderer should follow the same per-batch unconditional-rebind pattern rather than assuming a particular bind persists from `Render()`'s own setup. |
| 2026-09-13 | Verified: full suite **526/526** (524 baseline + this phase's 2 new tests), `check_api_leaks.sh` clean, both Debug (validation-layer-clean, zero warnings/errors) and Release configs rebuilt and passing | Verify only in Release, matching the config the rest of this session's Vulkan work had used | The Y-flip bug above was found specifically *because* a Debug validation-layer run was attempted for an unrelated reason — re-verifying the final fix in both configs confirms the fix holds independent of `NDEBUG`, and that no validation warnings/errors remain now that both real bugs are fixed. | Phase 35.2 (Vulkan Image batching) is complete. Phase 35's remaining Vulkan work (compute-shader Gaussian blur, then shadow/layers/backdrop-blur mirroring GL3's Phase 34 scope, then real `SDL3VulkanBackend` frame-loop integration) continues next, now with a renderer whose Y-axis handling is confirmed correct for genuinely asymmetric geometry — worth deliberately testing with off-center, non-full-coverage shapes from the start of each future sub-phase rather than only at the end. |

## Public Repo Hardening — `.claude/` History Purge (2026-09-13)

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-13 | **Incident:** the user discovered `.claude/` (`CLAUDE.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `PHASE_STATUS.md`, `QUICK_REF.md`, `SCRATCH.md`, skill files) was tracked and pushed to the public GitHub remote (`github.com/voidptr-cxx/ImFrame`), across `develop` and most `feature/phase-*` branches. Investigated before acting: no real secrets/credentials found anywhere in tracked history (`git grep` for API-key/token/private-key patterns, and a full-history filename scan for `.env`/`.pem`/`.key`/credential-style names, both came back empty) — the actual exposure was a local Windows username and personal folder path (`C:\Users\<name>\OneDrive\Project Ideas\ImFrame\`) repeated in `CLAUDE.md`/`QUICK_REF.md` across roughly 200 commits and 29 branches (26 already-merged local branches, plus 3 additional branches — `phase-14-table-propertygrid`, `phase-26-viewport3d`, `phase-27-widget-tree-core` — that existed only on `origin`, never as local branches, and were missed by the first rewrite pass until cross-checked against `gh api repos/.../branches`) | Untrack `.claude/` going forward only, leaving past commits as-is | The user explicitly asked for a full history purge, not just stopping future leakage — past commits on a public remote remain browsable by anyone regardless of what future commits do. | Any future `.gitignore`d path found tracked on this repo should be treated as a "verify full history + all branches, including origin-only ones, before declaring it fixed" incident, not a simple `git rm --cached`. |
| 2026-09-13 | Rewrote all history with `git-filter-repo --path .claude --invert-paths --force`, run on a disposable clean-room clone (never on the working repo directly, per the tool's own documented safety guidance), after first taking two independent backups: a `git clone --mirror` snapshot of every ref pre-rewrite, and a plain file copy of `.claude/`'s contents (since the rewrite necessarily removes those files from the working tree of any branch reset to the new history) | `bfg-repo-cleaner` (older, Java-based, less actively maintained); manual `git filter-branch` (officially deprecated, much slower, easy to get subtly wrong) | `git-filter-repo` is the tool git's own documentation recommends over `filter-branch`; running it on a throwaway clone rather than the live working repo means a mistake costs nothing — the original is untouched until a rewrite is verified and force-pushed deliberately. The mirror backup is the actual rollback path if verification had failed; the `.claude/` file copy is what restores local Claude Code session continuity (`PHASE_STATUS.md` etc.) once the rewritten history is adopted locally. | Both backups (`ImFrame-FULL-BACKUP-<timestamp>.git`, `ImFrame-claude-backup-<timestamp>/`) were deliberately left in place alongside the repo, not deleted automatically — kept until the user confirms they're no longer needed. |
| 2026-09-13 | Force-pushed the rewritten history to every branch that existed pre-rewrite (`git push origin --force --all`), then deleted every branch confirmed merged into `develop` (`git branch --merged develop`) — all 29 non-`develop` branches qualified, none had unmerged content, leaving `develop` as the sole remaining branch on `origin` | Force-push only `develop` (the branch actually being worked on) and leave the old feature branches with their un-rewritten history in place | Leaving any branch un-rewritten defeats the purpose entirely — a leaked path is exactly as exposed via one branch as via thirty. Since every branch was already fully merged into `develop` (verified via `--is-ancestor` before deleting, not assumed), deleting them loses no unique history; `develop` alone is a complete record. | A bulk `git push origin --delete <25 branches>` was blocked by this session's own auto-mode safety classifier (bulk destructive remote operation) — the user was shown the exact branch list and re-confirmed via a follow-up question before the deletion was retried and completed. Worth remembering: destructive multi-target remote operations may need an explicit, itemized confirmation step even after a general "yes, do the purge" approval. |
| 2026-09-13 | Verified the live GitHub remote directly via a **fresh, independent** `git clone --bare` (not by re-checking any of the local working/rewrite clones) before declaring the purge complete — confirmed exactly one branch (`develop`), 152 commits, zero hits for `.claude/` anywhere in history | Trust the local rewrite clone's own post-filter-repo state as sufficient proof | A local clone can be correct while the actual push silently failed, partially succeeded, or the remote still serves cached/dangling refs — the only trustworthy check for "is GitHub actually clean now" is a clean-room clone from GitHub itself, done last, after every push and branch deletion. | Establishes the verification pattern referenced in `CLAUDE.md`'s new "Public Repo / Git Hygiene Rules" section (rule 5) for any future history rewrite on this repo. |
| 2026-09-13 | `.claude/` is now `.gitignore`d in full (previously only `.claude/settings.json` was) and will never be re-tracked; the local copy was restored from the pre-rewrite backup so Claude Code's own session-continuity workflow (Session Boot Sequence reading `PHASE_STATUS.md`/`DECISIONS.md`/`SCRATCH.md`) continues to work locally, just without those files being versioned/shared via GitHub. `CLAUDE.md` and `QUICK_REF.md`'s personal-path references were replaced with generic descriptions, and `CLAUDE.md` gained a new "Public Repo / Git Hygiene Rules" section | Keep a sanitized, public-safe subset of `.claude/` tracked (e.g. `DECISIONS.md`/`PHASE_STATUS.md` alone, since those two specifically were not found to contain personal paths) | The user asked to untrack "that path" (the whole folder) and confirmed treating this as an open-source project going forward with new rules rather than a private-repo reversal; a single "never track `.claude/`, full stop" rule is far less likely to regress than a curated per-file allowlist that has to be re-justified every time a new file is added under `.claude/`. | Anyone else working on this repo (now genuinely open-source) will not see phase-decision history via GitHub — if that history should eventually be made public, it should go into a deliberately-authored, separately-reviewed document (e.g. a public `CHANGELOG.md`/`ROADMAP.md`), not by re-tracking `.claude/` itself. |
| 2026-09-13 | **Superseded the entry directly above, same day:** after independently verifying no clones/forks of the pre-purge history exist, the user asked to re-track `.claude/`'s actual project-documentation files after all — "it contains phases progress detail, let track it back with restriction." Re-tracked `CLAUDE.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `PHASE_STATUS.md`, `QUICK_REF.md`, `SCRATCH.md`, and `skills/*.md` (re-scanned clean of personal paths first); `.gitignore`'s `.claude/` blanket rule was narrowed to name `settings.json`/`settings.local.json` specifically instead — the latter is what actually leaked the path last time (`settings.local.json` is the true per-developer override file; `settings.json` is shared hook config, also kept local by this project's existing convention) | Keep the whole-folder `.gitignore` rule and accept losing versioned phase history on GitHub going forward | The blanket rule was the right call *before* the user had verified exposure was contained; once confirmed, the original motivation for tracking `.claude/`'s docs (this project's own session-continuity workflow depends on `DECISIONS.md`/`PHASE_STATUS.md` being real, versioned history) outweighs a now-unnecessarily broad restriction. | Before adding any *new* file under `.claude/` to git going forward, re-scan it for absolute local paths first — this is now written into both `.gitignore`'s own comment and `CLAUDE.md`'s "Public Repo / Git Hygiene Rules" rule 2. |

## Phase 35.3 — Vulkan Gaussian Blur (`BlurPassVulkan`)

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-13 | `BlurPassVulkan` mirrors `BlurPassGL3`'s exact Gaussian weight formula (`sigma = max(radius * 0.5, 1e-4)`, `weight = exp(-i*i / (2*sigma*sigma))`, normalized by the running weight sum) computed inline in `GaussianBlur.glsl`'s compute shader, rather than precomputing weights into a UBO array the way `PHASE_34_PROPOSAL.md`'s original compute-shader section describes | Precompute weights CPU-side per distinct radius and upload as a UBO array, per the original proposal | `BlurPassGL3.hpp`'s own file comment already made and documented this exact tradeoff for the fragment-shader path — no measured performance problem motivates the added UBO-array machinery, and per-invocation `exp()` is trivial on modern GPUs. Using the identical formula on both backends also keeps GL3's and Vulkan's blur output visually consistent, which a UBO-array reimplementation with potentially different precision/rounding would risk breaking. | Any future backend's own Gaussian blur (Metal/DX12/WebGPU) should default to this same inline formula for the same reasons, unless a real, measured performance problem specifically motivates the UBO-array approach for that backend. |
| 2026-09-13 | One compute pipeline handles both the horizontal and vertical pass, direction selected via a `vec2 Direction` + `float Radius` push-constant block (12 bytes, no padding) rather than two separate compiled shader variants or a per-pass descriptor rewrite | Two separate compute shaders/pipelines (`GaussianBlurH.comp`/`GaussianBlurV.comp`, as literally named in `PHASE_34_PROPOSAL.md`'s file tree) | Mirrors `BlurPassGL3`'s own single-program, `uDirection`-uniform design exactly (see that file's header comment) — the two passes are identical except which axis they sample along, so a second pipeline would be pure duplication. Push constants (not a UBO) because the payload is 3 floats, and push constants avoid needing any descriptor write at all for this per-dispatch, per-call-varying data. | `GaussianBlur.glsl` is one file with a single `STAGE_COMPUTE` section (not two source files) — `cmake/CompileShader.cmake` was extended to accept a `"comp"` stage (previously only `"vert"`/`"frag"`) for exactly this shape of shader. |
| 2026-09-13 | `Apply()`'s return type is a new `BlurResult{VkImage; VkImageView;}` struct, not a bare `VkImageView` (which would have been the more literal mirror of `BlurPassGL3::Apply()`'s single `unsigned int` GL texture name) | Return only a `VkImageView`, matching `NativeRendererVulkan`'s own existing "`TextureId` is a raw `VkImageView`" convention | No Vulkan call recovers a `VkImage` from a `VkImageView` — a caller needing to read back, barrier, or copy the blurred result (every test in `BlurPassVulkan_test.cpp` needs exactly this) would otherwise have no way to get the image handle a `vkCmdCopyImageToBuffer`/`vkCmdPipelineBarrier` requires. GL's single texture name already serves both roles at once, which is *why* `BlurPassGL3`'s API could get away with one handle — Vulkan's image/view split has no such shortcut. | `Apply()`'s `source` parameter is `BlurResult` too (not two separate `VkImage`/`VkImageView` parameters) purely so the `radius <= 0` no-op path can hand back a complete, consistent `BlurResult` unchanged — `source.Image` is otherwise unused inside `Apply()` itself, since descriptor binding is by view only; documented explicitly in the header so it doesn't read as a mystery unused parameter. |
| 2026-09-13 | Ping-pong target images (`_imageA`/`_imageB`) are kept in `VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_GENERAL` for their entire lifetime once created, with exactly one `UNDEFINED -> GENERAL` transition recorded the first time `Apply()` runs after `EnsureTargets()` (re)allocates them (tracked via a `_targetsNeedInitialTransition` flag, since `EnsureTargets()` itself has no command buffer to record into — only `Apply()`'s own one-shot buffer does) | Transition each target to a more specific layout (e.g. `SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL`) between the write and the following read, the way a sampled-and-written resource normally would | `GENERAL` is the only layout that supports both `imageLoad` and `imageStore` on the same image, and nothing else (no sampling with a real `VkSampler`, no attachment use) ever touches these two images — a more specific layout would need transitioning back and forth every pass for zero benefit. A real `vkCmdPipelineBarrier` memory barrier (not a layout transition) is still required *between* the two dispatches, since `vkCmdDispatch` calls on the same queue give no implicit memory-visibility ordering the way render-pass subpass dependencies do. | Any future compute pass added to this codebase reading and writing its own transient storage images should default to this same "stay in `GENERAL` forever, barrier for memory visibility only" pattern rather than reaching for per-pass layout transitions that buy nothing here. |
| 2026-09-13 | Verified clean on the **first real attempt** — zero validation-layer warnings/errors in a Debug build, all 3 new tests (no-op below radius 0, impulse-spread, ping-pong reuse — the same three scenarios `BlurPassGL3_test.cpp` already covers) passing in both Debug and Release without any debugging cycle, unlike Phase 35.2's Y-flip/`TRANSFER_DST_BIT` bugs | — | Closely mirroring an already-correct, already-tested reference implementation (`BlurPassGL3`'s exact math and API shape) rather than reimplementing the algorithm from the proposal's own description from scratch is what made this land clean — the same pattern Phase 35.1 followed for `SDFRect.glsl` before Phase 35.2's Image-batch work deviated further from GL3's shape and introduced two real bugs. | Full suite **528/528** (525 baseline + 3 new tests), `check_api_leaks.sh` clean, both Debug (validation-clean) and Release rebuilt and passing. Worth remembering for future Vulkan sub-phases: the closer a new backend feature's implementation shape stays to an already-verified GL3 (or earlier Vulkan) reference, the lower the bug rate — deviate deliberately and defensively, not incidentally. |

## Phase 35.4 — Vulkan DrawShadow (`RenderShadowBatch`)

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-13 | `Render()`'s single "record everything into one command buffer, submit once at the end" flow was split into reusable `BeginMainCommandBuffer()`/`EndAndSubmitMainCommandBuffer()` halves, and `RenderShadowBatch()` calls both — ending/submitting/waiting the command buffer accumulated so far, doing its own silhouette-render and `_blurPass.Apply()` as fully separate one-shot submissions, then beginning a *new* command buffer for the composite draw and everything after it | Keep one command buffer per `Render()` call and find some way to record the blur inline | `BlurPassVulkan::Apply()` is itself a fully self-contained, synchronously-awaited submission (Phase 35.3) — it cannot be recorded as commands into a *different*, not-yet-submitted command buffer. The silhouette render must also be fully complete on the GPU (not just recorded) before `Apply()` reads it, since `Apply()` is a separate queue submission with no ordering relationship to unsubmitted work. Multiple synchronous stalls per `Render()` call (one per `Shadow` batch, plus the final one) is simply *more* of the same already-accepted stopgap (see `NativeRendererVulkan`'s own file comment), not a new category of technical debt. | Any future `BatchKind` whose implementation needs its own fully separate submission (not just more draws in the current one) should follow this same "flush, do the side work, begin a fresh command buffer" pattern rather than trying to force everything into one command buffer. |
| 2026-09-13 | The silhouette offscreen image (`_shadowSilhouetteImage`) is created with **both** `VK_IMAGE_USAGE_COLOR_ATTACHMENT_BIT` (for the silhouette render) and `VK_IMAGE_USAGE_STORAGE_BIT` (for `_blurPass`'s own `imageLoad`), kept in `VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_GENERAL` permanently | Transition it between `COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL` (render) and `GENERAL` (blur source) for each use | `GENERAL` is valid for *both* a colour attachment and a storage image (`vkCmdBeginRendering`'s attachment `imageLayout` legally accepts `GENERAL`), so no transition is needed between the render and the blur read at all — the same "stay in `GENERAL` forever" principle `BlurPassVulkan`'s own ping-pong targets already use (Phase 35.3), applied here too. | Any future renderer-owned image serving more than one GPU-side role (attachment + storage, or similar) should default to `GENERAL` for its whole lifetime rather than transitioning per-use, when nothing needs a more specialised layout. |
| 2026-09-13 | The blurred composite is drawn through a **second**, dedicated descriptor set (`_compositeDescriptorSet`, sharing `_imageDescriptorSetLayout`/`_imagePipeline`/`_imagePipelineLayout`) whose texture binding declares `VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_GENERAL`, rewritten after every `_blurPass.Apply()` call — not the per-`DrawImage`-batch `_imageDescriptorPool` `Render()` already manages | Reuse the existing per-batch Image descriptor allocation path, since it already exists for exactly this pipeline | Every real `DrawImage` texture is documented (and asserted, by convention) to already be in `VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL` — `Render()`'s per-batch descriptor writes hardcode that layout. `_blurPass`'s own output view never leaves `GENERAL` (Phase 35.3's own design), so binding it through the same path with the same hardcoded layout would be a genuine layout-vs-descriptor mismatch, not merely suboptimal. A second, purpose-built descriptor set sidesteps the conflict entirely rather than threading a "is this an internal or a user texture" distinction through the per-batch path. | Layer compositing and backdrop blur (Phase 35.5/35.6) will very likely need their own composited-texture draws too — check first whether they can reuse `_compositeDescriptorSet` (if only one such composite is ever in flight at a time, as here) or need their own, following whichever of this phase's two patterns actually fits their own timing. |
| 2026-09-13 | `RenderShadowBatch()`'s own silhouette-rect and blurred-composite quads are built by two new free functions in `NativeRendererVulkan.cpp`'s anonymous namespace (`BuildRectQuadVertices`/`BuildImageQuadVertices`), and the composite one deliberately uses a **plain, unflipped** UV table — not `NativeRendererGL3::BuildImageQuadVertices()`'s own V-flipped one | Port GL3's V-flip formula verbatim, since the two functions otherwise mirror each other closely | GL3's flip exists to compensate for two GL-specific facts neither holds on this backend: its vertex shader still negates Y (this backend's own no longer does, since Phase 35.2's fix), and GL's texture row order is bottom-up (a Vulkan image's row 0 is already its top row, matching pixel space directly). Copying the flip formula here would have been a **wrong port**, not a harmless one — the whole point of Phase 35.2's Y-flip fix was that Vulkan's own convention differs from GL's, and this composite draw is exactly the kind of place that difference resurfaces if not accounted for deliberately. | The Phase 35.4 shadow test (an off-center, asymmetric shadow+rect scenario, same as GL3's own Phase 34.4 test) passed on the **first real attempt** with this unflipped formula — reinforcing that the earlier Y-flip fix's reasoning was correct and that blindly mirroring GL3 code without re-deriving backend-specific conventions is the actual risk, not a shortcut. |
| 2026-09-13 | Two real bugs, both caught by the Debug validation layer on the very first test run (not found by pixel-output debugging): (1) the silhouette image's one-time `UNDEFINED -> GENERAL` transition barrier's `dstStageMask` only covered `COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT`, but its `dstAccessMask` also included `SHADER_READ_BIT` (needed for `_blurPass`'s later `imageLoad`), which is only valid at the `COMPUTE_SHADER` stage; (2) `BlurPassVulkan`'s own ping-pong images (`_imageA`/`_imageB`) were created without `VK_IMAGE_USAGE_SAMPLED_BIT`, so binding `_blurPass`'s output as a `COMBINED_IMAGE_SAMPLER` (this phase's own composite draw) failed outright | — | Both are exactly the kind of easy-to-miss, spec-precise requirement (a barrier's stage mask must cover every stage its access mask is valid for; a descriptor's usage-flag prerequisites depend on how it ends up consumed, not how it was originally designed) validation layers exist to catch, and did, immediately, in a Debug build — reinforcing this session's own established rule (`.claude/DECISIONS.md`'s Phase 35.2 entry) to always verify new Vulkan work in Debug, not just Release. | Fixed by widening the barrier's `dstStageMask` to include `VK_PIPELINE_STAGE_COMPUTE_SHADER_BIT`, and adding `VK_IMAGE_USAGE_SAMPLED_BIT` to `BlurPassVulkan`'s own ping-pong image usage flags (retroactively — those images were designed in Phase 35.3 for `imageLoad`/`imageStore` + read-back only, before any consumer needed to sample them through a real `VkSampler`). Full suite **529/529**, `check_api_leaks.sh` clean, both Debug (validation-clean) and Release rebuilt and passing. |

## Phase 35.5 — Vulkan Layer Compositing (`PushOpacityLayer`/`PushBlendLayer`/`PopLayer`)

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-13 | `SetTarget()`'s signature gained a `VkImage targetImage` parameter (alongside the existing `VkImageView targetView`) | Keep the view-only signature and special-case depth-0 some other way | `CompositeBlendLayer()`'s backdrop copy needs a real `vkCmdCopyImage` source, and no Vulkan call recovers a `VkImage` from a `VkImageView` — the exact same reasoning `BlurPassVulkan::Apply()` already established for its own `BlurResult{Image; View}` return type (Phase 35.3). Every real caller of `SetTarget()` is a test helper that already owns both handles (`ScratchImage`), so this is a clean, low-risk signature change, not a compromise. | All 5 existing `NativeRendererVulkan_test.cpp` call sites updated; `ScratchImage` gained an `Image()` accessor alongside its existing `View()`. Any future real integration (`SDL3VulkanBackend`'s live frame loop) must pass both handles too. |
| 2026-09-13 | Layer push/pop needs **no separate submission** the way `RenderShadowBatch()` does (Phase 35.4) — `PushLayer()`/`PopLayer()` just end and re-begin rendering instances within the *same*, still-recording main command buffer | Route layer compositing through the same "flush, do side work, begin a new command buffer" pattern Shadow uses | Nothing about opacity or blend-mode compositing needs a separate queue submission — no compute dispatch, no fence wait. `vkCmdCopyImage` (blend mode's own backdrop copy) is a transfer command illegal *inside* an active rendering instance, but recording it between an `vkCmdEndRendering`/`vkCmdBeginRendering` pair in the *same* command buffer is perfectly legal and sufficient. | Established a lighter-weight second pattern alongside Shadow's heavier one: "just needs different render targets and/or non-render commands within one command buffer" vs. "needs an entirely separate submission" — `DrawBackdropBlur` (next) should default to whichever actually fits its own real requirement, not copy either pattern reflexively. |
| 2026-09-13 | Opacity-layer compositing needs its own **second graphics pipeline** (`_premultipliedImagePipeline`) — identical shader modules/vertex-input/pipeline layout to `_imagePipeline`, only the blend-state factors differ (`ONE`/`ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA` vs. `SRC_ALPHA`/`ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA`) | Reuse `_imagePipeline` and somehow vary blend factors per draw | Vulkan bakes blend factors into the pipeline object itself; changing them per-draw needs `VK_EXT_extended_dynamic_state3` (`vkCmdSetColorBlendEquationEXT`), an extension this codebase doesn't otherwise require. A popped layer's own captured render is already premultiplied by its own coverage (rendered normally, then read back) — using `_imagePipeline`'s own factors (correct for a *non*-premultiplied real `DrawImage` source) would double-apply alpha, exactly the bug `NativeRendererGL3::CompositeOpacityLayer()`'s own comment already documents and fixes with its own `glBlendFunc` override. | Any future renderer-owned premultiplied composite (this phase's own `_premultipliedImagePipeline`, reused directly rather than adding a third near-identical pipeline) should default to this same blend-state pair; `Blend.glsl`'s own pipeline needed a **third**, distinct approach instead (`blendEnable=false` — see below) since it computes the full composited result itself. |
| 2026-09-13 | `Blend.glsl` needs no vertex buffer at all — its vertex shader generates the fixed, full-viewport quad directly from `gl_VertexIndex`, and `CompositeBlendLayer()`'s own draw is a bare `vkCmdDraw(cmd, 6, 1, 0, 0)` | Give it a vertex buffer like every other pipeline (reusing `_shadowQuadVertexBuffer`, or a new dedicated one) | This composite is always exactly the same fixed NDC quad (no per-draw position/size parameterization the way `DrawRect`/`DrawImage`/the shadow composite have) — generating it from `gl_VertexIndex` is a standard, idiomatic Vulkan technique for exactly this shape of draw, and avoids one more buffer to manage for genuinely no benefit. Mirrors `NativeRendererGL3::CompositeBlendLayer()`'s own fixed `kBlendFullscreenQuad` VBO in spirit, just without needing a VBO at all on this backend. | `Blend.glsl`'s own file comment documents why UV (0,0) maps to NDC (-1,-1) without any flip — this backend's Y convention (Phase 35.2) already matches both this shader's own inputs (a popped layer's texture, and the backdrop copy), both rendered/copied by this same renderer with the identical non-flipped convention. |
| 2026-09-13 | `CompositeBlendLayer()` is split into two methods — `CopyBackdropForBlend()` (the `vkCmdCopyImage` + surrounding barriers) and `CompositeBlendLayer()` (the actual draw) — called from `PopLayer()` on either side of its own `vkCmdBeginRendering` for the resumed parent target | One method doing both, called once | `vkCmdCopyImage` is a transfer command and illegal while a rendering instance is active; the composite draw needs one active. An initial single-method draft called the whole thing before `vkCmdBeginRendering`, which would have recorded `vkCmdDraw` with **no** active rendering instance at all — caught by re-reading the draft against Vulkan's own render-pass-scoping rules before ever building it, not by a validation-layer failure at runtime this time. | Worth remembering as a category: any future composite that needs *both* a transfer-class operation (copy/blit/clear outside a render pass) *and* a draw against the result should expect to split into "prep" and "draw" halves called from either side of the relevant `vkCmdBeginRendering`, the same way this pair now does. |
| 2026-09-13 | `Blend.glsl` was ported to SPIR-V verbatim from `NativeRendererGL3`'s own `kBlendFragmentSource` (same 12-blend-mode math, same Porter-Duff recomposite formula), with `uMode` (a GL uniform) replaced by a push constant, matching `GaussianBlur.glsl`'s own established "small per-dispatch scalar -> push constant, not a UBO/uniform" convention (Phase 35.3) | Keep it as a uniform via a small UBO, closer to GL3's own uniform-based shape | No descriptor-set churn needed for a single `int` that changes at most once per `CompositeBlendLayer()` call; consistent with this backend's own precedent rather than GL3's (GL has no push-constant equivalent, so its own uniform choice doesn't transfer as "the pattern to match"). | Verified clean on the **first real attempt** for both the opacity and blend-mode tests (mirroring `NativeRendererGL3_test.cpp`'s own Phase 34.5 opacity and Multiply-mode scenarios) — zero validation-layer warnings, no debugging cycle. Full suite **531/531** (529 baseline + 2 new tests), `check_api_leaks.sh` clean, both Debug (validation-clean) and Release rebuilt and passing. |

## Phase 35.6 — Vulkan Backdrop Blur (`RenderBackdropBlurBatch`)

| Date | Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Impact |
|------|----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| 2026-09-13 | `RenderBackdropBlurBatch()` follows Shadow's (Phase 35.4) heavier "flush, separate submissions, resume on a new command buffer" pattern, not Layer's (Phase 35.5) lighter "same command buffer" one — the choice flagged as open in Phase 35.5's own DECISIONS.md entry | Try the lighter Layer-style pattern first, since both read back a copy of already-rendered content | `_blurPass.Apply()` is involved again here (same as Shadow), and the backdrop copy needs every batch recorded so far in this `Render()` call to actually be complete on the GPU, not merely recorded into a not-yet-submitted command buffer — the same reasoning `RenderShadowBatch()`'s own first step already established. Layer compositing never called `_blurPass` at all, which is *why* it could stay lightweight; this sub-phase confirms that was the right distinguishing factor to check for, not "reads back already-rendered content" in general (`CompositeBlendLayer()`'s own backdrop copy also reads back content, yet needed no separate submission). | The real distinguishing question for any *future* batch kind is "does this involve a separate, self-contained GPU submission (a compute dispatch via `BlurPassVulkan`, or similar)?", not "does it read existing framebuffer content?" — worth stating explicitly since both this phase and Layer superficially look similar (both copy-then-composite) but needed different patterns. |
| 2026-09-13 | A **new**, dedicated `_backdropBlurCopyImage` (not `_backdropImage`, Phase 35.5's own blend-layer backdrop copy) holds the padded region being blurred | Reuse `_backdropImage` for both roles | Different size/lifecycle (sized to the *padded requested rect*, not the whole target) and different consumption (read via `_blurPass`'s own `imageLoad`/`imageStore`, `STORAGE_BIT`, vs. sampled via a real `VkSampler`, `SAMPLED_BIT`, for blend-mode compositing) — mirrors `NativeRendererGL3`'s own identical choice to keep `_backdropBlurCopyTex` and `_backdropTex` as two separate textures despite the conceptual overlap. | Confirms the project-wide pattern (first stated in Phase 35.4's own entry) of giving each renderer-owned image exactly the usage flags its *actual* consumers need, rather than a generic "just in case" superset. |
| 2026-09-13 | The backdrop-region copy's source/destination offsets are used directly, with **no coordinate flip at all** — unlike `NativeRendererGL3::RenderBackdropBlurBatch()`'s own `glY = viewportY + viewportHeight - authorY` conversion | Port GL3's flip formula, treating it as boilerplate every backend needs | GL's `glCopyTexSubImage2D` addresses window coordinates bottom-up; this backend's `vkCmdCopyImage` addresses image coordinates top-down, already matching this codebase's own "pixel space, Y=0 is top" convention directly — no conversion needed, the same "GL3's flip doesn't transfer, Vulkan's own convention already matches" reasoning `BuildImageQuadVertices()`'s own comment already established (Phase 35.4) applied to a genuinely different kind of coordinate (a copy region's offset, not a UV table) this time. | The Phase 35.6 seam test (an intentionally Y-asymmetric red-above/blue-below split, mirroring `NativeRendererGL3_test.cpp`'s own Phase 34.6 test) passed on the **first real attempt** — real evidence this offset math was derived correctly rather than assumed. |
| 2026-09-13 | The backdrop-blur composite draw uses `_imagePipeline` (the standard, non-premultiplied blend pipeline), **not** `_premultipliedImagePipeline` (Phase 35.5's own opacity-layer pipeline) | Use the premultiplied pipeline, since this is also compositing renderer-captured content back onto itself | Matches `NativeRendererGL3::RenderBackdropBlurBatch()`'s own choice exactly — it calls plain `RenderImageBatch()`, the same standard-blend path `RenderShadowBatch()`'s own composite uses, *not* the premultiplied override only `CompositeOpacityLayer()` needs. The premultiplied override exists specifically because an *unmodified* captured layer, composited at `Opacity < 1`, needs its whole premultiplied value scaled down uniformly; a backdrop-blur's content isn't being re-scaled by any additional factor here, so the standard blend is correct, matching this class's own Shadow composite (Phase 35.4), which made the identical choice for the identical reason. | Confirms `RenderShadowBatch()`'s own choice of `_imagePipeline` (not premultiplied) was correct by cross-checking against GL3 a second time, rather than assuming Phase 35.5's newer pipeline should be the default for any future renderer-owned composite. |
| 2026-09-13 | Verified clean on the **first real attempt** for both new tests (the Y-asymmetric seam/crop test and the `MaxBackdropBlurPerFrame` rate-limit test, mirroring `NativeRendererGL3_test.cpp`'s own Phase 34.6 scenarios) — zero validation-layer warnings, no debugging cycle, continuing the pattern started in Phase 35.3/35.5 (only Phase 35.4 needed real fixes) | — | Closely mirroring an already-correct, already-tested GL3 reference (exact same rate-limit default, exact same padding/crop math, exact same test scenarios) while deliberately re-deriving the few backend-specific differences (no Y-flip, no window-coordinate conversion, which pipeline to reuse) rather than blindly porting GL3 code wholesale continues to be the reliable pattern across every Vulkan sub-phase this project has done. | Full suite **533/533** (531 baseline + 2 new tests), `check_api_leaks.sh` clean, both Debug (validation-clean) and Release rebuilt and passing. This closes out Phase 35's GL3-parity effects work (Shadow/Layer/BackdropBlur, Phases 35.4-35.6) — `NativeRendererVulkan` now matches `NativeRendererGL3`'s full Phase 34 feature set. Remaining Phase 35 scope: live frame-loop integration, then DX12/WebGPU "first SDF rect" sub-phases (Metal deferred indefinitely). |
